# Findings Log

> **What this is.** A running, dated journal of everything that happens to this codebase — every change, every decision, and every *finding* (a bug discovered, a gotcha, a thing that turned out not to work the way you assumed). It's the project's narrative memory. Where ARCHITECTURE and the ENCYCLOPEDIA describe the app *as it is now*, this log describes *how it got there and why*.
>
> **Why it matters.** Most "wait, why did we do it this way?" debt comes from lost context. This log is the answer to that question, written down at the moment you still remember it. In an AI-assisted workflow, **append an entry after every meaningful change** — it's part of the Definition of Done (STANDARDS §10), and it gives your assistant (and future-you) the real history instead of guesses.
>
> **How to use it.** Newest entries at the top. One entry per change/finding/decision. Keep them short — a few lines each. Tag the type so the log is scannable.
>
> **Status:** Living document · **Started:** 2026-10-03
>
> **The product was renamed from MilO to Dually Trip Tracker on 2026-10-13 (ADR-010).** Entries from that day on say Dually. Everything written before keeps the old name, and the old addresses, as it was written.

---

## Entry types

- **`[CHANGE]`** — something was built, modified, or removed
- **`[FIX]`** — a bug was fixed
- **`[DECISION]`** — a choice was made (link an ADR if it's a big one)
- **`[FINDING]`** — something was discovered: a gotcha, a constraint, a surprise, a "don't do X because Y"
- **`[DEBT]`** — a corner was knowingly cut (what, why, cost to fix later)

---

## Log

### 2026-10-13

**`[FIX]` The unit tests could pass from Gradle's cache after a picture they check had changed**
Found on 2026-10-14 while stacking the mascot's redraw on the app's rebrand: the gate passed with `testDebugUnitTest FROM-CACHE`, and the same tests run with `--rerun` failed one (`MascotPicturesTest`, below). Several tests read files straight from disk (the mascot's pictures, the icon's drawings, `colors.xml`, the strings, `changelog.json`, the website's `index.html`), and Gradle did not count those files as the tests' inputs, so a run whose code had not changed could be handed back from the build cache after one of them had. `app/build.gradle.kts` now names `src/main/res`, `src/main/assets` and `website/` as inputs of every test task; a file added to `website/` was seen to make the tests run again. The mascot job's own "gate passed" was such a cached pass.

**`[DECISION]` A patch of the moonwalk's middle part may be stored without a see-through channel**
`MascotPicturesTest` asked that every frame of `mascot_stop_and_spin.webp` carry one. Since the redraw one patch does not: frame 51, 75 by 65 pixels on his chest while the grin squashes, where every pixel is his body, so `cwebp` stores no see-through channel for it. It replaces only what lies under it, which was his body too, so it draws the same either way; the emulator's showing of the moonwalk played it. The test now asks it of every whole frame, and says why a patch may lack it.


**`[CHANGE]` The opening screen shows the face and the words "Trip Tracker"**
Phone only. Shawn had seen the big "D" on Android's opening screen and wrote: "should be the face icon. i think it looks better with the text under it Trip Tracker. the D at the top of the app when app is loaded is good." The opening screen draws the launcher icon, so the face icon of the same evening already put the face there; the words were added as Android's "branding image" (`android:windowSplashScreenBrandingImage`), which Android places near the foot of the screen, not straight under the icon. They are pictures in five densities, because a vector drawing cannot hold letters: `tools/make_splash_wordmark.py` has Google Chrome draw them (it applies the typeface's kerning, which Pillow without its layout library does not) and Pillow make the smaller sizes; 52 KB in all. Seen on an emulator (Android 16) on a cold start; not yet on the phone.

**`[CHANGE]` The launcher icon is the face: the mascot's dials and his grin**
Phone only (the car screen shows no launcher icon of its own drawing). **Built, not yet seen on a phone or an emulator.** Shawn sent the design's "Icon A, Face" and asked: "what about using this for the app icon". The rebrand of the morning had taken "Icon B, Monogram" (the "D") for the icon, because the face was the mascot's and the mascot was not to be touched then; by the evening he had asked for the mascot in orange, with the "D" that turns into this grin.
- **What is not obvious.** Android's vector drawings have no dashed strokes, and the design draws the dials' marks as dashes, so `tools/make_launcher_icon.py` writes each mark as a line of its own from the design's numbers, and writes the same drawing as an SVG to look at. The file in `res/drawable` is the script's, and CI says so (`--check`).
- **It is cut by no launcher.** The design's square of 150 is laid on the middle 72 of the icon's 108 (what a square launcher shape shows), and everything of the face is within 28 of the middle, inside the 33 that a round shape leaves.
- **The "D" stays** as the layer for themed icons (a face cannot be one colour), and in the four other places the mark stands. `AppMarkTest` follows the letter to `ic_launcher_monochrome.xml`; `LauncherIconTest` holds the face to the mark's letter (its mouth is the letter's outer outline, turned) and to named colours.
- **Not done here:** the 512-pixel icon for Google Play and the website's pictures still show the "D"; they are made with the listing's pictures.

**`[DECISION]` At 48 pixels the face's marks are too fine to see, and that was accepted**
Drawn at a home screen's sizes (48 and 60 pixels, three launcher shapes), the two dials, the needles, the red zone and the grin read as a face; the white marks round the dials are a third of a pixel wide and are only a texture. They were left as the design draws them rather than thickened, so that the icon is the design's at every size. If the phone shows it muddy (device check RB-10), fewer and thicker marks are one number each in the script.

**`[CHANGE]` The mascot is rebranded: orange, the brand's D on his chest that turns into a grin, and soles that light up amber as he steps. Every picture of him is drawn again**
Phone and website; the Android Auto screen is not touched. **On the branch `feat/rebrand-mascot`; not yet seen on the phone.** Seen on the emulator (a phone 411 dp wide, a debug build): the wave, the moonwalk, the party on demand and the run after "I sent it" played to their ends, the grin and the soles as decided, nothing jumping or missing. In the first party the frame before the pop stayed 218 ms instead of 42 and the popper went off 0.17 s late; it was the first sound that app process played, and two more parties did not do it. Only pictures changed, and the website's two files that name them: no Kotlin, no test and no test's limit. Shawn had said "do not change the maskot tell we review together". Shown the green mascot on the orange website: "oh. i thougth we were going to change the mascot color", then "lets change it. can you also make the change to the soles of the feet?" Of the soles: "i want the soles to be black clear through plastic. when he steps i want the soles to light up amber. same as a cars lights. foot down amber. foot up clear black". Of the letter: "also can we have the D animate too. change from the D vertical and rotate to the horizontal side ways smile. same as the supplied claude design". On the review sheets and films: "i like it but i think the shoes lights should be different color. i dont care for the orange now. can we make them more amber when he steps down. Your color suggestion. everything else is perfect". APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, The greeting ("How he looks"), The one-year party, The run after a report is sent, Website, Design system; ADR-007, addendum of 2026-10-13; ADR-010, section 5; device checks MR-1 to MR-5.
- **Changed:** the nine pictures in `app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/`, under their own names, with the same frames, sizes and times; `website/index.html` and `website/styles.css` (the new file names, the picture's alt text, and the comment on the strip, whose `TODO(debt)` is gone). **New:** `website/mascot/dually-standing.webp`, `dually-waving.webp`, `dually-dials.webp`. **Gone:** `website/mascot/standing.webp`, `waving.webp`, `dials.webp`. Outside the repository, in `design/mascot/` (git-ignored): the model and the scripts that draw and pack him. **Not drawn again:** `design/mascot/renders/`, pictures nothing ships, which still show him green.
- **What he looks like now.** An orange block, the brand's Hi-Vis Orange `#FF5A12`, with his dial housings, shoes, ankle cuffs and the fedora's band the same orange. The brand's D on his chest where the M was: the app icon's own letter, the mark's dark `#1E1A17`, the same height (0.38 of a unit), place, depth and chamfered rim as the M. His dials have a white ring, white marks and a red zone (Redline `#FF3D2E`) at the top of each scale; the speedometer has one now too, 200 to 240, as the design's dials do. Needles `#FF4A1C`. The party cap is amber paper `#FFB648` with orange stripes (it was lime with green); the popper's lip, knob and bead are amber (they were lime); the confetti's most frequent colour is orange (it was lime). Gloves, arms and legs, chrome, brake lights, the fedora's felt, the cap's rim and pom-pom, and the smoke are as they were.
- **The grin.** The D rests on his chest as a letter. It turns a quarter turn clockwise in about 0.6 s, with a small run-up and a small overshoot, as the design's flip does; at 90 degrees the letter is swapped for a mouth of the same outer shape: a dark inside (`#2A1712`), a bar of two white teeth along the flat side and a pink tongue (`#F2546B`) in the bowl. They are shapes on his chest, not a sticker, and the mouth can talk: the bowl squashes up towards the teeth. **Where he grins:** in the wave, from landing facing the viewer until the first frames of `TurnAway`, saying a word or two (two squashes); in the moonwalk, from the hat pose through the hip thrusts, the kick (one squash) and the spin, turning back during `MoonwalkOn`; in the party, from the pop to the end; on the website, the turn while his arm goes up (the strip's five raise frames) and the grin while he waves, his needles flicking up like eyebrows with the turn (on the website only). **Never in the run after a report:** his chest is never turned to the viewer there. His chest faces the viewer (within 45 degrees, uncovered) for 63 frames in a row in the wave, 27 in the moonwalk's hat pose, 55 in the party from the pop on, and no frame of the run.
- **Why the D sits on a plate that turns.** A recess cannot turn in the thing it is cut into, so the D is not cut into his body as the M was. It is cut into a round plate of the body's own orange, which lies flush in a round pocket in his chest and turns on the bone `mark`. The plate cannot be told from the chest: it was looked for in pictures with the contrast three times as strong, and it stands a fifth of a thousandth of a unit proud, a tenth of a pixel in the largest picture there is.
- **In the model.** Four bones were added: `mark` (the turn; everything of the mark hangs on it), `mark_talk` (the squash), `mark_counter` (the letter's island) and `mouth` (the teeth and the tongue). Four sliders: `grin`, `talk`, `sole_l`, `sole_r`. Every clip has seven curves more: the two soles, the mark's turn and the two sliders of the mark. The `.glb` leaves the teeth and the tongue out, for the hat's reason (a `.glb` has no slider, 2026-10-10); nothing in it says how far the soles are lit.
- **What it weighs.** No test limit was raised.

- **Not yet:** the phone (device checks MR-1 to MR-5). This pays two `[DEBT]` entries further down this day: "The mascot's wave shows the old lime on the amber card" and "The mascot shows the old colour and the old letter".

**`[FINDING]` Given the brand's orange itself, he is drawn paler: his material is `#FF4F03`**
The brand's orange is `#FF5A12`, and that is what his chest should look like in the pictures. Given `#FF5A12` as his material, he came out paler under the studio's lights, `#FF6A31`. So the model's material is `#FF4F03`, and its plastic under the clear coat mirrors less; under the same lights his chest is drawn `#FF5C1B`. **Whoever changes his colour again sets the material darker than the colour he is to look,** and measures his chest in a drawn frame, not the material.

**`[DECISION]` The soles' amber: `#FF9A00` first, then `#FFBF00` on Shawn's word**
- **What a sole is.** Smoked, see-through black plastic, as Shawn asked. Unlit it hides 72 % of what is behind it: black on the dark page, smoked brown on the website's amber card, smoked grey on a pale ground. Lit, it is amber all through, with no glow round it.
- **The first amber, `#FF9A00`, was one more orange under his orange shoes.** Shawn, on the review sheets and films: "i dont care for the orange now. can we make them more amber when he steps down. Your color suggestion".
- **`#FFBF00`, a turn signal's amber,** drawn about `#FEC12E`. `#FFB300`, `#FFBF00` and `#FFCC00` were compared at the app's size, beside the brake lights and the gloves: `#FFBF00` is the turn signal's, and `#FFCC00` begins to be yellow.
- **The thickness stayed,** 0.07 of a unit, about 5 dp where he is 150 dp high: at the app's size a lit sole reads as a clear amber band under each shoe. Shawn had accepted a slightly thicker sole if this one did not read; it did.
- **Two more points Shawn accepted:** when he stands or waves both feet are down, so both soles are lit the whole time; and amber light on the website's amber card is hard to see.

**`[DECISION]` When a sole is lit: one rule from the poses, and the moonwalk's exception**
- **The rule:** a sole is lit from the frame its foot touches the floor until the frame it leaves it. It comes on within one frame and goes out over two (half lit, then 0.15). It is worked out from each pose: a foot is on the floor when the lowest point of its sole is within 0.008 of a unit of it. In every clip a sole on the floor is at most 0.005 above it, and one in the air at least 0.012, so the line between them is clear in all of them.
- **The exception is the moonwalk's glide.** The foot that slides back flat is dark; only the foot planted on its toe is lit, the one that keeps its place on the floor, so the two lights take turns.
- **Shawn's cases:** standing and waving, both lit; in the hop turns, both dark in the air; in the kick, the kicking foot dark; running and running on the spot, they alternate with the steps, and on the spot each sole flashes four times a second; in the slide, both lit, with the red brake lights; in the party, lit but in its two jumps.

**`[FINDING]` No motion changed: the proof**
Only his colours, the letter and the soles were to change. Checked against the model and the pictures as they were before:
- **The clips.** Every one of the 4,729 curves of the 28 clips, 140,094 keys, is identical before and after. The seven curves each clip has more are the soles' and the mark's.
- **His shape.** Drawn as one grey from four sides, his outline differs in 0 pixels; only the letter differs.
- **The pictures.** Against the pictures they replace, the outline of every frame of all nine app pictures and of the website's three differs only along a rim one pixel wide: at most 148 pixels in a frame, and 0 once that rim is taken off. Frame counts and times are unchanged.

**`[FINDING]` Every number the packers print is the one the code holds, so no code changed**
The app plays the pictures by numbers that stand in its code as the scripts printed them (an axis, a sole's height, a reach, a stride), and no test checks most of them. All drawn again by the documented commands, the packers printed exactly what the code holds:
- **The wave:** 540 by 440 pixels; axis 118.4 dp; soles 5.0; reach 79.2 and 61.2; stride 71.43.
- **The moonwalk:** 480 by 480; axis 89.2; soles 4.5; reach 54.8 and 81.6; stride 80.60.
- **The party:** 675 by 800 at 1.640625 pixels to the dp; axis 337.5 pixels; soles 37.5 pixels.
- **The run:** the sheet's frame 650 by 430; axis 167.6; soles 4.8; the eight reaches; step 15.4525; 20.604 short of the spot; 468 by 180 dp; spot 156.0; soles 8.4; the slide's 45 values.

**`[DECISION]` The run on the spot is packed one step harder, and its limit was not raised**
At the packer's colour 50, `mascot_run_on_the_spot.webp` came to 665,534 bytes, 534 over its limit of 665,000 in `MascotPicturesTest`, because its soles flash four times a second. It is packed at colour 49 instead (`SPOT_COLOUR` in `pack_run_slide.py`) and weighs 661,660. No limit was raised.

**`[DECISION]` The website's three pictures of him have new names**
`standing.webp` is `dually-standing.webp` (30,596 bytes to 30,440), `waving.webp` is `dually-waving.webp` (227,916 to 220,674), and `dials.webp` is `dually-dials.webp` (7,834 to 7,258). **Why:** a picture that changes gets another name, since browsers keep the site's pictures for a week (`firebase.json`); under the old names a returning visitor could have seen the green mascot for that week. `index.html` and `styles.css` name the new files, and the picture's alt text is "Dually's mascot: an orange block with two dials for eyes and a D on his chest". The strip is 720 by 608 pixels a frame, 11 frames (5 of the arm going up, 6 of the wave), with the dials' squares and places exactly as the stylesheet holds them. **It is packed on the card's `--amber`, so the lime shape that showed while he waved is gone,** and the stylesheet's `TODO(debt)` about it was taken out.

**`[CHANGE]` The website is rebranded: Dually Trip Tracker, Shawn's new look, the address dually.top. Built, not live**
Website only; nothing in the app changes here (the app is rebranded by a change of its own). **One commit on the branch `feat/rebrand-website`, not pushed and not merged, so nothing was deployed: the live site is still "MilO Trip Log" at `milotriplog.top`.** Shawn has not seen it. **Why:** Google Play refused the app's listing under its Impersonation policy and named the icon (a black M on a lime square), the name "MilO Trip Log" and the listing's screenshots; another company's app, "Milo Track - Mileage Tracking", has a like name and does the same thing. Shawn: "we need to rebrand the app ... to comply"; "the rebrand is a must. i think it will fix everything. and i actaully really like the look"; "So the first thing we will do is change the name to Dually Trip Tracker."; "also please change the footer on the website to owned and operated by 2795748 Alberta Ltd."; "here is the theme and color and design ... we will start with the website and app do not change the maskot tell we review together"; and, the same day, "i will register a new domain name Dually.top". His design is a canvas, "Dually Rebrand Concept": a hero, the icon, the colours and the type. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Website; ADR-003, addendum of 2026-10-13.
- **Changed:** `website/index.html`, `compare.html`, `privacy.html`, `404.html`, `styles.css`, `mark.svg`, `icon-512.png`, `og-image.png`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`; `tools/changes_page.py` and the page it writes, `website/changes.html`. **New:** `website/fonts/archivo.ttf`, `ibm-plex-mono-medium.ttf`, `ibm-plex-mono-semibold.ttf`, `Archivo-OFL.txt`, `IBMPlexMono-OFL.txt`; `tools/make_site_pictures.py`. **Gone:** `website/fonts/sora.ttf`, `Sora-OFL.txt`. **Not touched:** `website/mascot/`, `website/screenshots/`, `firebase.json`, `.firebaserc`, the workflows, `app/` (the list of changes among it), and every document but this log, the encyclopedia's Website entry and ADR-003.
- **What is not obvious from the pages.**
  - **The stylesheet's names changed their meaning.** `--accent` was the lime of the hero tile; it is the orange now, and the hero's ground is `--amber`. `--quiet` is `--row`. The class `accent` is gone: the amber card is `hero` alone and is no `tile`. Whatever read those names from outside the site has to follow, and one thing did: the mascot's packing script (the `[DEBT]` below).
  - **The mascot's rules were carried over, not written again.** The part of `styles.css` from "Nothing below moves for a visitor whose system asks for less motion" to the end of the two needles' keyframes was taken from the old file by line number and set into the new one; with the comments taken out, the old and the new are the same 2,588 characters. Two comments in it were corrected, because they said "lime". What did change is where he stands and how large: his width, height and margins, which are layout.
  - **A card of words is two columns from 900 pixels** (the class `split`): the page is 1,200 pixels wide now, as the design draws it, and a line of words across a card that wide would run to some 150 letters. With the headline in the left third a line has 90 to 96.
  - **Install is an orange button only from 720 pixels.** On a phone the top line cannot hold both: the mark, the wordmark and the label are 287 pixels and the button 80, of 328 at a width of 360. A button alone on the pages' second row looked like a mistake, and the amber card's own button is the next thing on a phone's screen.
  - **Every page asks for Archivo ahead of its stylesheet** (`<link rel="preload" … crossorigin>`). Without it the words are first drawn in the visitor's own face and jump when Archivo's 659 KB arrive. The Content-Security-Policy allows it as it stands (`font-src 'self'`): Chrome fetched the file once and refused nothing.
  - **The questions' rows and What's new's rows have a rule round them.** The design's row colour, `#1F1A15`, is drawn there on a phone's screen of `#0D0B09`; on a card of `#221D18` it is all but the card's own colour, and a row could not be told from its ground.
  - **The email's subject changed with the name:** "Dually Trip Tracker support". The privacy policy says "last changed 13 October 2026", because its text changed: the app's name, the site's address and the support address. The sitemap's four dates are 2026-10-13.
  - **`icon-512.png` is a full square, without the mark's rounded corners,** as it was: it is for the Google Play listing, which rounds the corners itself and asks for a full square.

- **Checked** in headless Chrome 155 on this Mac, the pages served by a throwaway local server that sends `firebase.json`'s own headers and rewrites: all five pages at 360, 393, 768, 1280 and 1440 pixels. Nothing is wider than the screen; of some 480 pieces of text none is under its contrast limit, the lowest being the dark word on the orange button at 5.97 to 1; no request leaves the site and the Content-Security-Policy refused nothing; no script, no inline style, no handler; every picture has its alt and its size; every link of the site's own leads to a file. The keyboard's ring is amber on dark and dark on amber. With "less motion" asked for, nothing moves and neither `dials.webp` nor `waving.webp` is fetched, as before. `python3 tools/changes_page.py --check` passes. The pictures and the figures are in `scratchpad/design-shots/rebrand-web/`.
- **Not checked:** any other browser; a real phone; a validator (none is on the Mac, and nothing was downloaded: a Python script checked that every tag closes in order, that no id is there twice and that the structured data is valid JSON whose questions are the page's own). **Not run: the app's unit tests.** One of them fails on this branch, by reading (the `[FINDING]` below).

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the website's rebrand**
He gave the name, the footer's line, the design, the address, and the word that the mascot stays. These he was not asked.
1. **The headline is the design's:** "Fire up the truck. Dually's already counting." It is true of the app as the site describes it: a trip starts by itself when the phone connects to the truck's Bluetooth, with the app closed.
2. **The paragraph under it is not the design's,** which read "Pairs with your truck's Bluetooth, starts the log when you turn the key, and sends accounts a clean monthly km report as PDF or CSV." Three things in it are not so. The app starts a trip when the phone connects, not at the key. It sends nothing: it hands the report to the email app and the person presses send (the privacy page promises exactly that). And the report is a PDF and a CSV, both, in kilometres or miles. The page says: "Pairs with your truck's Bluetooth, starts a trip when your phone connects, and makes a clean monthly mileage report for your accountant, as a PDF and a CSV, ready for you to email."
3. **The old headline and paragraph left the page with the lime tile.** "Business mileage that logs itself." is still the title of a shared link. What the old paragraph said and the new one does not (GPS, Business or Personal by the work hours) is said further down the page, as before.
4. **The small label over the headline is the design's,** "Work-truck km log · Android". The line "For Android 14 or newer · Free · How to install" stayed, under the two actions; the design has no such line.
5. **The two actions:** "Download for Android" (the design's words; it was "Download the latest APK") opens the newest GitHub Release as before, and "See a sample report" leads to the report further down the same page.
6. **The label beside the wordmark is "Trip tracker",** where the design has "Km log": with the wordmark it reads as the name.
7. **The design's two phone cards under the hero were left out.** They are drawings of an app screen ("Truck connected · logging", "Monthly report → accounts"), and the real screens are four pictures further down. A drawing that the rebranded app may not match would be one more thing on the page that is not the app.
8. **The pages are the six the site had,** in the top line: Compare, What's new, Privacy, Support, GitHub, and Install last, as the design's orange button. The design's own three ("How it works", "Reports", "Get the app") name sections the site does not have.
9. **The footer:** "Owned and operated by 2795748 Alberta Ltd." is its first line, on every page, the generated one and the not-found page, which had no footer. **"© 2026 Shawn Kowalchuk. All rights reserved." stayed under it,** and "A personal project" still says who built the app: whether the copyright and that tile should name the company too is his to say. **The line "… is an independent project, not affiliated with any other app or company named Milo" is gone:** it named the old name twice and defended it.
10. **The tags of What's new:** "New" filled amber, "Improved" and "Fixed" outlined, "Security" filled ember. The design's Redline is "gauges only", so no tag is red.
11. **Buy me a coffee keeps Buy Me a Coffee's yellow.** It is that company's colour for that button, not the site's.
12. **The link picture has no screenshot and no mascot,** only words and the mark: the screenshot showed the old app, and the mascot waits for review.
13. **`tools/make_site_pictures.py` is committed,** so the two pictures can be drawn again after a change, as the sounds' scripts are. It needs Pillow, which no other script in the repository needs; it is run by hand and is no part of the build or of CI.
14. **The three pictures kept their file names** (`mark.svg`, `og-image.png`, `icon-512.png`), against the site's own rule that a changed picture gets another name. At the new address every file is new to every browser. At the old one it matters, and the answer there is to send visitors on (the encyclopedia's gotcha).
15. **IBM Plex Mono Medium is in the folder and declared, as asked, and nothing uses it.** Every use of the mono face is SemiBold, so no visitor pays for a second file.
16. **The Wikidata link stayed** in the structured data, though the item says the old name: it is Shawn's item to rename.

**`[FINDING]` The mascot on the amber card: he reads well standing, and a lime shape shows while he waves**
He was not changed: the same three pictures, the same rules. He stands at the right of the words where the design has its own mascot, 450 pixels wide on a wide screen (he was 360), and under the words on a phone.
- **Standing,** he is easy to see on amber: black arms, white gloves, a green body. Green on amber is loud, and he looks like the figure of another brand, which he is: **he is green, and he has a black M on his chest, the letter and the look of the icon Google Play named.** Nothing on the page says what the M stands for any more.
- **While the pointer is on him, a lime shape shows** where his lowered arm was, and a lime edge along his right side. `waving.webp` carries the old card's lime wherever the standing mascot would show beside the waving one (the encyclopedia says why the standing picture cannot be taken away instead): some 7,000 to 24,000 pixels of that lime in each of the strip's eleven frames, and none in the standing picture.
- **Another ground does not help.** On Bone `#FFF4E8`, one of the four grounds the design itself offers for the hero, the lime shape is plainer still and his white gloves all but vanish. Both grounds, standing and waving, are side by side in the evidence folder. He stays on amber.
- **What ends it** is the strip packed again on amber, which changes a file of the mascot's and so waits for Shawn's review of him; or no wave until then, which changes his rules and waits for the same.

**`[FINDING]` `SupportTileTest` fails on this branch alone**
Read, not run. `the website's Support link is the address the app writes to` looks in `website/index.html` for `href="mailto:` followed by the app's `SUPPORT_ADDRESS`, which is `support@milotriplog.top` in `feature/settings/SupportTile.kt`. The page says `support@dually.top` now. The test is right to fail: the two addresses must be one. It passes again when the app writes to the new address, which is the app's own change. Until both are on one branch, CI is red for whichever comes first.

**`[DEBT]` The mascot's wave shows the old lime on the amber card** (paid later the same day: "The website's three pictures of him have new names", at the top)
The `[FINDING]` above. Left because the mascot was not to be touched. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `website/styles.css`. Cost later: `pack_clips.py` run again with the card's colour, which it must now read from `--amber` (it read `--accent`, and that is the orange now: run as it is, it would pack an orange shape in place of the lime one), and the strip under another file name, since browsers keep the old one for a week.

**`[DEBT]` The typefaces are whole files, and one is not used**
A first visit fetches 799 KB of fonts where it fetched 111 KB: Archivo with every weight, width and script it has (659 KB), and IBM Plex Mono SemiBold (140 KB). Compressed on the way, as a host may do for `.ttf`, they are about 310 KB; whether Firebase Hosting does was not checked. Cutting Archivo down to the Latin letters and the three cuts the site uses (Regular, ExtraBold at two widths) would leave a fraction of it, and needs a font tool that is not on the Mac; nothing was downloaded for it. `ibm-plex-mono-medium.ttf` (137 KB) is in the folder and declared, and no page uses it. Cost later: one run of a subsetting tool, and the two `@font-face` ranges narrowed to what is left.

**`[CHANGE]` The app is rebranded: Dually Trip Tracker, orange where it was lime, a D where it had an M, new typefaces. Built, not released**
Phone and Android Auto screen, the widget, the notifications and the report. **On the branch `feat/rebrand-app`, not pushed; nothing was released and nothing has been on the phone.** The website is rebranded by a change of its own, and the mascot is not touched. **Why:** Google Play refused the listing under its Impersonation policy and named the icon (a black M on a lime square), the name "MilO Trip Log" and the screenshots; another company's app is called "Milo Track - Mileage Tracking". Shawn: "we need to rebrand the app ... to comply"; "So the first thing we will do is change the name to Dually Trip Tracker."; "here is the theme and color and design"; "i actaully really like the look". ADR-010 has the decision; APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Design system", has the look.
- **Changed:** every string resource that named the app (99 lines) and 109 lines of words the code writes (event log, export file, file names), with the tests that expect them; `Color.kt`, `Type.kt`, `AppHeader.kt`, `FigureText.kt`, `FigureRow.kt`, `DashboardWheels.kt`, `MonthTiles.kt`; `res/values/colors.xml`, the launcher icon's two drawables, `ic_stat_trip.xml`, `widget_mark.xml`, `widget_home.xml`; the report (`ReportPage.kt`, `ReportBlocks.kt`, `ReportLayout.kt`, `ReportText.kt`, `ReportPdf.kt`, `ReportDocuments.kt`, `ReportTexts.kt`); `TransferFiles.kt`; `SupportTile.kt`; `app/build.gradle.kts`; `changelog.json` and the page made from it; the five living documents, `CLAUDE.md` (the name twice, the website's address once), one line at the top of this log and of ADR-001 to ADR-009. **New:** `AppMark.kt`, `ReportTypefaces.kt`; `res/font/archivo.ttf`, `ibm_plex_mono_medium.ttf`, `ibm_plex_mono_semibold.ttf` with `licenses/Archivo-OFL.txt` and `IBMPlexMono-OFL.txt`; the tests `BrandNameTest`, `StoredNamesTest`, `AppMarkTest`, `MiloExportImportsTest`, `ColorMeasures.kt`, and a real export file written by the last MilO build as their fixture; ADR-010. **Gone:** `res/font/sora.ttf`, `licenses/Sora-OFL.txt`, the string `app_mark`, the text style `markLetter`, the report's text styles for the mark's letter.
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **The application ID is to change, and the code was made ready without choosing one.** Shawn deleted the app's entry in the Play Console during this work, and a deleted entry's ID cannot be counted on to be free. So the ID became one value in `app/build.gradle.kts` (`miloApplicationId`, with `-Pmilo.applicationId=…` to build under another without touching the file), still `com.shawnkowalchuk.milo` until he has chosen. Nothing else had to change for it: the manifest already took `${applicationId}` for the report files' provider, and the code already asked Android for the package name everywhere (the widget's views, the install source, the system screens it opens, the report files' addresses). The `namespace` stays: it is the code's package.
  - **A new ID makes the rebranded app a second app, not an update.** That turned "an update must lose nothing" into "an export from the old app must import into the new one", which is what was proven (below).
  - **One renamed word was the name of a stored file.** The safety copy an import keeps was found again by its file name's start, `MilO-before-import-`. Renamed with the other words, a copy that is on a phone would have been orphaned: "Put that data back" gone, and the file never removed. New copies are named after Dually and `SafetyCopies` finds both names (`TransferFilesTest`). It is the one place where a word a user can read (the Log names the file) and a name something is stored under were the same thing.
  - **The mark's letter is drawn, not written.** It was the letter "M" of the typeface, as text on the screens and the report and as a traced outline in the icons. The "D" is the design's own path, which no typeface has, so the screens draw it (`AppMark`), three drawables repeat the path, and the report draws it from four numbers. The report got a page item for it (`PageItem.MarkLetter`), and lost the two text styles that were only ever the letter.
  - **The widget's mark is the status line's own drawable.** As a picture beside the text it failed lint (`UseCompoundDrawables`); the layout is one view shorter for it and draws the same.
  - **Which typeface a text is set in is decided by its role, in `Type.kt`, and nowhere else.** Three of Material's roles were both a figure and a piece of text in different places. Two styles were added so that no screen had to choose a font: `listFigure` (the figure at the end of a list's row, which was `titleSmall`, a tile's title) and `smallTileFigure` (a sentence that was set in the app's name's style, which is a headline now).
  - **A large readout is pulled together by 0.06 of its size, and its unit is not.** Every character of a monospace has a full cell, so at 44 sp the decimal point stood in a gap as wide as a digit ("0 . 5"). The unit after it used to be pulled by the same length as the figure, which ran "km" together in a monospace; it is left as the typeface spaces it.
  - **The label's line stayed at 1.26 times the text size,** which was Sora's own. Archivo asks for 1.09, and at its own line every tile would have been lower than the layouts were made for.
  - **The report's Business tile is Amber, not the accent.** On the screens the accent tile is orange. On paper a large block of the orange behind dark words is heavy, and the design names Amber for "grounds that are to stand out without being the accent". The mark and the total stay orange.
  - **The What's new line about the rebrand does not say the old name,** because nothing a user reads may. It says "the app under its earlier name", and tells a person how to bring their trips across.
- **Proven on an emulator** (Android 16, `emulator-5790`, read-only, the debug build): `main`'s build (MilO) and this one under a throwaway ID (`com.shawnkowalchuk.milo.rebrandproof`, on the command line only) installed side by side. Each opened and showed its own trips; with a trip being recorded in MilO, Dually's database held none and its Home said "Start trip", and the other way round; both processes kept their numbers throughout. "Export all data" in MilO (4 trips, 1 sent report, 140 GPS points), "Import data" in Dually: the tables of trips and of sent reports came out the same in every column, and the 140 points the same but for their row numbers. Of the settings, the thirteen an export carries were the old app's; the connect sound's choice and the odometer's reading, which it does not carry, were not. Pictures of every screen at 393 and 411 dp wide, the launcher's list with both icons, the launch screen, the first start, the notification, the widget, the PDF, and Home, Trips, the Report screen and Settings at 360 dp and with the font at its largest.
- **The gate:** `spotlessCheck`, `lintDebug` and `assembleDebug` pass; `assembleRelease` builds minified (18,879,729 bytes unsigned, where `main`'s is 18,569,089: 0.3 MB more, all of it the fonts); `tools/changes_page.py --check` passes. **`testDebugUnitTest`: 3,097 tests, one fails on this branch alone** (the `[FINDING]` below), in eight runs of nine; in one run a second, unrelated test failed (the `[FINDING]` after it).
- **Not proven:** anything on the phone or on HyperOS; the Android Auto screen, which the emulator cannot show (unit tests hold its words and its colour); the release build on a device; a printed page.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the app's rebrand**
He gave the name, the design, the word that the mascot stays, the domain, and that the Play Console entry is made anew. These he was not asked.
1. **"Dually" on the screens, "Dually Trip Tracker" in five places:** the first start's orange tile, the Version tile, the support email's subject and last line, and the foot of every page of the report. The first start's tile used to be a sentence ("MilO logs your business driving by itself"); it is the name now, and the sentence stands under it.
2. **The tick's dot stays the accent, so it is orange.** The first design made "in order" the accent, and the brand's own drawing of the interface puts an orange dot before "Truck connected · logging". A green for "in order" would have been a colour nobody drew. Whether a tick on orange reads as "all good" at a glance is device check RB-13.
3. **What is wrong stays the old light red; Bluetooth stays blue and Android Auto violet.** The brand has no colour for them, and its Redline is for gauges only.
4. **What is waiting is Amber words on a dark amber tile,** as it was pale yellow on a dark tile; the "Fixed" tag on What's new is solid Amber with dark words, as the design draws a small tag. A pressed link is Amber, because the design's Ember is too dark for words on a tile.
5. **Headlines are 112 percent wide,** the width the design's own drawing of the interface uses for "Dually" in a top line; its 125 is for the name at poster size.
6. **Readouts in IBM Plex Mono:** the kilometres of a tile and their unit, the odometer's wheels, a figure at the end of a list's row, a figure typed into its own field, Setup's count, the Log's time and tags; on the report the two large figures, the times, the kilometres, the subtotals, the total and the odometer. Not a figure inside a sentence.
7. **Comments in the code keep the old name** until the code beside them is changed: 1,600 lines that no user sees, in files other branches are working on (ADR-010, decision 3).
8. **The encyclopedia's three mascot entries and its Website entry were not brought up to date.** The mascot is reviewed apart; the website's entry is rewritten by the website's own change, and two edits to the same lines would only collide.

**`[FINDING]` One unit test fails on this branch alone, and passes when the website's branch is with it**
`SupportTileTest`, "the website's Support link is the address the app writes to", reads `website/index.html` and expects the app's support address in it. The app's address is `support@dually.top` on this branch; the page on this branch still says `support@milotriplog.top`, and is changed by the website's branch (`feat/rebrand-website`, whose page has the new address four times, read with `git show`). That branch's own entry in this log says the same test fails there, the other way round. **So neither can be merged green by itself: the website's first, then this one on top of it, or both together.** The test is right and was not weakened. `website/changes.html` will also differ between the two branches (this one's list of changes, that one's page frame): run `python3 tools/changes_page.py` once after they meet.

**`[FINDING]` A second unit test failed once in nine runs, and it is not the rebrand's**
In the last run of the whole gate, with the release build's R8 running beside the tests, `HomeWidgetViewModelTest`, "a switch that cannot be stored is said on the tile and in the log, and not applied", failed before its first line: `UncaughtExceptionsBeforeTest`, carrying "Dispatchers.Main was accessed when the platform dispatcher was absent and the test dispatcher was unset". That is another test's coroutine, which came back from `Dispatchers.IO` after its own test had ended and put the main dispatcher away, and the next test to start was handed its exception. Five runs before it and three straight after it had only the one failure above. Nothing in this change touches a ViewModel or a dispatcher. **Which test leaks was not found,** and whether `main` does the same was not tried: it showed once, on a busy Mac. If it shows in CI, the test to look for is one whose ViewModel starts work on `Dispatchers.IO` and does not wait for it.

**`[FINDING]` Archivo's width axis does take effect, in Compose and in the PDF**
A variable font that is asked the wrong way draws at its defaults and nothing fails, and Archivo's default is weight 600 at width 100. Measured on the emulator with three builds that differed in one number: the top line's "Dually" (17 sp) is 138 px wide at width 100, 150 px at 112 and 166 px at 125, and the screen's name beside it 111, 121 and 134. In the PDF, poppler's `pdffonts` lists the app's name as `ArchivoRoman_800wght_112wdth`, the text as `ArchivoRoman_400wght`, `ArchivoRoman_500wght` and `Archivo-SemiBold`, and the figures as `IBMPlexMono-Medium` and `IBMPlexMono-SemiBold`. Both settings are named in every entry of a family and on every paint, and `TypefaceTest` reads them back.

**`[FINDING]` The first start and What's new were seen running for the first time**
Both entries of the encyclopedia said "never run anywhere". On the emulator a new install opened the first start's page, OK led to Setup, "Done, go to Settings" to Settings; the Version tile opened What's new with its tags. Their device checks (OB-1 to OB-7, WN-1 to WN-9) were not gone through.

**`[FINDING]` Android 16's emulator shows no status-bar icon for the trip's notification, and the small icon was seen once all the same**
The trip's notification is silent, and Android 16 keeps a silent notification's icon out of the status bar and shows the launcher icon in the shade. The small icon, the square with the D cut out, was seen in the status bar once, by chance, in two pictures taken a minute before a trip was started: for a notification that was gone when the shade was opened to read it, most likely the daily "No trip recorded today", which is withdrawn when a trip starts. It is drawn as the path says: the picture is in the evidence folder, beside a drawing of the same path made on the Mac. Which notification it was is a guess. Device check RB-11.

**`[FINDING]` At 360 dp with the font at its largest, words break in the middle, and they did before**
"Setup needs attentio-n" in the amber tile and "Octobe-r report" in the top line. The build before the rebrand breaks the same words at that size, a letter or two earlier, because Sora was wider. No figure is cut off at 360 dp or at the largest font size. Not a regression; not fixed here.

**`[DEBT]` An export does not carry the odometer's readings, and that matters now**
Known since 2026-10-07 (the `TODO(debt)` in `TransferStorage.kt`): the readings, the parked limit, the unit, the daily check, the sounds' choices and the widget's rate are not in the export file, because adding them needs a new format and a reader for both. It did not matter while an update kept the settings file. **With a new application ID the export is the only way across,** and those are set again by hand. All but one can be: the odometer's earlier readings, each with its day, cannot be typed in again, so a report for an earlier period prints an estimate where the old app printed a reading. The format was not extended in this change, on instruction. **Cost to fix later:** format 4 with a reader for 1 to 4, and it would have to be in the old app's last build to help anyone, which means one more release under the old name.

**`[DEBT]` The pictures of the old look are still in the repository's front page**
The README shows `website/screenshots/`, which are the lime interface with the M and "MilO": the third thing Google named. They are the website's files and were not touched. They have to be made anew from the rebranded app before the listing is sent in, and the pictures taken for this change (393 and 411 dp, in the evidence folder) are debug-build pictures of test data, not listing material.

**`[DEBT]` The mascot shows the old colour and the old letter** (paid later the same day: "The mascot is rebranded", at the top)
He is green with an "M" on his chest, and he walks over Home at a start of the app. Left on Shawn's word ("do not change the maskot tell we review together"); ADR-010 lists him among what has to be settled before a release.

**`[CHANGE]` Twelve built-in sounds, and one "Sounds" tile with a screen for each sound**
Phone only; nothing on the Android Auto screen or the widget. **Built, not yet proven on the phone, and nobody has heard the ten new sounds from a phone.** Shawn, 2026-10-13: "can you please give me some ideas for some more free sounds to include in the release internal. also i would like to have the audio section more compact. not the entire list of sounds under each connect sound and trip start sound." Of seventeen ideas he chose ten: "short ones (1, 2, 3, 10, 11, 12) suit the connect sound; the livelier ones (4, 7, 8, 14) suit the trip start", which are a seatbelt chime, a key fob, a turn signal, a counter bell, a coin and a sonar ping, and a horn tap, an engine rev, a burnout and a race start. Of three layouts drawn for him he chose "A: one tile, two rows; tapping a row opens the list on its own screen". He was sent the ten as files to listen to, and may still ask for some to be tuned or dropped. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Connect and trip-start sounds (with "What moved where"), Settings, Design system; device checks SD-1 to SD-20.
- **New:** ten sounds in `app/src/main/res/raw/` (`sound_*.wav`, 817,614 bytes) and the script that makes them, `tools/make_more_sounds.py`. `data/settings/BuiltInSounds.kt`; `platform/trip/BundledSounds.kt`; `feature/settings/SoundsTile.kt`, `SoundListScreen.kt`, `SoundListRows.kt`, `SoundListState.kt`, `SoundListViewModel.kt`, `SoundWords.kt`; `core/designsystem/component/LinkSwitchRow.kt`; `app/SoundListEntry.kt`; `res/values/strings_sounds.xml`. **Gone:** `feature/settings/SoundTile.kt`. **Changed:** `SoundListStorage.kt`, `SettingsStore.kt`, `MiloSettings.kt`, `TransferStorage.kt` (a comment), `TripSoundPlayer.kt`, `TripStartSounds.kt`, `OwnTripSound.kt`, `SettingsScreen.kt`, `SettingsUiState.kt`, `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `SettingsScreenPreview.kt`, `MiloBackStack.kt`, `MiloNavigation.kt`, and comments in `AppContainer.kt` and `HomeWidgetTile.kt`. **Not touched:** `TripService.kt`, `TripWorker.kt`, the trip rules, the party, the run's sounds.
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **An update changes nothing because nothing is converted.** Which built-in sound a moment plays is one more stored value for each of the two (`connect_built_in_sound`, `driving_off_built_in_sound`), and "no such value" reads as the first sound of that moment's list: the chirp, the chime. Every settings file written before today is such a file. No start-up step rewrites anything, so there is nothing that could go wrong at the first start after the update.
  - **The first of the list does three more jobs,** and they are one rule (`firstBuiltIn`): a stored name this version does not know (a later version's sound, after a restore, an import or a step back) plays it; a sound of his own that cannot be played falls back on it; and a sound of his own that is removed leaves it. The unknown name is left in the file, so the version that knows it plays it again.
  - **Choosing a sound of his own forgets the built-in sound chosen before it,** in the same write. Otherwise "his own sound cannot be played" and "his own sound was removed" would each have to choose between the first of the list and whatever he had chosen once, and the Log's line for the fallback would name a sound he no longer expects. A file that names both (which only a build from before today can write, handed a newer file) plays his own and falls back on the first.
  - **A sound is named by its resource, never looked up by a name** (`BundledSounds.kt`). The release build removes resources nothing refers to; a lookup by name would have compiled, passed every unit test and played nothing in the release build. `BuiltInSoundFilesTest` reads the resource table the other way round, to hold each sound to the file with its name.
  - **The Log's old words are kept for the two old sounds:** "playing the bundled chirp", "playing the bundled chime". The device checklist and earlier entries of this log quote them.
  - **`StartSound.Usual` got its third value with a default,** the first of the list, so that `TripStartSoundsTest` could stay exactly as it was (one import was added, for a setter that moved). That test builds what the service was handed before, for all sixteen ways the switches and his own sounds can stand; it passing unchanged is the proof that a MilO in which nothing was chosen is handed the same.
  - **The sounds' screens have a ViewModel of their own, and the two files of the 2026-10-11 `[DEBT]` are under 300 lines again** without being split for it: `SettingsViewModel.kt` 276 (was 324; choosing, playing, adding and removing moved to `SoundListViewModel`, the two switches stayed), `SettingsStore.kt` 298 (was 326; its three setters for the sounds stand in `SoundListStorage.kt` beside the sounds' other storage, as extension functions like every other `…Storage.kt`). No behaviour changed in either.
  - **Which sound's screen the file picker was opened from needs no saved value any more.** It was a remembered `TripSound` on the Settings screen; now it is the screen itself, which the back stack brings back with its sound.
  - **Play stops when the screen is left** (`TripSoundPlayer.stop`, new). Until today nothing stopped a sound started from Settings; a clip of his own can be minutes long and is played as an alarm. The trip service's player is never stopped this way.
- **What it weighs.** The release APK, built unsigned the same way from `main` (c011b92) and from this change: 18,569,089 bytes where it was 17,692,537, which is 876,552 more (856 KB, 4.95 %). The ten files are 817,614 of that, in the APK byte for byte and stored, not packed; the rest (58,938) is code and words.
- **Tests.** 3,064 pass in the gate on this Mac (3,013 before; 51 more): `BuiltInSoundsTest` 18, `ChosenBuiltInSoundsTest` 8, `BuiltInSoundFilesTest` 4, `SoundListStateTest` 9, `SoundListViewModelTest` 10, one more in `SettingsViewModelTest` and one in `TransferStorageTest`. Existing tests that were changed, and why: `SettingsUiStateTest`, `ScheduleWeekStateTest` and `ReportSettingsUiStateTest` (the screen's state lost the sounds' passing values, so `settingsUiState` has one argument fewer; the three sound cases of the first moved to `SoundListStateTest`); `SettingsViewModelTest` (two constructor arguments fewer); `OwnTripSoundTest` (`useBuiltIn` names the sound; "going back when the built-in sound is already in use" now finds the choice written down by name, and still the same sound); `SoundListStorageTest` (one expected value names the chirp and the chime); and one import each in `TripStartSoundsTest`, `BackupAftermathTest`, `NothingRecordedCheckWaitTest`. **Still without a unit test, as before:** the player's own turn-taking and its fallback when a file fails, which need Android's player. Both were seen on the emulator (the `[FINDING]` below). What a moment hands the player, and what stands behind a sound of his own, is pure and tested.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, started for this on its own port, read-only, without a window and without sound, and shut down; the phone not touched): the two `[FINDING]`s below. The stills, the stitched pages and the logs are in `scratchpad/design-shots/sounds/`.
- **Not run:** on the phone. **Not heard:** anywhere. **Not tried:** TalkBack itself (what a screen reader is told was read from the screen's accessibility tree, not listened to); HyperOS's file picker; an update of the release build signed with the real key.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the sounds**
He chose the ten sounds and layout A. These points he was not asked. The first nine were set for this work before it began; none of them was changed. The rest were taken in it.
1. **Twelve built-in sounds, one list for both moments;** in each moment's list the sounds made for it stand first.
2. **The names:** Chirp, Chime, Seatbelt chime, Key fob, Turn signal, Counter bell, Coin, Sonar ping, Horn tap, Engine rev, Burnout, Race start. Where the sound in use is named, a built-in one is just its name, no longer "Built-in chirp".
3. **Nothing changes for a MilO that is updated;** a stored choice this version does not know plays that moment's first sound.
4. **One tile, "Sounds",** with the rows "Connect" and "Trip start": name, sound in use, arrowhead, and the switch at the end, pressed by itself.
5. **The list on its own screen,** opened as the pairing screen is: when it plays; "Built in"; "Your sounds", each with its Remove; "Add a sound"; Play on every row; a press chooses at once.
6. **Played as before:** as an alarm, one at a time, the second waits; the fallback is the moment's first sound; the party's pop and the run's sounds untouched.
7. **WAV, no new dependency;** a sound may be written at 22.05 kHz if it loses nothing by it.
8. **Phone only.**
9. **Two lines in the list of changes,** under 0.3.1.
10. **Six of the ten are written at half the rate:** the `[FINDING]` below.
11. **Their level is the chirp's,** 55 % of full scale. The copies he listened to were written at 80 % (3.3 decibels louder), for a computer's speakers.
12. **A sound of his own, once chosen, forgets the built-in choice before it** (the `[CHANGE]` above). So Remove, and a copy that is gone, lead to the chirp or the chime, never to "the one before".
13. **"Remove" stands beside every one of his own sounds,** in use or not. Until today only the one in use on a tile could be removed, by a button under the list. Still without a question first: nothing is lost that cannot be added again from the phone.
14. **Play is a square with the triangle at the end of each row,** the design's square button, and "Remove" a small button with the word, as the pairing screen has "Pair" at the end of a device's row. A word for Play on all twelve rows would have been a column of the same word.
15. **Play on a sound of his own that cannot be played is heard as the first sound of that screen's list,** as a trip would play it, with the Log's line. The alternative, silence, would say nothing at all on the screen.
16. **Leaving the screen stops Play** (Back, the bar, MilO put away, the file picker), and so does Remove on the sound that is playing. The work order described this as how it was; it was not, and is now.
17. **"Add a sound" stands inside "Your sounds",** under the rows, and a refused file's sentence under it, moved into view. While he has added none: "None yet. A sound you add from this phone can be used for both sounds."
18. **The sentence that the sounds play like an alarm is the last line of each sound's screen;** the sentence on when the sound plays is the first. Neither is on the tile.
19. **What a screen reader is told.** The row's words are one button: "Connect, Seatbelt chime, button, double tap to choose the connect sound". The switch has a name of its own, the sound's full name: "Connect sound, switch, on". On the screens, a row is a radio button with its name; Play is "Play Seatbelt chime"; Remove is "Remove r2d2.mp3".
20. **One new shared part, `LinkSwitchRow`,** and nothing else new in the design system (Design system's entry says why no existing part would do).
21. **The Log:** a built-in sound is "the bundled seatbelt chime" and so on, after the two old names; a choice is "Connect sound: the bundled coin is now used" (it was "the built-in sound is used again").
22. **How the choice travels:** Android's backup carries it, and a restore keeps it; an export file does not, and an import leaves this phone's choice (the `[DEBT]` below).
23. **The script writes into `res/raw/` when it is run with no argument,** and into a folder that is named, to listen first. Its list says how each sound is written.
24. **The stored names** are the script's file names without "sound_" (`seatbelt_chime`), and `chirp` and `chime`.
25. **Removing a sound later** (if he drops one after listening) is three deletions and nothing for the phones: a phone that had chosen it reads a name it does not know, and plays the first of the list.

**`[FINDING]` The ten sounds as files, and the six at half the rate**
- **The script is the one that made what he listened to.** Run as it came (`scratchpad/more-sounds/make_more_sounds.py`) with a level of 0.8, it gives the ten files he was sent, byte for byte. Run with its own default, 0.55, it gives the sounds at the app's level. In `tools/` every sound's own function is unchanged, line for line; what was added is the header, a default folder, and how each sound is written.
- **Four are written whole,** at 44.1 kHz, and are byte for byte that script's files at 0.55: the turn signal, the counter bell, the engine rev and the burnout. They have noise in them (a relay's click, a strike, an engine, a tyre), and 13 to 40 decibels under their whole is above what half the rate can hold.
- **Six are written with every second sample,** which is a file at 22.05 kHz, the chirp's and the chime's rate, and half the size: the seatbelt chime, the key fob, the coin, the sonar ping, the horn tap and the race start. They are notes alone. Measured on the whole files: 86 to 96 decibels under each sound's whole lies above 11 kHz (that is the rounding of 16-bit samples, not sound), and nothing within 60 decibels of its loudest note lies above 8.4 kHz (the key fob's third harmonic; 2.7 to 6.7 kHz for the other five). Each of the six files in the app was held against the whole one: exactly every second sample, all of them.
- **Why thinned, and not made at the lower rate.** That was tried first. The notes are built by adding up a phase sample by sample, so at another rate each harmonic starts a little differently: the same notes, but not the same samples, and the level, which is set by the loudest sample, moved with it. Made whole and then thinned, a file is what he listened to and nothing else.
- **What it saved:** 283,560 bytes of 1,101,174.
- **The three sounds MilO had are byte for byte what they were,** and so are the run's two: their sha256 before and after are in the evidence folder. The new script cannot write them: it names ten other files.
- **Not known:** how any of the ten sounds from the phone's speaker. The engine and the burnout's first half are built on notes of 48 to 240 Hz with their harmonics, and a phone gives little below 300 Hz.

**`[FINDING]` The sounds on the emulator**
Android 16, 1080 by 2400 pixels, 411 dp wide (and set to 393 dp, and to the largest font size, for the stills). **The emulator had no sound.** What played was read from two logs together: MilO's own Log names the sound; Android's audio log (`dumpsys audio`, "playback activity") says that MilO made a player, that it was an alarm, at which rate, and when it started and stopped, which for a sound played to its end is the file's length. An emulator has no truck: every trip was started with Start, and a drive was fed in as GPS fixes at 55 km/h.
- **The page.** Settings is 298 dp shorter out of the box and 565 dp shorter with one sound of his own added (the two tiles were 442 and 709 dp from the top of the first to the bottom of the second; the one tile is 144 dp).
- **Play on each of the twelve rows:** twelve players, each `USAGE_ALARM`, each at its file's rate, each stopped 0.01 to 0.09 seconds after its file's length; twelve lines "Settings screen, Play pressed. Connect sound: playing the bundled …", each naming its row.
- **Cut off, again, and stopped.** Play on the key fob 1.1 seconds into the burnout: the burnout stopped and the key fob began 40 thousandths of a second later. Play twice on Race start: stopped at 1.26 seconds and begun again. Back 0.8 seconds into a three-second clip of his own: stopped. The Home key: stopped. The same clip left alone: 3.06 seconds.
- **Choosing.** A press on a row marked it and played nothing; Back led to Settings at the same place, and the tile named the sound. His own sounds: a file added (marked, named on the tile), a second one added, each chosen and played, the one in use removed (gone, its copy gone, and "Chirp" marked, not the seatbelt chime chosen before it); a text file with an audio name refused in red under "Add a sound", the marked row unchanged.
- **Trips.** With the coin for the connect and the engine rev for the trip's start: "Connect sound: playing the bundled coin" at the press (a player at 22.05 kHz for 0.51 s), and 4.1 seconds later, at the first fix, "Trip-start sound: playing the bundled engine rev" (44.1 kHz, 1.46 s). With the connect sound switched off: no player and no line for it, and the engine rev as ever. With the trip-start sound switched off, in the release build: the connect sound only.
- **One waits for the other.** An eight-second clip of his own as the connect sound, the truck driving from the start: the first fix came 4.2 seconds in, and the engine rev began 8.1 seconds in, 17 thousandths of a second after the clip had ended, not over it. (With a short connect sound it began 4.1 seconds in.) This is the player's turn-taking, which no unit test can reach.
- **The fallback.** The copy of the sound in use was removed by hand (`run-as … rm`, which only a debug build allows). At the next trip: "Connect sound could not play the chosen sound: java.io.FileNotFoundException: … (No such file or directory)", then "Connect sound: playing the bundled chirp", and a player at 22.05 kHz for 1.49 s: the chirp, not the coin chosen before his own.
- **The update.** The build of `main` was installed first, a clip of his own made its connect sound and its trip-start sound switched off. This change's build installed over it showed "Connect: my-own-clip.wav" with its switch on and "Trip start: Chime" with its switch off, and played the clip at the next trip.
- **A fresh install:** "Chirp" and "Chime", both on; a trip played the chirp (1.49 s) and then the chime (0.83 s).
- **The minified release build,** in a copy signed with Android's throwaway debug key, installed over the debug build: the tile with the settings it found; a built-in sound chosen for each moment and named on the tile; Play on six rows; a file refused, one added, chosen, played and removed; a trip with "Connect sound: playing the bundled sonar ping" and "Trip-start sound: playing the bundled burnout", and the two players to match; a switch off. **And the saved screens:** with "Trip-start sound" open, MilO's process was ended in the background (`am kill`) and MilO opened again on that screen, Back leading to Settings. That is the one place a name is read back at run time (the screen's key, with which of the two sounds, saved by the constant's name), which is why it was tried in the build R8 rewrites. All twelve sound files are in that APK, under short names, byte for byte.
- **The largest font size** (twice the usual), at 393 dp: the tile's rows grow and nothing is cut or overlaps; on a sound's screen the name of a sound of his own breaks into lines of five or six letters beside its two buttons.
- **The build of the commit itself,** on the emulator started afresh: the debug build installed new ("Chirp" and "Chime"; the turn signal chosen for the connect and played; a clip added, in use, played and removed, and "Chirp" marked again; the race start chosen for the trip's start; a trip with "Connect sound: playing the bundled turn signal" and "Trip-start sound: playing the bundled race start"), then its minified release build over it, signed with the throwaway key (the tile as it was left; the horn tap chosen; two rows played; the screen brought back after its process was ended, the horn tap marked; a trip with the turn signal and the horn tap). Its release APK is the size of the one run before it, to the byte: between the two, only comments and these documents changed.
- **The file picker's answer finds its screen again.** With the picker in front, opened from "Connect sound", MilO's process was ended (a `kill` of the debug build's own process, as Android does when it needs the memory); the file picked then started a new process, which opened on "Connect sound", added the file and made it the connect sound, and left the trip-start sound as it was. The same with Android's "Don't keep activities" switched on, from "Trip-start sound". Until today a saved value on the Settings screen said which sound the picker was for; now the screen the back stack brings back says it.
- **Once, the emulator's Android restarted itself, and it was not MilO.** In that second session, as a trip ended and MilO took back its request for GPS fixes, Android's own system process hung, and Android's watchdog restarted it a minute later: "Blocked in handler on foreground thread (android.fg) for 66s", in `GnssLocationProvider.stopNavigating` and `GnssStatusProvider.onReportStatus`, the emulator's GPS code waiting on a lock of its own. MilO's process did not crash, and Android's crash log held nothing of MilO's; after the restart Android started MilO for its boot broadcast, as after a reboot. Once in eleven trips ended that morning, each of them fed its fixes from the Mac, one a second, which no phone does; nothing of this change is near that code. The check it interrupted was run again and passed. **If an emulator "goes back to the home screen by itself" during such a test, look for `WATCHDOG KILLING SYSTEM PROCESS` in logcat before suspecting MilO.**
- Android's crash log held nothing of MilO's throughout.

**`[DEBT]` The built-in sound chosen for each of the two sounds is not in the export file**
It means the same on any phone, since every MilO carries the same twelve, and so belongs with the settings that travel. Adding it changes the export file's form, which needs a new format version and a reader for both. An import leaves this phone's choice as it is; Android's backup carries it. It waits with the parked limit, the daily check's two, the odometer's readings, the widget's rate, the unit and the trip-start sound's switch for the format that takes them all (`TODO(debt)` in `TransferStorage.kt`). Cost later: one field in `TransferredSettings`, the export's mapping, and a format 3.

**`[DEBT]` Two rough edges of the sounds' screens**
Both are tagged `TODO(debt)` where they are, in `SoundListScreen.kt` and `SoundListRows.kt`.
- **Opened with a sound of his own in use, a sound's screen shows no marked row** until it is scrolled: "Your sounds" is under the twelve. The order was decided (built in first), and the tile he came from names the sound. If it is found confusing: bring the marked row into view when the screen opens. Cost later: a few lines.
- **At the largest font size a long file name beside Play and Remove breaks into very short lines.** Readable, and every button can be pressed. If it matters: put Remove on a line of its own under the name at large font sizes, as a sent report's Remove stands under its figure. Cost later: one row's layout.

### 2026-10-11

**`[DECISION]` The mascot's source and the marketing kit are no longer in the repository**
Shawn: "can we make the website/marketing/design masctot on github no accessable becuase the repo is public?" GitHub has no private folder in a public repository. He was given three ways (the whole repository private; `design/` and `marketing/` moved out; leave it) and chose to move the two out, then: "just store them locally. git ignored". `website/` stays: it is what the site is deployed from, and its pages are public on the web anyway.
- **What changed.** `design/` (the mascot's Blender file, the scripts that build, animate, draw and pack him, his renders; 56 files, 19 MB) and `marketing/` (125 files, 28 MB) were taken out of the tree and added to `.gitignore`. On his Mac they stay where they were, in the project's folder. Nothing in the build, the tests or CI reads either folder; six comments in the app and one in `tools/make_run_sounds.py` name scripts there, and still say where the numbers and pictures came from.
- **What this does not do.** Every version of both folders up to today is still in the repository's history and in its pull requests, for anyone who looks. He was told so before he chose. Only a private repository, or a rewritten history with GitHub's help, takes that back; neither was asked for.
- **The pictures themselves are public as before:** the app carries the mascot's packed frames (`app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/`) and the website its own (`website/mascot/`).
- **What it costs.** The two folders are no longer backed up by a push. A copy as of this day is in `~/MilO-backups/design-and-marketing-2026-10-11/` on the same Mac; a backup off that Mac is his to arrange. And a git worktree does not have them: work on the mascot is done in the project's own folder, or with a copy of `design/` put into the worktree, where the same `.gitignore` keeps it out.

**`[FINDING]` An outside review of the code, and what held up**
Shawn had another AI model (DeepSeek) read the project on 2026-10-11 and passed on its five findings. Each was checked against the code before anything was done; this entry is here so that the one that was wrong is not tried again.
- **Files over 300 lines (STANDARDS section 3): true.** Its counts were exact. The `[DEBT]` entry below.
- **"The `@OptIn(ExperimentalCoroutinesApi::class)` marks are dead weight since kotlinx-coroutines 1.7": false.** With every one of them taken out, `compileDebugKotlin` fails on 1.11.0 with seven errors ("This declaration needs opt-in"), one for each `flatMapLatest`: in `HomeViewModel` (three), `TripsViewModel`, `ReportSources`, `EventLogViewModel` and `HomeWidget`. The comment in `HomeViewModel` says the same. They stay until the library drops the mark.
- **`error("A row without a fix has no button")` in `SetupTexts.kt`: true, and out of reach.** Its one caller asks only for a row that has a fix. The `[CHANGE]` entry below.
- **`runBlocking` in `PairedTruck.now()`: true, and meant** (ADR-002; the comment on the class). Up to one second on the thread of a Bluetooth broadcast, once per process; after that the answer is in memory. Nothing changed.
- **"The mascot's pictures are the largest part of the download": true, with old numbers.** On 2026-10-11 they are 4.1 MB of a 15.6 MB release file on `main` (the party 2.31 MB, the moonwalk 0.90, the wave 0.88), and about 4.3 MB with the wave's new pictures. Each weight was accepted with its animation. If the download's size ever matters this is where to take it from, the party first: it is the heaviest and plays one day a year.
- What it found in order: exact pinned dependencies, secrets outside the repository, the one clock, one inbox for the trip's triggers.

**`[CHANGE]` A Setup row without a fix can no longer ask for a button's words**
No behaviour changes. `SetupRow.fixLabelRes()` read the row's fix and stopped MilO with `error(...)` if there was none. It is `fixLabelRes(fix)` now: the fix is handed in, the `when` has no branch for "none", and the mistake cannot be written. The outside review proposed returning an empty label there. That was not done: a button without words would have hidden the mistake where the compiler now prevents it. `SetupTextsTest` holds the same words as before.

**`[DEBT]` Twelve files of the app are over the 300 lines of STANDARDS section 3**
Counted on 2026-10-11 on this branch: `ReportBlocks.kt` (456), `TripWorker.kt` (381), `TripService.kt` (379), `TripStatusScreen.kt` (378), `TripLedger.kt` (351), `Color.kt` (344), `AppContainer.kt` (342), `TripDao.kt` (341), `TripLogText.kt` (336), `SettingsStore.kt` (326), `SettingsViewModel.kt` (324), `ReviewPrompts.kt` (301). The review counted eleven on `main`; the twelfth, `ReviewPrompts.kt`, came with the review card and is over by one line. An earlier entry of 2026-10-07 names four of them at fewer lines; they have grown with nearly every feature since.
- **Leave alone:** `Color.kt` (the palette and its tokens in one place) and `AppContainer.kt` (the one list of what is built from what). Splitting either makes it harder to read.
- **Split after 0.3.1 is out, each in a branch of its own (STANDARDS section 6), with no behaviour change:** `ReportBlocks.kt`, `TripStatusScreen.kt` (also over the 200 lines of a Composable's file), `TripLogText.kt`, `TripDao.kt`, `SettingsStore.kt`, `SettingsViewModel.kt`, `ReviewPrompts.kt`.
- **Wait with the three that record a trip:** `TripWorker.kt`, `TripService.kt`, `TripLedger.kt`. They are 50 to 80 lines over, they are the reviewed engine (ADR-002), and the phone has driven with them for a week. Moving their code about for a line count, before trip detection has had more days in the truck, risks the one thing the app is for. Split them with the next change that has to open them anyway.

**`[CHANGE]` The mascot runs across the Report screen when a report has been sent**
Phone only; the Android Auto screen is not touched. **Built, not yet proven on the phone; its two sounds have been heard nowhere.** Shawn, 2026-10-10: "i was thinking one where the mascot runs on to the screen. than he runs in one spot in the center for 2 seconds. than sort of turns and slides out. if we can make smoke from his shoes or something visual that would be cool. the lights on his butt should be off tell he stops and slides and than turn on like a car stopping." He approved the film of the preview (commit 3de174e; `design/mascot/README.md`, "The run and the slide"). On 2026-10-11: "i want this animation included. and used when the user completes sending a report by email"; asked when exactly, he chose the answer "I sent it", not "Mark as sent"; and of three films with sounds made in code he chose the third. The app now plays that film over the Report screen after "I sent it", with its two sounds. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, The run after a report is sent; ADR-007, the run's addendum; device checks RS-1 to RS-14.
- **New:** three pictures in `app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/` (`mascot_run_in.webp`, `mascot_run_on_the_spot.webp`, `mascot_brake_and_slide.webp`, 1.52 MB together) and two sounds in `res/raw/` (`report_run_whirr.wav`, `report_run_squeal.wav`, 277 KB). `core/designsystem/component/MascotRun.kt`, `MascotRunPicture.kt`, `MascotRunPictures.kt`; `feature/sentrun/SentRunViewModel.kt`; `platform/sentrun/SentRunSounds.kt`; `app/SentRunObjects.kt`. Outside the app: `design/mascot/scripts/pack_run_slide.py`, `tools/make_run_sounds.py`. **Changed:** `MiloApp.kt` (draws it: three lines), `MiloReportEntry.kt` ("I sent it" tells two listeners), `ReviewObjects.kt` (`CelebrationThenCard` waits for the run), `AppContainer.kt`, `Mascot.kt` (`MascotPlayed` may draw nothing; two functions other files can now reach), `MascotParty.kt` (`everyFrame` and the half frame's start, shared).
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **It is the greeting's leg and the party's two pictures, put together.** The run in is a sheet the app steps through and moves, exactly as a greeting's leg (`SteppedLeg`, `MascotPose`); the rest is two animated pictures the app starts one after the other, exactly as the party's (`everyFrame`, the first frame shown by the app, the picture started half a frame's time before that frame is up). No second way of playing the mascot was built.
  - **From the spot on, nothing is moved.** He is still going while he brakes and slides, and his smoke stays where it was made. A picture of him on the spot would have to be moved in step with its frames, which Android does not tell (ADR-007's first lesson). So the frames were laid where the film laid them when they were packed, on a picture as wide as he goes, and the app only puts that picture on the screen's spot.
  - **The review card's turn hangs on the run, not the run on the card.** He runs after every "I sent it"; a moment for the card is due after one of them at most. So the run is drawn by a part of its own (`SentRunOverReport`), and the place #60 kept for "what MilO plays first" only waits for it (`afterCelebration`).
  - **Leaving the Report screen is one of the ways a run ends, and Google Play must not be asked then.** The moment it waited for is cancelled a drawing of the screen later; in between, a run that says "over" could have had Google Play asked for a card for a screen that was just left. `afterCelebration` asks whether the Report screen is still on top, by what the app's own level wrote down as it was composed. A unit test holds the order in which it would have gone wrong.
  - **The sounds are made ready at "I sent it" and started on their frames.** The drawing calls back in the drawing of the screen that first shows him at the spot, and in the one that first shows his brake lights lit; the ViewModel starts a player that has been waiting since the answer (the `[FINDING]` below).
