  - **Nothing of the Report feature changed.** `ReportRecords` told one listener and tells one function still; the app's entry hands it a function that tells two.
- **What it weighs.** The release APK, built unsigned the same way from the commit before this one and from this one, is 17,692,537 bytes where it was 15,858,110: 1,834 KB more, 11.6 %. The five new files are in it byte for byte, stored and not packed again (`res/MY.webp`, `res/CX.webp`, `res/Ts.webp`, `res/W1.wav`, `res/mP.wav`): 1,524 KB of pictures and 277 KB of sound; the rest is code.
- **Tests.** 3,013 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,961 before; 52 more). `MascotRunTest` 21, `SentRunViewModelTest` 17, `SentRunAtISentItTest` 9, and five more in `MascotPicturesTest` for the three files. **No existing test was changed** but that one's opening comment, which counts the pictures.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, started for this on its own port, read-only, without a window and without sound, and shut down; the phone not touched). The debug build: "I sent it" after a real hand-over to the email app (Gmail took the report), at 411 dp three times and at 274, 393 and 610 dp; "Not sent" and "Mark as sent"; Back pressed before he was drawn, as he got to the spot, while he ran on the spot and while he slid; MilO put away while he ran on the spot; the phone's animations off, and a start from nothing with them off, and on again; both of MilO's sounds off; the ringer on vibrate and on silent; the copy named as installed by Google Play, for the review card. The wave, the moonwalk and the party on demand, with this build and with the build before it, side by side. The minified release build, in a copy signed with Android's throwaway debug key, once. And the build of this commit itself, installed new on a freshly started emulator, three times. MilO stayed open throughout, and Android's crash log held nothing of MilO's. The measurements are the `[FINDING]` below; the stills, sheets and films are in `scratchpad/design-shots/report-run/`.
- **Not run:** on the phone. **Not heard:** anywhere.
- **Also in this change, and belonging to the one below:** the line "Google Play may ask for a review" in the list of changes said "never twice in one day"; it now says that MilO leaves 30 days between two requests. And the 30 days were seen on the emulator (the `[FINDING]` below).

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the run across the Report screen**
He asked for the animation of the film, after "I sent it", with the third film's sounds. These points he was not asked. The first nine were set for this work before it began; the rest were taken in it.
1. **The moment** is the one the review card goes by: "I sent it" has stored a report. The review card's turn comes when the run is over.
2. **Over the screen, like the greeting:** no press, no Back, no screen reader, nothing dimmed. Gone at once, with its sounds, when the Report screen is left or MilO is put away, and not back afterwards. Once for each "I sent it".
3. **With the phone's animations off: nothing, and no sound.** Pictures that cannot be read: nothing, no sound, one `ERROR` line.
4. **Along the top of the bottom bar, 150 dp high,** in from the left edge, the spot in the middle of the screen's width, out at the right. **Which line was looked for on the Report screen and chosen here:** the greetings' own, his soles 4 dp above the bar's tile. At its foot the Report screen has the bar, Android's gesture line under it, and above it whatever is scrolled there, often the lime button "Email the report" that was just pressed. The bar's top edge is the one floor there is. Lower he would run across the bar's four buttons; higher he would run through the tiles with nothing under his feet.
5. **Screen sounds, not signals for the road:** a phone on silent or on vibrate stays silent; silent too when both of MilO's sounds are switched off. Each started by the animation on its frame.
6. **Pictures per ADR-007, the smoke drawn into them,** and the lightest packing that still looks like the film.
7. **The halo's known limit is logged** if it shows (it does: the `[DEBT]` below).
8. **Phone only.**
9. **One "new" line in the list of changes,** under 0.3.1.
10. **The audio usage is "assistance sonification"** (`USAGE_ASSISTANCE_SONIFICATION`, content type sonification): Android's own name for a sound of the user interface. It is played on the system stream, at the volume the phone gives its own screen sounds, and Android mutes that stream when the ringer is on silent or on vibrate. The other candidates: "media" and "game" are not muted by the ringer at all; "notification" is muted by it too, but these are not notifications, and a notification's volume and Do Not Disturb rules are another thing. **MilO does not read the ringer's mode itself.** On the emulator Android did the muting and said so; on HyperOS it is a device check (RS-4).
11. **How loud:** the squeal's loudest sample at 40 % of full scale and the whirr ten decibels under it, which is what the film had in each of its two channels. The chirp, the chime and the pop have 55 %; a held note at that level is far louder to the ear than a click. One number in `tools/make_run_sounds.py` changes both.
12. **A script of their own for the sounds,** `tools/make_run_sounds.py`, and `make_party_pop.py` left alone: the three sounds MilO had are byte for byte what they were (their sha256 before and after are in the evidence folder).
13. **A player of their own,** `SentRunSounds`, and `TripSoundPlayer` left alone. That class plays as an alarm, asks for the phone's own speaker, makes a sound wait its turn and writes a line to the Log for every sound: four things these two must not do or do not need.
14. **No line in the Log for a run that plays.** One `ERROR` line if the pictures cannot be read, and one if a sound fails.
15. **The run in is a sheet of sixteen frames with its dust,** not eight of him alone as the preview's trial weighed it: the film shows a puff at each footfall, and a sheet of him alone would have had them appear all at once as he reached the spot. 60 KB.
16. **The rest is two animated pictures that stand still, cut at the brake,** and not one picture, and not a picture the app moves (the `[CHANGE]` above says why).
17. **The shadow under his feet is laid into the two animated pictures,** where the film drew it under him in every frame; in the run in the app draws it, as in the greetings.
18. **On a screen wider than 620 dp the spot is right of the middle,** 310 dp from the right edge: the drawn frames take him no further, and no frame was drawn that the film did not have. Every phone held upright is narrower.
19. **A frame is shown for 42 ms,** as in everything of the mascot's, where the film has 41.7. The seams are the party's, with the party's half frame.
20. **A second "I sent it" while he runs starts no second run;** an answer on a screen that has been left since starts none.
21. **The names:** "the run" in the documents; `MascotRun` in the design system; `sentrun` for the feature and the platform package, since "run" alone means something else in code.
22. **Nothing was done to make him stand out from the lime button.** Where it is behind him he is green on lime; the film he approved was on the dark page. It is a device check (RS-2), and his to judge.

**`[FINDING]` What the run weighs, and what was tried**
118 frames: 16 on the sheet, 57 and 45 in the two animated pictures. 1,523,866 bytes.
- **The frames were drawn again at the app's size,** 375 pixels high (2.5 to the dp), not made smaller from the film's 394. Before that, three frames were drawn again at the film's own size and held against the film's: 2 of 255 apart at most, the renderer's noise. So what the app carries is the film's frames, drawn by the same file.
- **At the film's own pixels (2.625 to the dp)** the two animated pictures came to about 1,435 KB in a trial, where they are 1,372 KB: fewer pixels buy 4 %, as the party found.
- **Where the bytes are.** On the spot a frame is 11 KB, as a greeting's. From the brake on it is 16 KB, and up to 25 KB while the trail is longest: he and his halo are 10 to 13 KB of it, the rest is smoke, every puff of which swells or shrinks in every frame. The frames after he has left a screen 411 dp wide, which only a wider screen shows, are 101 KB together.
- **Stored as the box that changed,** every frame but the first of each picture: the picture is 1,170 pixels wide and a frame's box 400 to 975. That is 2 % lighter than whole frames, and half the work for the phone.
- **The see-through channel** at 50 on the spot and at 80 from the brake on, where every frame has the glow. At 80 throughout, the first picture would weigh about 160 KB more.
- **The shadow** costs 9 KB in the first picture and 28 KB in the second.
- **The sheet:** sixteen frames with the dust 152 KB; eight of him alone were 91 KB in the preview's trial.
- **The sounds are stored as they are:** 277 KB, a seventh of what the run adds. At half their rate they would be half that, and not the sounds he chose: the hiss has a top that half the rate cannot hold.
- **Not tried:** colour under 50 (the wave found 3 % in it, and soft dials); him and the smoke as two pictures (the party found that heavier, and a puff behind his shoe would be drawn in front).
- **`pack_run_slide.py` reads its own pictures back** and holds each frame against the one that was laid, over the dark page: 0.94 of 255 a pixel on average at worst on the sheet, 0.67 and 0.71 in the animated pictures.
- **The limit in `MascotPicturesTest`:** the run's three under 1.6 MB, counted apart from the greetings' four and the party's two.

**`[FINDING]` The run on the emulator, measured**
Android 16 on an emulator whose screen is the film's size (1080 by 2400 pixels, 411 dp wide, drawn 60 times a second). Small scripts, kept out of the repository (in the evidence folder), did the reading. One found in every recorded frame which of the 118 packed pictures the app was showing and where, by holding the frame against each picture laid where the app's arithmetic puts it, over the pixels in which the picture is solid, so that the Report screen behind him does not come into it. One put a recording on the emulator's clock by Android's statistics of the frames MilO drew (`dumpsys gfxinfo framestats`; a frame an animated picture draws by itself is marked 2 there and counts) and read Android's audio log (`dumpsys audio`); the wall clock was laid on the counting clock by lines the system log shows by both. **The emulator had no sound.**
- **The film, read by the same script:** each of the 107 frames in which enough of him is on the screen shows the app's picture for the same frame, three frames on (the film begins with three frames of the empty page), within 1 pixel of the app's place.
- **Every frame in its order,** in every recording: three at 411 dp, one each at 393, 274 and 610 dp, and one of the release build. None left out, none of another place.
- **The run in.** Every frame within 0.11 pixels (0.04 dp) of where its step belongs at 411 dp; 0.27 pixels at 393, 0.39 at 274, 0.16 at 610. Blender's own drawing lets a planted foot stray 0.4 of the film's pixels, 0.15 dp; the app adds as much again at the most.
- **The seams.** The animated picture's first frame came 33 to 52 ms after the sheet's last, and stood for 65 to 68 ms where the film gives it 42. At the brake the first picture's last frame stood for 49 to 53 ms and the second's first for 64 to 67 ms. Nothing came between. The pictures that stand still are laid on whole pixels: half a pixel (0.2 dp) to the left of the exact place at 411 dp, about a pixel (0.4 dp) at 274 dp, none at 393 and 610 dp.
- **Against the film, at 411 dp,** a frame from every quarter second of each: the same pictures in the same places, the app one frame behind by the brake and two to three by the end. From the spot to the brake 2.40 to 2.50 seconds where the film has 2.375.
- **The lengths, by the rules and as recorded:** 97 frames at 274 dp, 111 at 393, 112 at 411, 124 at 610.
- **The sounds and their frames.** In seven recordings Android reported each player started between 0.3 ms before and 2.8 ms after the screen refresh its frame was drawn for; the frame was finished 17 ms after that refresh. The players had been made ready 0.8 to 1.4 seconds before the whirr. The whirr was reported stopped about 1.97 seconds after it started and the squeal about 1.34 (the files are 1.89 and 1.25 seconds long).
- **"Not sent" and "Mark as sent":** no frame of him in the recordings, and no player made.
- **Left in each part.** Back 0.4 seconds after the answer, before he was drawn: nothing drawn, the two players let go, neither started. Back as he got to the spot: gone, and the whirr cut 43 ms after it began. Back while he ran on the spot: gone, the whirr cut after 0.27 seconds, no squeal. Back while he slid: gone, the squeal cut after 0.5 seconds. The Home key while he ran on the spot: the same, and no mascot when MilO was opened again.
- **Animations off:** nothing drawn; the two players made and let go a tenth of a second later, neither started. A start from nothing with them off: Home, MilO running, nothing in the crash log (the fault of this morning's `[FIX]` is not back: nothing here follows the setting inside `snapshotFlow`). On again: he ran, with both sounds.
- **Both of MilO's sounds off:** he ran, and no player was made.
- **The ringer on vibrate, and on silent:** he ran; each player was started and, within 35 ms, marked "muted … streamVolume" by Android; the audio service listed the system stream among those the ringer mutes. With the ringer on normal no such line was written.
- **The review card after the run** (the copy named as installed by Google Play, Google's real library, the real Play Store): "I sent it" at 15:48:12.4, the squeal reported stopped at 15:48:17.3, Google's `requestInAppReview` at 15:48:17.6. Before this work it came a second after the answer. Google Play showed nothing, as it does there.
- **The 30 days, seen** (the change below): the same copy, opened on Home half a minute later with 16 recorded trips and "5 trips" never asked for, asked Google Play for nothing and wrote "…it asked Google Play to show the card less than 30 days ago, and leaves 30 days between two requests… in 30 days at the earliest".
- **The wave, the moonwalk and the party on demand, with the build before and with this one:** his green in sight in 159 recorded frames in both builds for one greeting, 160 in both for the other, and 127 and 125 for the party; a frame from every quarter second of each, side by side.
- **The release build** played the run once: every frame in its order, both sounds started on their frames.
- **Not measured:** the first frame he is seen in and the last four, in which only smoke is at the edge: fewer than 400 solid pixels of the picture are on the screen in them. And the first run after the install was recorded while this Mac was building; it dropped frames and is not counted.
- **A just-started emulator's recorder keeps fewer frames than the app draws.** The build of the commit, installed new half a minute after the emulator had started, was run three times: Android's statistics list 116, 113 and 116 frames that MilO drew, as in every other run, and the sounds started 2.42, 2.46 and 2.40 seconds apart; the recordings hold 85, 87 and 131 frames. The emulator's load was 10 at the first. A recording made there says nothing of how smoothly a run was drawn; the statistics do.

**`[FINDING]` A picture wider than the screen was drawn squeezed to the screen's width**
The two animated pictures are 468 dp wide, and a phone's screen about 400. Laid out as the party's picture is, with a size and nothing else, they were given no more room than the screen has: Compose holds a part of the screen to its parent's room unless it is told otherwise. Android then drew each picture into that room, 12 % narrower than it is and as high as ever.
- **It could not be seen.** In the recording he was a mascot, in the middle, with smoke; a sheet of stills looked right. The script that holds each recorded frame against the packed pictures found it: the run in's frames lay on the recording to 5 of 255, and no frame of the animated pictures nearer than 44, at any place. A still of the two laid over each other showed him thinner than he should be.
- **The fix** is one modifier in `MascotRun.kt` (`wrapContentSize`, unbounded, aligned at the lower left), with a comment that says why. The party's picture is never wider than the screen, and the greetings' frames are 216 and 192 dp.
- **A lesson for the next such check:** hold the recording against the pictures before looking at it.

**`[FINDING]` The first sound a MilO process plays starts a third of a second late, unless it is made ready first**
In the first build each sound's player was made on its frame, as the party's pop is. Android's audio log on the emulator: the whirr's player made at 13:36:07.976 and reported started at 13:36:08.351, 375 ms later, eight frames into the run on the spot; the squeal's, the second player of that process, 29 ms after it was made. (The party's `[FINDING]` of 2026-10-10 has the same of its pop: 82 ms for a process's first sound.) So both players are made when "I sent it" is recorded, most of a second before the first is due, and only started on their frames: 3 ms at the most between the screen refresh and the reported start, in every recording since. They are let go when the run is cut short.

**`[FINDING]` The film's sound track, read back**
No note said which sounds the third film had or how loud. The film's own track was decoded and held against what `drift()` and `whirr()` of the trial's script make, cut as `preview_run_slide.py` cuts a sound.
- **The squeal is `drift()` whole,** laid from 4 ms before the brake's frame; **the whirr is `whirr()` from 14 ms in** (the script found it to begin 20 ms in, and keeps 6 before that), faded in over 80 ms, laid from 6 ms before the frame he gets to the spot. Both at a shift of nought against the film, likeness 0.998 and 0.989.
- **In each of the film's two channels the squeal is 6 dB under what the script made, and the whirr 16:** the 10 dB it was laid under the squeal, 3 dB for the mix, and 3 dB that the film's maker takes off a one-channel sound when it makes two channels of it. The app's files have exactly those levels.
- `tools/make_run_sounds.py` holds the two functions and that cutting, in the standard library alone. What it makes before the cutting is sample for sample what the candidates were made of; two runs write the same bytes.

**`[DEBT]` The run's halo is right on a dark ground only, and on the Report screen the ground is lime**
The greetings' fault (the entries of 2026-10-10 and of this morning), where it shows far more often. The halo round his lit brake lights is stored in the pictures as red that covers a part of what lies behind. **What is behind him depends on how far the Report screen is scrolled. Seen on the emulator at 411 dp,** where the lime button "Email the report" stood just above the bar after it was pressed: from the brake until he is gone, about a second, he slides past it, and round the lights the lime is an orange patch, darker than the button. Over the dark tile above it and the dark gap under it, it is the film's red glow. **At 393 and 610 dp** the screen had been scrolled further to reach the button, the list of sent reports was behind him, and the halo was the film's glow throughout; **at 274 dp** the button was behind his shoes and the lights above it, on the dark tile. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `MascotRunPicture.kt`; device check RS-12. **Cost to fix:** as that entry says, once for all three: the glow drawn by the app as light added to the screen, which is right on any ground; or the slide's frames packed without the halo (`render_run_slide.py --plain` draws them), which loses the glow on the dark parts too. A day for the first, an hour and a new film for the second.

**`[DEBT]` What else is left rough in the run**
- **Whether HyperOS mutes these two sounds on silent and on vibrate is taken on Android's word.** What: MilO sets the kind of sound and reads no ringer mode. Why: the brief asked for the usage that does it, and stock Android does it. Cost to fix if RS-4 fails: `AudioManager.getRingerMode()` asked before a run's sounds are made ready; three lines and a test.
- **He is two to three frames behind the film by the end,** and each animated picture's first frame stands for 65 ms where the film gives it 42. It is the party's seam, shared. A shorter seam is one number (`START_AHEAD_MS`), which the party shares; changing it is a change to the party, to be measured there too.
- **With the phone's animations off the two players are made and let go unused.** Nothing is heard. Making them only once he is drawn would need one more word from the drawing to the ViewModel.
- **`MiloApp` is three lines longer:** 223 after the review card, 226 now. Its `TODO(debt)` says so.
- **`MascotPicturesTest` is 572 lines,** where it was 453: one file holds all nine pictures.
- **The sounds are 277 KB of plain samples** in the package.
- **The run was not drawn on a tablet or an unfolded phone,** where the spot is right of the middle. The arithmetic is tested; nobody has looked.

**`[CHANGE]` The review card's moments keep 30 days apart**
Phone only. **Unit tested; seen once on an emulator, in the work above (a moment reached on the day of a launch waited, with its line in the Log); not run on the phone.** After MilO has asked Google Play for its review card once (Google Play's flow launched), the next moment is not acted on until 30 days have passed. Until now the rule was "never twice in one day". ADR-009 listed the longer gap under "Left open", and the code had a `TODO(debt)` for it; both are gone. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Google Play's review card, step 6; ADR-009, decision 4 and "Decided on 2026-10-11, after the first build"; device checks RV-5, RV-7 and RV-8. No line in the list of changes: nobody using MilO will notice.
- **Changed:** `core/review/ReviewRules.kt` (`DAYS_BETWEEN_LAUNCHES`, `launchedLately`, `daysToWait`, the reason `ASKED_WITHIN_THIRTY_DAYS`, `ReviewsStored.lastLaunchAtMs`, `afterLaunch`), `data/settings/ReviewStorage.kt` (a fifth value, `review_last_launch_at_ms`), `platform/review/ReviewPrompts.kt`, `ReviewLogText.kt` and `ReviewNotes.kt` (the Log's lines; `SaidAtHome`).
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **Two stored times, because two things hold a moment back for different lengths.** A launch holds every moment for 30 days. A request Google Play gave no flow for (no Play Store, an error, no answer in ten seconds) holds the rest of that day, as before, and must not begin 30 days of silence: nothing was shown. One time for both could not tell them apart after a failed request that followed a launch. So `review_last_attempt_at_ms` is still "the last time MilO went to Google Play", and `review_last_launch_at_ms` is new.
  - **30 times 24 hours, not 30 days of the calendar.** No zone comes into it, and the hour that a change of the clocks adds or takes does not count. A test holds it over 1 November 2026, when Edmonton's clocks go back.
  - **Nothing is stored while a moment waits.** A number of trips that is reached inside the 30 days is simply still reached, and not asked for, at every look after them. So "stays due" needed no new state, and both numbers reached inside the 30 days are asked for once, together, as a MilO updated with 150 trips always was.
  - **The report's moment still passes when it is held,** as it did for the day: it is a moment on a screen, kept in memory.
  - **The order of the reasons.** Not from Google Play, then a drive, then the 30 days, then the day. So over Home a drive still holds a moment back without a word, also inside the 30 days.
- **The Log's lines, made true.** The line of a launch now ends "MilO does not ask for this again, and asks for nothing else for 30 days". A moment that waits has a line of its own: "…it asked Google Play to show the card less than 30 days ago, and leaves 30 days between two requests, because Google Play does not show its card more often. Nothing is used up. MilO asks the next time Home is open, with no trip being recorded and no vehicle connected: in 16 days at the earliest". The line "once already today … on another day" is left for the day of a failed request, which is the one case it is still true of.
- **Tests.** 2,961 pass in the gate on this Mac, fifteen more than before. `ReviewRulesTest` has 32 where it had 23: the 30 days to the millisecond; the day before and the day after; both numbers of trips reached inside them; a hundred inside the five's; a report inside them; the days left; the change of the clocks; a launch that lies ahead of now; a failed request beside a launch; a drive inside them. `ReviewPromptsTest` 28 where it had 25 (the wait and its line over Home and after "I sent it", once for each run and once more for a second launch; a launch the settings file refused still holds the 30 days). `ClockJumpReviewTest` 4 where it had 2. `ReviewStorageTest` 10 where it had 9. **Existing tests that changed,** because they said the old rule: in `ReviewRulesTest`, "never twice in one day, from midnight to midnight" and "the report's moment is held the same way on the day the trips were asked for" (both began with a launch, which now holds 30 days; they are the tests of the 30 days), and "the three moments in a row"; in `ReviewPromptsTest`, "a hundred trips are asked for when they come", "the same day's second moment is held" and "at Home the day's hold is said once a day", for the same reason, and the wording of the launch's line; in `ReviewViewModelTest`, one test's name and the words it looks for in the Log; in `ClockJumpReviewTest`, "a request made inside the trick is dated today, and tomorrow is free", which is now two tests, one for a launch and one for a failed request.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the 30 days**
He was not asked. It was listed as his to decide, and decided for him the same day:
1. **30 days,** the example in Google's documentation of "too often": an app that asks more than once within a month. Google publishes no limit and may change it.
2. **Counted from a launch, not from a try.** A request that Google Play answered with no flow showed nothing, and holds only its day.
3. **The Log says how many days are left,** counted up to whole days, so that a missing card can be explained from the Log alone.
4. **Over Home the line is written once for each run of MilO's process while a number of trips waits,** not once a day and not at every opening. What was said is kept in memory, as the day's line was (the `[DEBT]` of the review card, below, second point; it now covers up to 30 days, and the cost to fix is the same sixth stored value).
5. **Nothing was done for a phone that holds the first build's values.** That build was in an open pull request and in no release, and only a copy from Google Play launches at all. Such a copy would have no time of its last launch and would wait for nothing.

**`[CHANGE]` MilO stays upright when the phone is turned**
Phone only; the Android Auto screen is not touched. **Built, not yet proven on the phone.** Shawn: "can we please make the app allways portrait fixed. I dont like when i flip my phone and it goes landscape". One line in the manifest, `android:screenOrientation="portrait"` on MilO's one activity.
- **What is not obvious.** Lint fails the build on that line twice (`LockedOrientationActivity`, `DiscouragedApi`), and a lint warning is an error here. Both are answered on the activity itself with `tools:ignore` and a comment that says why, so the two checks stay on for anything added later.
- **It holds on a phone and not everywhere.** MilO targets Android 17. From Android 16 on, an app that targets it is not held to a fixed orientation on a screen 600 dp wide or more: on a tablet or an unfolded phone MilO turns as before. That is why no code that handles a turn was removed (the greeting ending at a turn, the forms keeping what is typed), and why the older device checks that turn the phone were left as they are, with a note.
- **A side effect:** the moonwalk's red glow in front of the lime tile (the `[DEBT]` of 2026-10-10) needed the phone on its side or a very narrow window. On a phone only the narrow window is left.
- **Not chosen: following the phone's own auto-rotate lock.** He has that switch and still asked; an app that is upright by itself is what he described.
- **Tests:** none added; there is nothing to test below a device. The gate passes. Device checks UP-1 to UP-4. UP-4 asks about a holder that carries the phone on its side, the one case where this choice costs something.

**`[CHANGE]` The wave has a new course: in at the left, a stop with the brake lights on, the wave, and on and off at the right**
Phone only. **Built, not yet proven on the phone.** Shawn, 2026-10-11: "can we redo the first animation of the one where he walks onto the screen and waves. i want to use the model with the lights on his butt. and also after he turns and waves for him to continue walking off the screen. can we have hime start from the left side of the screeen walking off the right side". He was shown two films of it over a picture of Home (the preview: commit ce7c1e6, and `design/mascot/README.md`, "The wave, walked through"): A, in which the brake lights go out as he lifts his foot for the first step off, and B, in which they stay lit until he is gone. He chose A and asked for no change. The app now plays what film A showed, in place of the wave it had: he walks in from the left, stops in the middle with his brake lights coming on behind him, hops round to face the viewer and waves, hops round to the right with the lights lit, and walks on and off at the right edge as they go out. No fade, no sound. It takes turns with the moonwalk as the wave before it did. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, The greeting; ADR-007, the addendum of 2026-10-11; device checks WW-1 to WW-10.
- **New:** two pictures in `app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/`: `mascot_walk_in_and_off.webp` (184 KB) and `mascot_stop_and_wave.webp` (942 KB). **Gone:** `mascot_walk.webp` and `mascot_turn_and_wave.webp`. Nothing in the app draws them any more and nothing of them is left in it. **Changed:** `MascotWalk.kt` (the wave's numbers; `MascotWalk`, now two walks, written again), `MascotGreetingKind.kt` (`SteppedLeg`, which every leg of both greetings is made of; a way across always has a leg out), `MascotGreeting.kt` (the fade is gone), `Mascot.kt` (the wave's two files), `MascotMoonwalk.kt` (below). Outside the app: `pack_wave2.py` packs the app's two pictures, where it packed a trial; `pack_greeting.py` packs nothing by itself any more and is what the other packing scripts share.
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **The wave is the moonwalk's course the other way round.** The walk in ends on its cycle's last frame, so it begins partway into a cycle with him just beyond the left edge; the walk off begins on its cycle's first frame, after the middle part's last frame has had its 42 ms. One thing is new: the two legs show different frames, because he is seen from behind as he leaves. The sheet holds both cycles, and the leg off asks for its frames 12 to 23.
  - **The moonwalk's code changed in three lines and a comment.** Its two legs were built with a small class of its own, `Leg`; the wave's legs need the same, so it stands in `MascotGreetingKind.kt` now as `SteppedLeg`, word for word. No assertion about the moonwalk's glides was touched, and all of them pass.
  - **Nothing in `feature/greeting/`, `MiloApp` or the settings changed.** The turn, the count, what an update and a new install get, a start that never drew him: all as they were. "The wave" is still the greeting an even count asks for; a phone that had the old wave gets the new one when the wave's turn comes.
  - **No greeting fades any more.** The fade was the old wave's way of leaving; with it went the one case in which a greeting had no leg out. A greeting is over when the leg out's last frame has had its time: the frame after it would lie wholly beyond the edge.
- **What it weighs.** The greetings' four pictures are 2,023 KB where they were 1,778 KB: the wave's two are 1,125 KB where they were 880 KB. The release APK, built unsigned the same way from this branch's base (d4e6f3f, to which ce7c1e6 added files under `design/` only) and from this change, is 15,791,643 bytes where it was 15,546,883: 245 KB more, 1.6 %. Both new pictures are in it byte for byte, as `res/w9.webp` and `res/JT.webp`; the two old ones are not.
- **Tests.** 2,853 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,848 before; five more). `MascotWalkTest` has 20 where it had 15: the ten tests of the old walk are fifteen of the two new ones (where he starts, the last frame of the walk in, each walk's half of the sheet, when he is gone, the clock, the lengths at the four widths, and the stride held against where `render_wave2.py` found four of the shoes' edges on the floor); its five tests of a greeting's course are untouched. `MascotPicturesTest` has 18 where it had 17: the wave's five are six, for the two new files, and its limits are new (the `[DECISION]` below). **Existing tests changed beyond the wave's own:** `MascotMoonwalkTest` has 16 where it had 17. Its three tests of "the two greetings side by side" are two: one asked the old walk for its poses, which `MascotWalkTest` now does for the new one, and the two others say what the wave is now (a sheet of 24 frames, a leg out, from the left to the right). One line was added to them about the moonwalk, that its sheet has 14 frames. `GreetingViewModelTest`: a picture's name in a message that is passed through.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, the debug builds of this branch's base and of this change, started for this on its own port and shut down; the phone not touched). Seen: the new wave from first to last, laid beside film A at every quarter second; the base build installed, first start, its wave and its moonwalk; this build installed over it, and then the new wave, the moonwalk, the wave, the moonwalk over four starts from nothing; a new install of this build, whose first start showed no greeting, whose next showed the new wave and whose third the moonwalk; Start trip pressed while he stood in the middle, and the trip started while he waved on; Home left by the bar's Trips button while he walked in, while he waved and while he walked off; MilO put away while he waved; Back pressed while he waved, and the moonwalk when MilO was opened again; nothing with the animations off, twice, and the wave once they were on again (after the `[FIX]` below); the wave and the moonwalk at 274, 393 and 610 dp wide and on its side; the moonwalk of the base build and of this one side by side, in sight for 163 frames in both; the party on demand in both builds side by side, on the screen for 121 and 120 frames. MilO stayed open throughout but for the fault of the `[FIX]`, and Android's crash log held nothing else. The measurements are the `[FINDING]` below.
- **Not run:** on the phone. And the release build with this change was built and weighed, not run: it comes out unsigned without the key, and nothing here reaches code by its name. WW-10 is its first run.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the wave, walked through**
He asked for the wave again with the brake lights, in at the left and off at the right, and chose film A. These points he was not asked:
1. **It takes the old wave's place and its turn.** MilO still has two greetings. He said "redo the first animation", not "add a third"; and a third would have been a third megabyte.
2. **Both walks are on one sheet of 24 frames,** not on a sheet each as the preview's trial packed them. One sheet is one picture to read, to hold and to step through, which is what the code that plays a greeting knows; two would have needed a third picture in every place that knows two. The weight is the same to 230 bytes (183,610 against 101,680 and 82,160). The memory is the same too if both sheets are held together: 22.8 MB of graphics memory for the seven seconds, where a greeting took about 12 (the `[DEBT]` below).
3. **It is packed as the moonwalk is: colour at 50 of 100,** where the old wave had 60. Laid beside the film at a phone's size, the packed frames at 60, 50 and 45 could not be told from the film or from one another. Enlarged three and five times, 50 is as 60; at 45 the small numbers on the dials begin to go soft, and at 40 they smear, as the moonwalk's did. So he is as sharp as the mascot he takes turns with, and 70 KB lighter than at 60. Every other setting is `pack_moonwalk.py`'s own, and the frames are packed by its functions.
4. **The limits in `MascotPicturesTest`:** the wave's two pictures under 1.2 MB, where each greeting had one megabyte; all four under 2.2 MB, where they had two. The moonwalk's megabyte and the party's 2.4 MB are as they were. The wave is 104 frames where it was 72, and nothing honest brings it under a megabyte (the `[FINDING]` below).
5. **No fade.** Film A has none: he walks off. The greeting is over when the last of him has passed the right edge.
6. **He is seen at once,** a hand first: the walk in begins partway into a cycle, just beyond the left edge, as in the film. The old wave began up to a cycle further out.
7. **A frame is shown for 42 ms,** where the film has 24 frames a second, 41.7 ms: the moonwalk's decision 7, for the same reason. By the end the app is a frame behind the film.
8. **The names** `mascot_walk_in_and_off.webp` and `mascot_stop_and_wave.webp`. New names, because the files hold other pictures than `mascot_walk.webp` and `mascot_turn_and_wave.webp` did, and this log speaks of both.
9. **The scripts keep the preview's names** (`render_wave2.py`, `pack_wave2.py`, `preview_wave2.py`, `check_wave2.py`). `render_greeting.py` stays as it is, because every script that draws a picture reads its camera, its stage and its halo from it; run by itself it still draws the old wave's frames, which nothing packs for the app.
10. **The line in the list of changes** is "improved", under 0.3.1. The earlier lines were left as they are: 0.3.0's says what 0.3.0 did, and the moonwalk's says nothing of which way the wave goes.
11. **Film B's frames are still drawn** by `render_wave2.py` and are not packed. The clips say A.

**`[FINDING]` What the new wave weighs, and what was tried**
The wave is 104 frames: 24 on the sheet and 80 in the middle part. The one before it was 72, 12 and 60.
- **Colour,** both pictures together: at 60 of 100, as the old wave, 1,195 KB; at 50, as committed, 1,125 KB; at 45, 1,095 KB; at 40, 1,052 KB. Colour is not where the weight is.
- **Other settings, tried on six frames of the middle part:** noise shaping at 80 where it is 50, 4 % less, and the flat green is drawn differently; without the sharper colour edges (`-sharp_yuv`), 4 % less, and the green bleeds into the white gloves, which is why it is there; the see-through channel at 40 or 30 where it is 50, 2 and 5 % less, with coarser edges; more passes, another filter for the see-through channel, fewer segments: nothing, or 1 %. None was taken.
- **Nothing in the middle part could be stored as a patch,** and one frame as "the frame before": he bobs with every wave, so every frame is the whole of him. It holds 79 pictures for 80 frames' time, at 11.9 KB a picture. (The moonwalk's are 9.8 KB: he is seen from the front for 49 of these 80 frames, with both dials, the M and both gloves in view, where there is most to draw.)
- **Ten frames have a glow** and keep their see-through channel almost whole: the turn away, with his lit brake lights to the viewer. As he stops the lights come on behind him, and no frame there was taken for one with a glow.
- **The two walks' sheet** is 184 KB, 7.7 KB a frame.
- **`pack_wave2.py` reads its own pictures back** and holds each frame against the drawn one over the dark page: 1.04 of 255 a pixel on average at worst on the sheet, 1.27 in the middle part. Run twice, it writes the same bytes.
- **Fewer pixels were not tried,** for the moonwalk's reason: he would be softer than the mascot he takes turns with.

**`[FINDING]` The wave on the emulator, measured**
Two small scripts, kept out of the repository, found in every recorded frame which of the 104 packed pictures the app was showing and where, by laying each over the frame's green; and, in the middle part, where the wave is the same poses several times over, how near the frame was to each of the 80 in all its colours. Seven recordings of the wave: three at 411 dp wide, one each at 274, 393 and 610, and one on its side (914 dp). The same scripts read film A.
- **Every frame in its place.** In both walks no frame showed the step of another place, and every frame stood within 0.1 dp of where its step belongs, across and up and down.
- **The shoes.** `render_wave2.py` says where it drew the heel's edge and the toe's edge of each shoe in every frame in which that edge is on the floor. Laid on the recorded frames, such an edge strayed at most 0.45 dp from left to right while it stood, and 0.15 dp up and down. In film A, read the same way, 0.43 and 0.10; in the frames as Blender draws them, 0.40. What is left is the view (README, "The wave, walked through").
- **The seams.** The walk in's last frame, one step short of the middle, was followed by the middle part's first 32 to 51 ms later; the edges that were on the floor in both moved at most 0.06 dp. The middle part's last frame was followed by the walk off's first, in the same place, 65 to 69 ms later: its 42 ms and up to one drawing of the screen. The edges moved at most 0.03 dp. No other picture came between, in any recording.
- **The middle part.** In every recording each recorded frame was the frame that was due at its moment, or the one before or after it; all 80 were on the screen when they were due, in their order, in 3.30 seconds, which is its length. **Its first frame was on the screen for 13 to 17 ms, where a frame has 42:** Android shows an animated picture's second frame as soon as it is ready, as the party found. That frame is the walk's mid-step, so the raised foot comes down a fortieth of a second sooner than in the film. The moonwalk's middle part begins the same way and was left so on 2026-10-10. To give it its time the app would show that frame itself and start the picture half a frame's time before it is up, as `MascotParty` does; that is a change to the course both greetings share, and was not made for a fortieth of a second nobody saw.
- **The lengths.** By the rules 151 frames at 274 dp (6.3 s), 171 at 393 (7.2 s), 173 at 411 (7.3 s), 207 at 610 (8.7 s) and 259 on its side (10.9 s). His green was in sight for 6.67 and 6.68 seconds at 411 dp, where it is 6.58 in the film.
- **Against film A, at 411 dp, where the emulator's screen is the film's size:** a frame from every quarter second of each, the moment taken from the walk in's own frames. The same picture in both from the first quarter second in which half of him is in sight until 4.5 seconds, each within 0.15 dp of the other's place (the film stands half a pixel off the app's arithmetic: it was drawn by itself at its own number of pixels). From 4.75 seconds on the app is one frame behind: a frame is 42 ms where the film's is 41.7, and the seam into the walk off waits for a drawing of the screen. He is a little softer than in the film, which was drawn at the screen's own 2.625 pixels to the dp.
- **The moonwalk and the party, as they were.** The moonwalk of this branch's base build and of this build, a frame from every quarter second, side by side: the same poses in the same places, in sight for 163 frames in both. The party on demand, the same: on the screen for 121 frames in the base build and 120 in this one.
- **How it ran there.** 23.7 to 24.0 recorded frames a second where 23.8 is every frame; this emulator draws with the Mac's graphics chip. What a phone's screen does with it is WW-2.
- **A trap in the measuring.** The recorder smears a frame in which the waving hand is at its fastest, and such a frame is then nearer to a neighbour's picture than to its own by a third. Asked "which picture is this", two frames in a recording were named wrongly; asked "is it the one that was due", none.

**`[DEBT]` The wave's halo is right on a dark ground only, as the moonwalk's**
The same fault, for the same reason, as the entry of 2026-10-10 below: the halo round the lit brake lights is stored in the pictures as red that covers a part of what lies behind. **Seen on the emulator on 2026-10-11:** held upright at 393, 411 and 610 dp wide he stands in front of the dark trips tile and the dark page, and it is the glow of the film. **On its side, and at 274 dp wide, he stands in front of the lime Start trip tile, and the glow is an orange mist that darkens the lime.** In the wave that lasts half a second: ten frames after he has turned away, and two as the lights go out. As he stops the lights are behind him and nothing of the glow is seen. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `MascotWalk.kt`; device check WW-9. **Cost to fix:** that entry's, once for both greetings: the glow drawn by the app as light added to the screen, or left out where the ground is not dark, or the greeting kept to a phone held upright. `render_wave2.py --plain` already draws the frames without the halo.

**`[DEBT]` The wave's sheet takes 22.8 MB of graphics memory, where a greeting's took about 12**
The sheet holds both walks, 24 frames of 540 by 440 pixels, and is held from before he is first drawn until he is gone: seven seconds on a phone. The old wave's sheet took 12.1 MB and the moonwalk's takes 12.9. It was taken for the simpler code (the `[DECISION]` above, point 2). Not seen to matter on the emulator; what it does on the phone's Android 14 is part of WW-1. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `MascotWalk.kt`. **Cost to fix:** half a day. Either two sheets, the walk off's read while the middle part plays and the walk in's let go then, which is a second reading in the middle of a greeting and needs an answer for a picture that cannot be read once he is on the screen. Or the walks' frames cut down to what a walking mascot needs, about two thirds of a frame, with the place of that cut in the full frame told to the drawing: a third less memory and one more pair of numbers that no test can check.

**`[FIX]` MilO no longer stops at a start from nothing while the phone's animations are off**
Phone only. Found on 2026-10-11 by the check of the new wave below ("nothing with the phone's animations off"), on an emulator. With "Animator duration scale" off, or "Remove animations", MilO showed its opening mark and stopped; opened again it stopped again, and Android said "MilO keeps stopping". **It was in `main` from the merge of the one-year party (d4e6f3f), and in no released version.** The build of that commit stopped the same way on the same emulator. The builds before the party did not (2026-10-10, below: "nothing with animations off").
- **Why.** `PartyWatch` follows the phone's animation setting, to tell the party whether Home can show it: `snapshotFlow { scale?.scaleFactor != 0f }`. Compose begins to follow that setting the first time the factor is asked for, and writes down what it finds, in a state of its own. Inside `snapshotFlow` nothing may be written: "Cannot modify a state object in a read-only snapshot". While the animations are on, what it finds is what was there, 1, and nothing is written. While they are off there is a 0 to write. At a start from nothing the party's watch is the first to ask.
- **The fix** is one statement in `PartyObjects.kt`: the factor is asked for once before it is followed. Nothing else of the party changes, and nothing of it is drawn or timed differently: its showing on demand was recorded with the build before and with this one and laid side by side.
- **Why it was not seen.** The party's own check of "animations off" switched them off while MilO was running, when the factor had long been asked for. The greeting's check was run before the party was built. And no unit test can hold it: it needs Android's own setting and Compose's window, and the project has no Robolectric (STANDARDS section 11).
- **What it would have cost.** Whoever has the phone's animations off could not have opened MilO at all. The process that stops is the one the trip service runs in.
- **Seen with the fix,** on the emulator: two starts from nothing with the animations off, Home drawn each time, no mascot, the count of greetings as it was, MilO still running and nothing in Android's crash log; with them on again, the wave that was due. Device checks MG-7 and MW-9 ask for exactly this and have not been run on the phone.
- **Not looked for:** other things that write as they are first asked for. There is one more `snapshotFlow` in the app, in `CameToFrontEffect`, which reads the window's focus; MilO opened and came back to the front through it many times in this work, with the animations on and off.

**`[CHANGE]` MilO asks Google Play for its review card at three moments**
Phone only. **Built, not yet proven on the phone, and the card itself has been seen nowhere.** Shawn: "also i would like three google play prompts for a review. one after 5 trips recoreded, 100 trips recorded and after submitting a report". Asked which way of recording a report as sent he meant, he chose the answer "I sent it" after the email app, not "Mark as sent". MilO asks Google Play, through Google's own library, to show its review card; Google Play decides whether it appears and tells MilO nothing. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Google Play's review card; ADR-009; device checks RV-1 to RV-9.
- **New:** `core/review/ReviewRules.kt` (the three moments and every rule, pure); `data/settings/ReviewStorage.kt` (four values in the settings file); `platform/review/` (`ReviewPrompts.kt`, the one object; `ReviewCard.kt`, the interface between MilO and the card; `PlayReviewCard.kt`, the only file that names Google's library; `ReviewLogText.kt`, the Log's lines; `ReviewNotes.kt`, the log writing and the catching of failures); `feature/review/ReviewViewModel.kt`; `app/ReviewObjects.kt`. One library, `com.google.android.play:review` 2.0.2, in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` and `app/build.gradle.kts`. `docs/adr/ADR-009-play-review-card.md`.
- **Changed:** `MiloApp` calls `ReviewWatch` and says when Home is free; `AppContainer` has `container.review`; `ReportRecords` is handed `onSentByAnswer` and `MiloReportEntry` passes it; `MiloSettings.reviews` with its line in `SettingsStore`; `TripDao.observeRecordedCount`, `TripRepository.observeRecordedTripCount` and `isRecordedTrip` in `TripTotals.kt`. One sentence on `website/privacy.html`, one line under 0.3.1 in the list of changes, and `website/changes.html` written again.
- **Nothing under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` or `platform/bluetooth/` was changed,** and nothing in the Report screen or its ViewModel. The review card reads two things of the trips: how many recorded trips are stored (one `COUNT` query), and what the trip controller publishes for every screen (`TripController.activity`). It writes its own four values in the settings file and its lines in the Log.
- **What is not obvious from the code.**
  - **Why `ReportRecords` tells, and not `ReportViewModel`.** The ViewModel is where "I sent it" and "Mark as sent" part ways, and it cannot be built in a unit test: three of the things it is handed need an Android `Context`. The difference between the two answers is what Shawn chose, so it had to be held by a test. `ReportRecords` runs in unit tests already; it is handed one more plain function, with nothing to tell out of the box, so every existing test builds it as before.
  - **Why a moment has a third standing, `said`.** A copy that Google Play did not install asks nothing and must use nothing up, and the Log has to say why. Without remembering that it has said so, a sideloaded MilO with 5 trips would write that line at every opening of Home, for good: Shawn's own phone runs such a copy. `said` is not `asked`: a copy from Google Play asks for it.
  - **The flow is launched first and remembered second,** in a block that cannot be cancelled, and what the settings file refuses to take is remembered in memory. Either way round, a file that cannot be written must not have MilO go to Google Play at every opening of Home.
  - **The task of the launch is not waited for.** It completes when Google Play's screen has closed, and says neither whether the card was shown nor whether a review was written. MilO has nothing to carry on with afterwards.
  - **A second before anything is asked** (`SETTLE_MS`). What's new opens a moment after MilO does, the party a tenth of a second after a trip's start, and "Did you send it?" needs a moment to close. Without it the card could be asked for in the frame before one of them.
  - **The ViewModel watches the trips only while a screen is fit for the card,** so a MilO in the background, where the recording happens, reads nothing for this.
  - **A flow says whether it was launched** (`ReviewFlow.Given.launch` answers false when the activity it was asked for is gone): the phone can be turned while the Play Store answers, and starting Google Play's screen from an activity that no longer exists is not something to find out about on a phone.
  - **The lines are `PROCESS` lines, not a category of their own.** A new category cannot be read by an older build, and would have to be placed under one of the Log's chips. Each line begins "Review card:".
- **What it weighs.** The release APK, built unsigned the same way before and after, is 15,613,142 bytes where it was 15,546,883: **66,259 bytes more, 0.4 %.** R8 keeps 24 classes of the library. The merged manifest gains one entry, the library's own see-through activity, and no permission: 14 before, 14 after, none of them INTERNET. The rules R8 runs with are line for line what they were.
- **Tests.** 2,941 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,848 before; 93 new): `ReviewRulesTest` (23), `ReviewPromptsTest` (25), `ReviewViewModelTest` (16), `ReviewBesideTripsTest` (3), `PlayReviewCardTest` (2), `ClockJumpReviewTest` (2), `ReviewStorageTest` (9), `RecordedTripsTest` (6), `ReportSentByAnswerTest` (7). **One existing test file changed:** `FakeTripDao` answers the new query. No assertion of an existing test changed, and every test of the trip controller, the report and the clock passes as it stood.
- **That trips are recorded as before is held by a test, not only argued.** `ReviewBesideTripsTest` records six trips through the real trip controller three times: with nothing watching, with the review card looking on in a copy that asks, and in a copy that does not. The trip rows, the GPS points, every request for the trip service and every line of the trips' log are the same in all three, and Google Play is asked nothing while a trip is being recorded.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16 with the Play Store app, started for this on its own port, read-only, and shut down; the phone not touched). The entry "The review card on the emulator" below says what was seen.
- **Not run:** on the phone. And **no card was seen:** Google Play shows none for an app that is not in the account's Play library. That needs a copy from Google Play's internal test track (RV-1 to RV-9).

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the review card**
He asked for the three moments and chose "I sent it". Everything else was decided for him. Most of it was set for this work before it began; where the work changed a point or added to it, it says so.
1. **Each moment once, ever,** remembered in the settings file. Not in an export file, and an import leaves it as the phone has it; Android's backup carries it. That is how the neighbouring values behave (`greetings_shown`, `whats_new_seen_version`).
2. **Which trips count was chosen in the work:** the trips MilO recorded itself that count on the Trips screen (finished; not being recorded, discarded or deleted). Business and Personal both count. **A trip typed in by hand does not:** it is a trip MilO missed, and five of those are no moment to ask for stars. The one-year party draws the same line.
3. **Over Home, and after everything else:** not during the first start's page or with Setup or any other screen on top, and not until the opening greeting, the one-year party and What's new are over. The report's moment on the Report screen.
4. **Changed from what was set: a connected vehicle holds the card back, as a trip being recorded does.** Set: "with no trip being recorded". But the parked rule ends a trip while he sits in the truck with the phone on its mount and Home open, and the fifth trip ending there would put the card in front of a driver about to leave. So: no trip, no paired vehicle connected, no wait beside a parked truck, and not before MilO knows (`mayBeDriving`). After End is pressed in the truck the card waits as well.
5. **Added: the same rule holds the report's moment, and there the moment passes.** Over Home the card waits for the drive to be over. After "I sent it" there is nothing to wait on: the moment is that answer. It comes again with the next report answered "I sent it". **Whoever always sends the report from the truck, with the phone connected and a trip being recorded, never has this moment:** the Log says so each time, and it is the one thing here Shawn may want otherwise (the other way is to keep the moment for the next time Home is free).
6. **Never twice in one day,** by MilO's own clock, whatever came of the first time. Updated with 20 trips: asked once. With 150: asked once, both numbers counted.
7. **Each occasion looks at its own moments.** After "I sent it" only the report's moment is asked for, also when a number of trips is waiting; that one waits for Home, on another day.
8. **Only a copy that Google Play installed asks.** Added: the Log says so once for each moment such a copy reaches, and that it said so is remembered without the moment being used up.
9. **Launched counts as asked.** Added: Google Play is given 10 seconds to answer; a flow that arrives after the screen was left, or after a drive began, is not launched and nothing is stored; a request with no flow holds the day like a launch, so a phone whose Play Store is broken is asked once a day and no more.
10. **A last time that lies ahead of now is not believed,** as ADR-006 decided for every such value.
11. **One second on the screen first** (above).
12. **Phone only, and no switch in Settings.** I do not think one is needed: it happens three times in the life of an installation, Google Play has its own limit on top, and a switch would be one more thing on a long screen. If Google's review or a user asks for one, it is one stored value and one condition.
13. **Changed from what was set: the library is `review`, not `review-ktx`** (the `[FINDING]` below).
14. **No fake of Google Play in any build.** The work was allowed Google's `FakeReviewManager` in the debug build. It was not needed: the real library runs on the emulator (the second `[FINDING]` below), and shows more than the fake would.
15. **The one place for something to be played before the card** is `CelebrationThenCard` in `app/ReviewObjects.kt`. A mascot animation is planned for the two numbers of trips. It gets the moment before Google Play is asked and says when it is over. It must ask nothing and promise nothing about a review: Google forbids both before the card.
16. **The words.** The Log's lines begin "Review card:". The list of changes says "Google Play may ask for a review", and the privacy page that Google Play learns that MilO asked.

**`[FINDING]` Google's Kotlin extensions for the review library stop the release build, and only the release build**
The work was set to use `com.google.android.play:review-ktx` 2.0.2. With it, the debug build, Lint and all the unit tests passed, and the release build failed in R8: "Missing class com.google.android.gms.common.annotation.NoNullnessRewrite (referenced from: … ReviewManagerKtxKt$sam$com_google_android_gms_tasks_OnSuccessListener$0.onSuccess)".
- **What it is.** One class inside `review-ktx` carries an annotation whose class is in none of the five versions of Play services' basement library in this Mac's Gradle cache (18.3.0 to 18.10.0), the one `review-ktx` was built against included. The library `review` itself and `core-common` do not name it.
- **Why CI would not have seen it.** CI builds and tests the debug build, which does not go through R8 (ADR-008). This is the first library added since R8 was switched on, and it did exactly what that ADR warns of.
- **What was done.** The extensions were left out. What MilO would have used of them is one function that waits for a task of the library's; it is fifteen lines in `PlayReviewCard.kt` (`Task.answer`). The other way was the rule R8 itself proposes, `-dontwarn` for a class nobody has; `app/proguard-rules.pro` says not to add such a rule for something that can be done without.
- **What to take from it.** A new library is built in the release variant before anything is written against it: `./gradlew assembleRelease -Pmilo.keystore.file=/nonexistent/milo.jks -Pmilo.signing.debugKey=true`. The gate does not do this.

**`[FINDING]` The real review library runs on an emulator, if the Play Store is named as the installer**
MilO asks only in a copy that Google Play installed, and a build from the Mac is not one. But Android records as the installer whatever `adb` is told: `adb install -i com.android.vending app-debug.apk` on an emulator whose image has the Play Store, and `PackageManager` answers "com.android.vending" for MilO. An install without `-i` takes the name off again. No test code is needed in the app for it.
- **What Google Play did there.** Every time MilO asked, logcat had the library's own lines: "ReviewService : requestInAppReview (com.shawnkowalchuk.milo)", the binding to `com.android.vending/com.google.android.finsky.inappreviewservice.InAppReviewService`, and "onGetLaunchReviewFlowInfo" between 20 ms and half a second later. So the Play Store gave a flow each time. MilO launched it, and **nothing appeared:** MilO's own screen stayed in front. The emulator's Play Store is signed in to no account and MilO is in nobody's library; Google's testing page says the card needs both.
- **So the emulator shows all of MilO's side with the real library, and nothing of Google's.** That is more than Google's `FakeReviewManager` gives, which shows no card either.
- **Three things about that emulator, for whoever does it next.**
  - Its data partition ran short of room on the first start (about 370 MB free of 6 GB; on a later start of the same read-only image, 1 GB): a second install of the 54 MB debug build then failed with `INSTALL_FAILED_INSUFFICIENT_STORAGE`. `pm uninstall -k` first, which keeps MilO's data, made room; on that first start one large unrelated app also had to be removed from the throwaway copy of the image.
  - Gmail there has no account. "Email the report" opens Gmail's welcome page inside MilO's task, and Back from it leaves MilO for the launcher. Opening MilO again with `am start … --activity-clear-top --activity-single-top` closes that page, and "Did you send it?" is asked.
  - A release build cannot be read with `run-as`. The trips were put in with the debug build and the release build installed over it, both signed with Android's throwaway debug key.

**`[FINDING]` The review card on the emulator**
Android 16, 1080 by 2400, the debug build unless said otherwise; the trips put into the database with the app stopped (a debug build allows that), all else through the app's own screens. Each round started from cleared data and the first start gone through. Run twice, the second time with the build of this commit. Stills, films and what was stored are in `design-shots/review` in the session's scratch folder, not in the repository.
- **A build put on with a cable, 5 recorded trips, Home opened three times:** nothing asked of the Play Store. One line in the Log, at the first opening: "…MilO asked Google Play for nothing: this copy of MilO was not installed by Google Play… This is written once". In the settings file `review_five_trips` is `said`, and no time of a last try. The same at the report's moment, after "I sent it".
- **The same data, the build installed again with the Play Store as its installer, and a trip started during the greeting:** Home was in front for 25 seconds with the trip being recorded. Nothing asked, no line. **One second after End trip** the library asked the Play Store, and the Log had "…MilO asked Google Play to show its review card. Google Play decides whether the card appears, and MilO is not told. MilO does not ask for this again". `review_five_trips` is `asked`. So the moment that had only been talked of was not used up.
- **The same day, a report handed to the email app and answered "I sent it":** nothing asked. "…it went to Google Play for the card once already today, and never does twice in one day. Nothing is used up. MilO asks after the next report that is recorded as sent with "I sent it"". `review_report_sent` is not stored.
- **4 recorded trips and 2 typed in by hand:** Home open for 14 seconds, nothing asked, no line. **With a fifth recorded trip:** the mascot greeted, and a second after he had gone the library asked the Play Store. Then "Mark as sent", confirmed: nothing asked, no line.
- **2 trips, "I sent it" with nothing asked yet that day:** the library asked the Play Store one second after the report was recorded. Then 150 trips the same day, Home opened three times in one run of MilO: nothing asked, and one line, "…asks at 5 and at 100. MilO asked Google Play for nothing: it went to Google Play for the card once already today…".
- **150 trips and nothing asked before:** one request, one line ("…asks at 5 and at 100. MilO asked Google Play to show its review card, once for all of them…"), and both numbers `asked`. Opened again: nothing.
- **The minified release build,** with 5 recorded trips and the Play Store as its installer: the same request in logcat, the same line on the Log screen, no crash. That is the build R8 rewrites, and the library is new in it.
- **Not seen:** the card. And nothing with a truck: "a vehicle is connected" was held by unit tests only, the emulator has no Bluetooth device to connect.

**`[FINDING]` What Google's pages say that bears on what was decided**
Read on 2026-10-11: developer.android.com, "Google Play In-App Reviews API", with its pages for Kotlin and for testing.
- **Google Play's own limit is longer than a day.** Its page says that an app which launches the flow more than once in a short time may find that nothing is shown, and gives a month as the example. MilO's three moments can fall well within a month: 5 trips in the first week, 100 in the third, the first report on the 1st. Each is launched and counts as asked; Google Play will probably show one card. "Never twice in a day" was set for Shawn and is kept; the `[DEBT]` below says what a longer gap would take.
- **A sideloaded copy can show the card after all, in one case.** Google's testing page: once the account has installed the app from Google Play, later builds can be put on that phone from the developer's own computer, and the card still appears. Android then no longer names the Play Store as the installer, and MilO asks nothing. So a phone that takes MilO from Google Play and later an APK from GitHub over it stops asking. The other way round (decision 8) every sideloaded copy would spend its three moments on a card that cannot appear.
- **No question before the card.** Google's rule is that the app asks the person nothing before the card or with it; its examples are questions about the app itself (does the person like it, would they give it five stars). MilO's "Did you send it?" comes before the report's card. It is about the email and was there before the card; MilO asks nothing about itself and asks nobody a second time because of the card. It is said here because a reviewer at Google could read the rule more widely than its examples.
- **The data safety form.** Google's page lists what the library collects (the rating and the text a person enters, for a public review) and says the publisher answers the form. README, "Publishing on Google Play", step 3, has a line for it: the form is to be looked at again before 0.3.1 is published.
- **The library is two years old and under Google's terms.** 2.0.2, the newest, is of 2024-10-18. Its licence is the "Play Core Software Development Kit Terms of Service", not an open-source one.

**`[DEBT]` What is left rough in the review card**
- **Two moments within Google Play's limit are both spent.** What: MilO's gap between two requests is a day; Google Play's is not published, and its own example is a month. Why: "never twice in a day" was the rule set, and one time of "the last try" serves both a launch and a failed request. Cost to fix later: a second stored time (the last launch), one more condition in `judgeReview`, tests; an hour. `TODO(debt)` at `attemptedToday` in `core/review/ReviewRules.kt`. Shawn's to decide (ADR-009, "Left open"). **Paid later the same day:** the entry "The review card's moments keep 30 days apart", above.
- **The line "once already today" can be written once for each run of MilO's process on such a day,** not once a day: which day it was last said for is kept in memory. Such a day comes at most twice in the life of an installation. `TODO(debt)` in `ReviewPrompts`.
- **`MiloApp` is further over its 200 lines:** it stood at 212 after the one-year party, and the review card adds eleven (what Home has to be free of is known only there). Its `TODO(debt)` says so. Splitting it is a change of its own, as the entry of 2026-10-10 says.
- **`ReviewPrompts.kt` is at its limit** (301 lines) after `ReviewNotes.kt` was split off it.
- **Nothing here has met the card.** How Google Play's light card sits over MilO's dark screens, and whether a trip that starts while it is up is recorded as ever (the trip service does not know of the card, so it should be), are RV-3 and RV-4.

### 2026-10-10

**`[CHANGE]` Home's odometer has no cell for the tenth: the last digit of the kilometres turns slowly on to the next**
Phone only. **Built, not yet proven on the phone.** Shawn, with the build of the three Home changes (below) on his phone that evening: "I like the slim tile but i think we have to adjust the odometer. as the 1/10 at the end looks like it is another zero on the odometer. can we have the last number turn slowly to the next number. can you give me some visuals." He was shown three moving drawings, each without a tenth: "Always creeping" (the last wheel turns the whole kilometre through), "Rolls over at the end" (it stands, then turns over the last 200 m) and "Bar fills, then it flips" (the digit stands above a small bar). He chose the first: "a for the odometer".
- **What changed.** The row is the digits of the whole kilometres and nothing after them. While a trip is counted the last of them is lime and stands between its digit and the next, as far on as the kilometre has been driven; a carry rolls the wheels before it over that same kilometre. At rest every cell is dark and alike.
- **What is not obvious from the code.** `DashboardWheels` needed no new parameter. It always drew "a figure in steps of its last wheel, and how far that wheel has turned"; Home now hands it whole kilometres and the part of a kilometre where it handed it tenths and the part of a tenth. What went is the gap before the last cell and the grey of a tenth at rest (a colour pair, `wheels.tenth`), and a figure may now have a single wheel (`wheelsOf` held it to two: a whole part and its decimal). `lastWheelTurn` is `pastItsTenth` now, because the wheel it was named for is gone: it still says how far a trip's exact distance is from the tenth it is printed as, and with `inTenths` that is the odometer to the metre.
- **The spring was not tuned again.** `ROLL_STIFFNESS` was chosen for a wheel that turned once in 100 m. The kilometre's wheel moves a tenth as fast, which is the easier case, and it was left alone. Device check HS-9 asks the truck.
- **Tests.** 2,671 pass in the gate on this Mac. `OdometerWheelsTest`: wheels that stand show Settings' number; counting, the last wheel is as far on as the kilometre has been driven; the digit changes at the whole kilometre and not where Settings' rounding does; the end of a trip; miles. `HomeOdometerTest`, `WheelPositionsTest` and `ColorContrastTest` were changed with it.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, 411 dp, started for this and shut down; the phone not touched). At rest in the real debug build: six dark cells, "102728" and "km", the same number as Settings' tile. Counting, in a throwaway copy of the build in which a trip started with the button moves the odometer (as on 2026-10-10 for the wheels' first version: an emulator has no truck, and such a trip moves none): a reading of 102,728, then 2.2 km at 50 km/h, recorded. In a frame every six seconds the lime digit stood where the line under the wheels said: the 8 a little out at "+0.1 km" and "+0.3 km", most of the way out with the 9 coming in at "+0.8 km", the 9 a third out at "+1.3 km", half of the 9 and half of the 0 at "+1.5 km" with the 2 before them half out and the 3 half in, and 102730 standing at "+2.0 km". The trip's own distance came unevenly in that recording (0.3 km in the first minute and a half, then half a kilometre between two frames, twice): that is the emulator's positions, as before, and the wheel followed each time. Pictures: `design-shots/odo-creep` in the session's scratch folder, not in the repository.
- **Not run:** on the phone, in the truck. HS-7 to HS-15 (HS-7 to HS-13 written again, HS-15 new).

**`[DECISION]` At rest Home's wheels show Settings' number, and do not stay between two digits**
Decided for Shawn; he was shown the wheel only while it turned. A dashboard's wheel stays where it stopped. Home's does not, for two reasons. **One:** what he asked to be rid of was a thing at the end of the row that made him read the number twice, and a last digit cut in half on a parked truck's odometer is that again, for hours. **Two:** it ends the difference between Home and Settings that the first version had and wrote down ("one figure, written two ways", below): with nothing being counted, both now print `OdometerFigure.value`.
- **The price.** While a trip is counted the wheels show the figure before its rounding, so the row changes place at the start and at the end of every trip by up to half a digit, without rolling there (the wheels never roll back, and half of these moves would be backwards). At 123,460.6 km the trip ends on 123460 with its 0 most of the way out, and the row then stands at 123461.
- **Not chosen: counting from Settings' number,** that is, letting the last digit change where the rounding does, half a kilometre after each reading. The row would never move at a trip's start or end. But a reading he has just typed would show its last wheel half turned before the truck has moved, and the digit would change in the middle of each kilometre he drives after typing it, which nobody could explain to him.

**`[CHANGE]` The one-year party is in the app: a pop at every trip's start that day, and the mascot's party over Home**
Phone only. **Built, not yet proven on the phone.** Shawn: "a one year use animation. After the person has used the app for a year this one will come up that day every time he starts a trip. i want it to fade in with the mascot in the center with a birthday cap. i want hime to put his hand behind his back and pull out a party popper and pull it and shoot confeti above his head. i want sound effects for this"; and: "the aniversery animation is only tobe used on that day for starting trips". His choices: a year counts from the first recorded trip, every year on that date; "Sound always, animation in MilO"; phone only; one pop and no other sound, made by MilO itself. He was shown a film of it and approved it: the last of several, with one sound, a pop made by arithmetic. (The cap, the popper, the confetti, the clip `OneYear` and the scripts that drew the film came with the commit before this one: `design/mascot/README.md`, "The party".) The app now plays what that film showed. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, The one-year party; ADR-007, the party's addendum; device checks OY-1 to OY-14.
- **New:** two pictures in `app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/` (`mascot_party_popper.webp`, 885 KB, and `mascot_party_pop.webp`, 1,426 KB) and `design/mascot/scripts/pack_party.py`, which packs them; `app/src/main/res/raw/party_pop.wav` (88 KB) and `tools/make_party_pop.py`, which writes it; `core/party/` (the day, which trips count, who plays the pop, what Home owes: pure); `platform/party/OneYearParty.kt`; `platform/trip/TripStartSounds.kt`; `feature/party/` (`Party.kt`, `PartyViewModel.kt`); `MascotParty.kt`, `MascotPartyPicture.kt` and `LongHold.kt` in the design system; `data/settings/PartyStorage.kt` (`party_owed_since_ms`); `app/PartyObjects.kt`. **Changed:** `TripRecorder.record` says whether its sounds hold a trip's first (`TripServiceLink`, one line in `TripWorker`); `TripService.announce` plays through `playAtMoment`; `TripSoundPlayer` can play the pop; `Mascot.kt` reads and draws the party's pictures; `greeting()` stands back for a party; `WhatsNewScreen` has the two-second rest; `MiloApp` and `AppContainer` wire it; `TripDao.findStarts`.
- **What is not obvious from the code.** The trip rules, the state machine and the trip rows are untouched: the controller only adds one fact to the order it already gives the service, "these sounds are this trip's first", and everything of the party's happens after the trip is open, in the coroutine that plays the sounds. `playAsSet`, which chose the two sounds, is now `soundsToPlay`, a plain function; a test compares it with the old choice, written out in the test, for all 16 ways the two switches and the two chosen files can stand and all four lists of sounds the controller sends. The party is asked only for a trip's first sound, so a trip-start sound that comes later never reads the trips. The clip is two pictures because the app has to know the moment of the pop. What Home owes is in the settings file because the MilO that is opened in the evening is often not the process that recorded the morning's trip.
- **What it weighs.** The two pictures are 2,311 KB and the pop 88 KB. The release APK, built unsigned the same way before and after, is 15,546,859 bytes where it was 13,081,041: 2,466 KB more, 18.9 %, of which 70 KB are code. The aim was 2.5 MB for everything it adds. All three files are in it byte for byte, under the short names the release build gives them (`res/a1.webp`, `res/vz.webp`, `res/WP.wav`).
