- **Tests.** 2,844 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,705 before; 139 new): `PartyDayTest` (14), `PartyPopTest` (12), `OneYearPartyTest` (17), `TripStartSoundsTest` (17), `TripControllerFirstSoundTest` (7), `ClockJumpPartyTest` (5), `PartyTest` (15), `PartyViewModelTest` (22), `PartyStorageTest` (7), `MascotPartyTest` (15), six more in `MascotPicturesTest`, two more in `GreetingTest`. **Existing tests changed:** the two stand-ins for the trip service (`FakeService`, `ClockJumpService`) take the new argument of `record`, and `FakeService` keeps what it was told; `FakeTripDao` answers the new query; `GreetingTest`'s helper has one more parameter. **No assertion of an existing test changed,** and every test of the trip controller, of the sounds and of the clock passes as it stood.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, the debug build, started for this on its own port and shut down; the phone not touched), in two sessions. The second found a fault, which was corrected: the `[FINDING]` "Asked for before Setup's Done was pressed" below. The entry "The party on the emulator, measured" says what was seen.
- **Not run:** on the phone. And the release build with this change was built and weighed, not run: it comes out unsigned without the key. OY-9 is its first run.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the one-year party**
He asked for the party, and chose from what he was asked: from the first recorded trip, every year; sound always and the animation in MilO; phone only; one pop of MilO's own making. These points he was not asked. Most of them were set for this work before it began; where the work changed one or added to it, it says so.
1. **The day** is the day of the year the first recorded trip started on, in the zone the phone is in now, one year on or more; a first trip on 29 February is celebrated on 28 February in a year without one.
2. **Which trips count was changed from what was set.** Set: the earliest trip row of any kind that storage still holds. Built: the earliest trip **MilO recorded itself**, whatever became of it (finished, discarded, deleted, open), and **by the start MilO recorded.** A trip typed in by hand does not count, and a trip whose times he changed counts by its recorded start. Why: he can type in a missed trip for any date, also one from before he had MilO, and one slip of the year on the edit screen would otherwise bring a party on a day that means nothing.
3. **The pop takes the place of the trip's first sound:** the connect sound, and for a trip a parked truck's moving started, the trip-start sound. Everything else about the two sounds is as it was. Silent when both are switched off; heard when either is on, also when the one it replaces is the one that is off.
4. **The pop belongs to the popper when Home is in front.** Added to what was set, so that "sound always" holds: if the party has not begun two seconds after the trip's start, the trip service plays the pop after all; and if a party that began is cut short before its popper went off, the pop is played as it ends. Only one of them can.
5. **The party over Home:** it takes no press and dims nothing; once for the trips started that day, at the start if Home is in front, otherwise the next time it is, that day only; with the phone's animations off, the pop alone. Added: **switched on again that day, the party that is owed plays.** And he is as large as the film showed him where the screen has the room, and smaller where it has not.
6. **The party plays instead of the opening greeting,** which keeps its turn.
7. **The pictures:** the confetti drawn in, two animated pictures cut at the pop, he with fewer pixels than the film had. **He is a little softer than in the film.** That is the price of 2.3 MB, and the one thing here Shawn may want otherwise once he sees it on the phone (the `[DEBT]` below says what sharper costs).
8. **The pop** is the sound of the film he approved, sample for sample, at another level: its loudest sample at 55 % of full scale, which is the number the chirp and the chime are written with (`PEAK` in both scripts). In the film's own sound track its loudest sample is at 41 %. A script of its own, `tools/make_party_pop.py`, so that the two old sounds' script is not touched: run again, that one wrote the chirp and the chime byte for byte as they are committed (the same checksums before and after). The pop keeps the film's 44,100 samples a second where the two have 22,050: they are tones, and this is noise, and at half the rate it would be another sound than the one he chose. Two runs of the new script write the same file.
9. **A crack at that level is quieter to the ear than the chirp,** and it stands in for the chirp that day. By its loudest sample it is level with the two; by its average over the eighth of a second the crack lasts it is about 10 dB under the chirp, because a crack touches its peak for a thousandth of a second and a tone stays there. Not changed: the level was to follow the two sounds' rule, and a crack cannot be made as loud as a tone without clipping. If it is too quiet on the phone (OY-3), `PEAK` is one number, and 0.9 would add 4 dB.
10. **Where the party is asked for on demand was chosen:** on the What's new screen, a finger rested for two seconds on the label of the version on this phone. Not a long press on Settings' Version tile, which was the suggestion: that tile is a button, a slow press on it would have fired a pop at alarm volume where a list of changes was expected, and Android's long press is four tenths of a second. The label is not a button, and two seconds are not an accident. It plays over Home, where the party belongs, and its pop is heard also with both sounds off, as Settings' Play is.
11. **It stores nothing on demand,** and the greeting's turn is not touched by any party.
12. **The line in the list of changes** says what the party is and when, and does not say how to ask for it on demand.
13. **What Home owes is this phone's** (`party_owed_since_ms`): not in an export file; Android's backup carries it with the settings.
14. **Pictures that cannot be read** are one `ERROR` line, and nothing is tried again for that trip.
15. **The names.** `mascot_party_popper.webp` and `mascot_party_pop.webp`; `party_pop.wav`; in the Log "MilO's party: ..." at a trip's start on the day and "Party pop: playing the bundled pop".
16. **Home counts as drawn once the first start's page has been left with OK** (`homeIsDrawn`). Setup's Done button need not have been pressed, which the greeting asks for. Whoever leaves Setup by Back has an ordinary Home from then on, perhaps for good, and would otherwise never see the party, on the day or on demand. Changed after the emulator showed what the greeting's rule does to a party (the `[FINDING]` below).
17. **A party asked for on demand while the phone's animations are off is dropped** as MilO reaches Home. The day's party is kept for when they come back; one on demand was a wish to look now, and a pop some minutes later would be a fright.

**`[FINDING]` Android does not keep an animated picture's first frame for its time, and a started picture waits to be drawn**
Two things about `AnimatedImageDrawable` that the greetings never met, found by laying a recording of the party over the film, frame for frame.
- **The first frame.** The clip's first thirteen frames are one picture, on which he is faded in. The first build stored it once, to be shown for 546 ms: that is what the file's form is for, and the moonwalk's held frames in mid-picture are kept for their time. Android let it go after about a tenth of a second and went on to the second frame. **The popper went off half a second early:** 2.77 s after the trip's start where 3.2 were due. The second picture's first frame, the pop's own, was on the screen for 17 ms, one refresh, where it has 42. It looks as if Android shows the second frame as soon as it is decoded, whatever the first frame's time; that is a reading of what was seen, not of Android's code.
- **What is done about it.** The app shows a picture's first frame itself, the picture not yet playing, and starts it half a frame's time before that frame's time is up (`START_AHEAD_MS`). The frames he stands still in are no longer in the file. Measured after it: he first moves 0.55 s after he is first drawn, the popper goes off 3.1 s after, and the pop's frame is on the screen for 50 ms.
- **A picture started while nothing else changes does not begin.** Android begins to play a picture when it next draws it. The first try of the above started the second picture one refresh after showing it, with him wholly there and nothing else changing: its first frame stood for 1.9 seconds, until the fade-out began to draw again, and the confetti then played through the fade. Starting a picture now changes a number the drawing reads, which draws again. The first picture had started all the same in that build, because coming to Home drew the screen for half a second anyway: it would not have on a phone with a quiet Home.
- **The greetings were left as they are.** Their animated pictures begin with a 42 ms frame in which the mascot is all but still, and are started in a drawing that changes him anyway.

**`[FINDING]` Asked for before Setup's Done was pressed, the party went to Home and never played, and the request stayed**
Found on the emulator in a second session, on a MilO installed fresh whose first start's page was left with OK and whose Setup was left with Back. The party over Home had the greeting's rule, "the first start is over", and that means Setup's Done button was pressed.
- **What happened.** The two-second rest led to Home and nothing played. The request was kept (`OneYearParty.askedFor`), so a second rest did nothing at all, not even go to Home; and the party would have played, with its pop at alarm volume, whenever Done was pressed later and Home came up. The same kept request waited behind animations that were off: asked for then, the party would have played when they were switched on again. On the day itself such a phone would have had the pop at every trip's start and never the party.
- **Why the first session did not see it.** There Setup's Done had been pressed before anything else was tried. **Which stage the phone's own MilO is in, nobody has looked.**
- **What was changed.** `homeIsDrawn` (`feature/party/Party.kt`): Home is drawn once the first start's page has been left. `PartyWatch` goes by it, and drops a request on demand that reaches Home with the animations off. Three more tests in `PartyTest`. The dropping is two lines in a Composable and has no unit test; it was seen on the emulator.
- **Seen after it, before Setup's Done:** the party on demand; a whole party at a trip's start on the day, 2.53 seconds from his first movement to the pop; and the request with the animations off, which led to Home, drew nothing, made no sound, and played nothing in the twelve seconds after they were switched on again.
- **A lesson for the next such check:** an emulator that has been set up by hand once hides what a fresh one shows. The fault was found only because the second session began from nothing.

**`[FINDING]` What the party weighs, and what was tried**
The aim was 2.5 MB for everything it adds. Packed as the greetings' pictures are, at the greetings' size, the party came to 3 MB, and at the film's own pixels to more than 4.
- **Where the bytes are.** A frame of him alone, 375 pixels high, is about 18 KB: 14 of colour and 4.5 of see-through channel. A frame with the confetti is about 37 KB: 25 and 12.5. Paper in the air doubles a frame, and a third of that is the see-through channel, which every small piece adds an edge to.
- **Size matters less than expected.** All 119 frames at colour 50 and see-through channel 50: he 375 pixels high, 2.97 MB; 450, 3.68; 480, 4.00; 525, 4.37; **the film's own 600, 4.16.** A frame made smaller is sharper than a frame drawn small, and packs worse; the film's own frames are the smoothest. So fewer pixels buy less than they cost in looks, but they were the only thing that bought enough.
- **At one weight, about 2.45 MB,** more pixels packed harder and fewer packed softer came out alike when each was shown at the film's size and held against the drawn frame: 375 pixels at colour 33, 34.2 dB; 400 at 28, 34.4; 420 at 22, 34.5; 450 at 15, 34.4. The smallest was taken: a frame of it is a third of the work for the phone, and the party must not fall behind its sound.
- **Him and the confetti as two pictures, one over the other,** was not lighter: him alone 1.95 MB and the confetti alone 1.13 MB by the trial in the mascot's README, against 2.97 MB for both in one. And it is not the film: a piece of paper that falls behind him would be drawn in front.
- **What was free.** The clip's last frame, in which nothing of him is left; one frame that is the frame before; sixteen frames in which only a part of him moves, stored as that part. And a frame that is fading out, which is packed with as much less colour as it is shown less strongly.
- **What was paid.** Colour at 40 before the pop and 28 from it on, where the wave has 60 and the moonwalk 50; the see-through channel at 30 for the confetti's frames. And the size: 1.64 pixels to the dp. Held beside the film's frame at the film's size, the numbers on his dials are soft; the confetti keeps its shapes and its colours. `pack_party.py` plays its pictures back against the drawn frames: 0.8 and 1.6 of 255 a pixel on average at worst.
- **With the packing as it ended up,** the two pictures weigh 2.31 MB with him 375 pixels high, 2.87 MB at 450, and 3.29 MB at the film's own 600.
- **The limit in `MascotPicturesTest`:** the party's two under 2.4 MB, counted apart from the greetings' four, whose limits were not touched.

**`[FINDING]` The party on the emulator, measured**
Android 16 on an emulator whose screen is the film's size (1080 by 2400 pixels, 411 dp wide, drawn 60 times a second); the debug build; the evening of 2026-10-10 and the first minutes of 2026-10-11. Three small scripts, kept out of the repository, did the reading. One found in every frame of a screen recording which of the clip's 130 frames the app was showing, by the packed pictures played back. One put a recording on the emulator's own clock by Android's frame statistics (`dumpsys gfxinfo framestats`): 119 or 120 of each recording's 120 frames in the statistics' span lay on a frame of the statistics. One read Android's audio log (`dumpsys audio`), which says to the millisecond when each player was made, started and stopped. **The emulator ran without sound hardware, and its recordings have no sound: nobody has heard the pop from the app.** What is known of the sound is what that log says.
- **How MilO came to be a year old.** An export file written for it, brought in with Settings' Import: a trip recorded a year ago to the day and since moved two days earlier by hand, and a trip typed in by hand for 1 June of that year. The Log named the day of the recorded start ("the day of its first recorded trip (2025-10-10)"), not the moved trip's day and not the typed one's. Done twice: for 10 October, and after midnight for 11 October.
- **Start trip pressed with Home in front** (seven times over the two evenings): no player was made at the trip's start, and one was made by the party. One pop for the trip, every time. With the build as it ended up the mascot was drawn 0.1 to 0.2 seconds after the trip's start, and the pop came 3.2 seconds after it.
- **A trip started with MilO in the background** (six times, by the command the widget's Start button sends): the pop 6 to 53 ms after the trip's line in the Log, and no chirp. MilO opened some ten seconds later: the party, with its own pop on the popper's frame.
- **End trip pressed in the middle of the party,** 1.7 seconds after Start: the trip ended, the trip service was gone, and the party played to its end with its pop. No other button was pressed under it.
- **With the phone's animations off** (three times): Start trip pressed with Home in front, the pop 19 to 48 ms after the trip's line and nothing drawn in nine seconds. Switched on again with Home in front: the party that was owed played, with its pop.
- **A fresh start with a party owed:** the party and no greeting, and the greeting's count stood at 5 before and after. The next fresh start greeted, and the count was 6.
- **Midnight, as the clock brought it.** At 23:26:49 on the 10th a trip was started in the background and MilO was not opened again: a party was owed. At 00:00:44 on the 11th MilO was opened: no frame of the party in the recording. Start trip pressed at 00:00:59, and a trip started in the background at 00:01:16: "Connect sound: playing the bundled chirp" both times, no line of the party's and no frame of it. What was stored for Home was still there, unchanged, and owed nothing; the greeting's count was untouched.
- **Every frame in its order,** in seven recordings: from his first movement (the clip's frame 13) to the last frame he is wholly there in (120), every frame was shown, in its order, and none of the other picture's. (The matcher names 106 of the 108. Frames 33 and 35 are the same picture as 32, in the film's own frames too: he feels about behind his back.)
- **The lengths.** From his first movement to the pop 2.53 to 2.57 seconds, where the film has 2.50; from the pop to the last frame he is wholly there in 1.98 to 2.02, where the film has 1.96. Held against the film at every quarter second he is in the same poses, one to two frames behind: a frame is 42 ms where the film's is 41.7, and each of the two pictures begins within a drawing of the screen.
- **The seam.** The last frame before the pop stood for 50 ms in each of the five recordings that were put on the clock, and the pop's frame for 50 or 67. A frame of 42 ms stands for 33 or 50 on this screen. Nothing came between the two.
- **The pop and its frame.** In those five the pop's player was made between 5 ms before and 6 ms after the screen refresh the pop's frame was drawn for, and that frame was finished 11 to 31 ms after the refresh. Android reported the sound started 3 to 8 ms after the refresh in four of them, and 82 ms after in the fifth, which was the first sound that MilO process played. It reported the sound stopped 1.03 to 1.09 seconds after it started; the file is 1.00.
- **On its side:** he is about half the size, in the middle of the screen, and plays from first to last.
- **Not by accident** (the second session, the corrected build): a tap on the grey line, a rest of one second and a rest of 1.7 seconds did nothing. MilO stayed on What's new and no sound was played.
- **Left in the middle, on demand:** the bar's Trips button 1.9 seconds into the party, and the phone's Home key the same. He was gone with the press, no pop followed, and he did not come back when Home did.
- **Left before the pop, on the day:** Start trip with Home in front, then the Trips button 1.5 seconds on; and again with the Home key. One pop each time, 1.6 and 1.7 seconds after the trip's start, as the party ended. No second pop at two seconds, no party when Home came back, and nothing left owed.
- **Start trip pressed under him, on demand:** the trip started with the chirp, as on any day, and he went on to his pop.
- **Not tried on any emulator:** both sounds switched off on the day, pictures that cannot be read, a trip the truck starts, and a leap day. Unit tests hold these.
- **He is softer than in the film,** as the `[DEBT]` below says: on the stills at the screen's own pixels the numbers on his dials are the place to look.
- **Nothing fell over.** Android's crash log has nothing of MilO's from either evening, and MilO wrote no crash file.
- **By the way, with a script that pressed the wrong thing:** two commas were typed into a text field of Settings on the emulator, and taken out again. The emulator was read-only and keeps nothing.

**`[FINDING]` Android 16 says that a sound from the background "would be muted"**
In the emulator's audio log (`dumpsys audio`), for the pop of a trip that was started with MilO in the background: "AudioHardening background playback would be muted for com.shawnkowalchuk.milo, level: full". The sound was played. It is the rule the encyclopedia has under "On Android 17 the sounds of an automatic start would be silent", announcing itself one version early, and it is about every sound MilO plays from a trip that started in the background, the chirp as much as the pop. The phone runs Android 14 and is not affected. Nothing was changed for it. One thing is not known and should be: whether a trip the truck starts through the companion service counts as "from the background" for this rule. The trip on the emulator was started by a command, as the widget's Start button starts one.

**`[DEBT]` The party's mascot is softer than in the film Shawn approved**
The film was drawn at a phone's own pixels: he 600 pixels high on a screen 1080 wide. The app's pictures have him 375 pixels high and are shown as large as the film showed him, so every pixel of them covers about one and a half of the phone's. The numbers on his dials are soft where the film's are crisp; his shape, his colours, the cap, the popper and the confetti read as in the film, and it lasts five and a half seconds. It was done for the weight: 2.5 MB was the aim for everything the party adds, and it adds 2.47. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `MascotPartyPicture.kt`; device check OY-2. **Cost to fix:** one number in `pack_party.py` (`APP_PIXELS`), the pictures packed again, three numbers in `MascotPartyPicture` and the limit in `MascotPicturesTest`: an hour. **What it costs in weight:** 0.56 MB more with him 450 pixels high, 0.98 MB more at the film's own 600, where the party would add 3.45 MB to the download. Shawn's to choose once he has seen OY-2 on the phone; he asked for the mascot's first renderer to be taken out for its weight (ADR-007).

**`[DEBT]` A MilO that starts inside a date trick on the eve of the party could pop a day early**
ADR-006 leaves one gap open: a MilO process that starts with nothing to check the phone's date against (the first after the phone was switched on, or after an update), in the very seconds the date is set a day ahead, goes by that date until it comes back. The party goes by the day, so a trip that started in those seconds, on the evening before the day, would have the pop in place of its connect sound. What it stored for Home lies a day ahead once the date is back, and is not shown. It takes a restart of the phone, the date trick and a trip's start within the same few seconds, on one evening of the year. Pinned by `ClockJumpPartyTest`; tagged `TODO(debt)` in `OneYearParty.kt`. **Cost to fix:** hand the party "is MilO's clock on probation?", as the two daily checks are handed it, and have no party while it is; an hour with its tests. Not done: the cure would take the pop from a trip that starts within two minutes of the phone being switched on, on the day itself, which is likelier than the fault.

**`[DEBT]` `MiloApp` is 212 lines, twelve over what the standards ask of a Composable**
STANDARDS, section 3: "no Composable over ~200". `MiloApp` stood at 199. The one-year party needs thirteen lines there: its ViewModel, whether a party has the screen (for the greeting), the watch on Home, the drawing over the screen, and the request that "Keep editing" drops. Everything else of the party's is in `app/PartyObjects.kt`. Tagged `TODO(debt)` at `MiloApp`. **Cost to fix:** move the wiring of the greeting and of the What's new notice out of `MiloApp` into their own files in `app/`, as the party's is; an hour, and a refactor of its own, not to be mixed into a feature (STANDARDS, section 6).

**`[CHANGE]` The moonwalk is in the app: a second greeting, which takes turns with the wave**
Phone only. **Built, not yet proven on the phone.** Shawn, of the mascot: "i want to have a animation with him moon walking on to the screen stops mid way. adoes some hip thrusts than spins and continues moon walking ... if we can add a hat for this animation it would be cool"; where it plays: "yes take turns with the wave, and show me a preview first"; of the preview (the entry "A moonwalk for the mascot, in a hat" below): "it looks good". The app now plays what that film showed: he moonwalks in from the right in his hat, skids to a stop in the middle with his brake lights on, holds his hat and thrusts his hips three times, kicks, turns once round, and moonwalks on and out at the left. No fade, no sound. One start he waves, the next he moonwalks. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, The greeting; ADR-007, the addendum; device checks MW-1 to MW-11.
- **New:** two pictures in `app/src/main/res/drawable-nodpi/` (`mascot_moonwalk.webp`, 130 KB, and `mascot_stop_and_spin.webp`, 768 KB), `design/mascot/scripts/pack_moonwalk.py`, which packs them, `MascotMoonwalk.kt` (the moonwalk's numbers and its two glides, pure) and `MascotGreetingKind.kt` (the two greetings, and what every greeting is made of) in the design system, `feature/greeting/GreetingTurn.kt` (whose turn it is, pure) and `data/settings/GreetingStorage.kt` (`greetings_shown`). **Changed:** `Mascot.kt` and `MascotGreeting.kt` play either greeting; `MascotWalk.kt` keeps the wave's numbers and walk; `GreetingViewModel` reads the turn and stores it; `greeting()` waits for the turn to be read; `MiloApp` hands the greeting on. The wave's two pictures are the committed ones, byte for byte.
- **What is not obvious from the code.** One course plays both greetings, so the wave now runs through code that was written again; its numbers, its pictures and its timing are untouched. On the emulator three recordings of this build's wave were laid over a recording of `main`'s, frame for frame: the mascot is in sight for as long (142 to 145 frames against 142 and 143), and his place agrees as well as two recordings of `main`'s build agree with each other. The moonwalk's glide in begins partway into a cycle, so that it ends on the cycle's last frame with him just beyond the edge at its start; the wave still walks whole cycles. The glide out is the same sheet played a second time. The greeting a start gets is read once and held for the whole start, because storing its own turn would otherwise change it under itself.
- **What it weighs.** The mascot's pictures are 1,778 KB where they were 880 KB. The release APK, built unsigned the same way before and after, is 13,046,669 bytes where it was 12,131,739: 915 KB more, 7.5 %. All four pictures are in it byte for byte, under the short names the release build gives them (`res/Il.webp` and the like).
- **Tests.** 2,668 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,630 before; 38 new): `MascotMoonwalkTest` (17), `GreetingTurnTest` (4), nine more in `GreetingViewModelTest`, six more in `MascotPicturesTest`, one more each in `GreetingTest` and `MascotWalkTest`. **Existing tests changed:** `MascotWalkTest`'s tests of a greeting's stages use the stages' new names (below); `GreetingTest`'s helper has one more parameter; `GreetingViewModelTest`'s one test builds its ViewModel with a settings file; `MascotPicturesTest`'s weight test is now the wave's, and the limit for all four is new. No assertion about the wave changed.
- **Run on an emulator** (Android 16, the debug builds of `main` and of this branch, started for this and shut down; the phone not touched). Seen: the moonwalk from first to last, laid over the preview at every quarter second; the wave and the moonwalk in turn over six fresh starts after a force stop, and over three more after Back in the same process; `main`'s build installed, greeted once, this build installed over it, and the next greeting the moonwalk; the same with a higher version, where What's new opened first with no mascot and the moonwalk came at the start after; a new install, whose first start showed no greeting, whose next showed the wave and whose third the moonwalk; Start trip pressed while he waved and while he moonwalked, and the trip started both times; Home left with the bar's Trips button seven times in the middle of a greeting; MilO put away, and the emulator turned, in the middle; nothing with animations off; both greetings at 274, 393 and 610 dp wide, and with the emulator on its side. MilO stayed open throughout, and its crash log stayed empty. The measurements are the `[FINDING]` below.
- **Not run:** on the phone. And the release build with this change was built and looked into, not run: it comes out unsigned without the key, and nothing here reaches code by its name. MW-11 is its first run.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the moonwalk greeting**
He asked for the moonwalk, for it to take turns with the wave, and approved the preview. These points he was not asked:
1. **The turn is a count.** The settings file holds how many greetings the phone has been shown (`greetings_shown`); an even count means the wave is due, an odd one the moonwalk. A count needs no second list of the greetings' names in the settings, and a third greeting can join the round.
2. **After the update the moonwalk comes first; on a new phone the wave.** Both phones have no count. They are told apart by the first start: a phone whose first start is over had MilO before and counts as 1; a phone in the middle of its first start is new, counts as 0, and that 0 is stored then and there, because at its next start it would look like the other kind.
3. **A greeting counts as shown from the moment the mascot is first drawn,** also when it is cut short a moment later. That was the task's rule, and it was kept. **One thing was added to it: a start that never drew him does not take the turn.** With the phone's animations off, with What's new or a notification's screen on top, or when a press leaves Home before his pictures are read, nothing was shown, and the greeting that was due stays due. Otherwise the update's own What's new would have taken the moonwalk's first turn, and Shawn would have seen a wave where he was promised the new thing.
4. **If the moonwalk's pictures cannot be read, the wave plays in its place and the turn passes on.** The Log gets one `ERROR` line that says so. If the wave's cannot be read either, there is no greeting and a second line.
5. **A settings file that cannot be read gets the wave** at every start. A count that cannot be stored is an `ERROR` line; the greeting goes on, and the same greeting comes again next time.
6. **He is seen at once.** The glide in starts with him just beyond the right edge, partway into a cycle, as the preview played it. The wave starts up to a cycle further out and is up to half a second out of sight; that was left as it is.
7. **A frame is shown for 42 ms, as in the wave's pictures,** where the preview's film had 24 frames a second, 41.7 ms. The picture's form counts in whole milliseconds. Over the whole greeting that is 0.06 seconds more.
8. **How hard the pictures are packed** (ADR-007, the addendum): colour at 50 where the wave's is at 60, the frames cut down, two frames not stored and five stored as patches. And the see-through channel finer than the wave's where there is a glow, which costs bytes and is not a choice (next entry).
9. **The limit in `MascotPicturesTest`:** each greeting under one megabyte, all four pictures under two. It was one megabyte for the mascot.
10. **The count is this phone's.** It is not in an export file. Android's backup carries it with the rest of the settings.
11. **The names.** `mascot_moonwalk.webp` and `mascot_stop_and_spin.webp`, beside `mascot_walk.webp` and `mascot_turn_and_wave.webp`. In the code a greeting's stages are now "coming", "in the middle" and "leaving", where they were "walking", "waving" and "leaving": the old names were the wave's.
12. **The line in the list of changes** names no performer and no song, and says that the moonwalk comes first after the update.

**`[FINDING]` Packed like the wave's pictures, the halo is drawn as rings**
`pack_greeting.py` stores the see-through channel at 50 of 100. For the wave that is right: the channel is then twelve steps from see-through to solid, and an edge needs no more. The moonwalk's frames have a soft red halo round the lit brake lights, which is nothing but that channel fading out. In twelve steps it is a set of rings, as plain on the dark page as a target. Found by laying a packed frame beside the drawn one, before anything was built. The trial that had weighed the moonwalk at 1.17 MB had packed it this way, so its figure was for a picture that did not look like the preview.
- **What it takes:** at 80 the channel has 96 steps and is within one of 255 of what was drawn, everywhere. At 70 and under it has sixteen steps or fewer. So `pack_moonwalk.py` packs a frame with a glow in it at 80 and the others at 50. A frame has a glow when more than twice as many of its pixels are partly see-through as in the frame with the fewest: the skid's 14 frames and the turn's 6. That costs 75 KB over packing all 80 at 50.
- **Kept whole (100) the channel would cost 38 KB more** and show nothing more.
- **The halo is right on a dark ground only** (the `[DEBT]` below).

**`[FINDING]` A frame of the mascot is about 10 KB, however it is packed**
The moonwalk is 94 frames. Tried on both pictures together: as committed, 898 KB. Without the five patches, 933. With every frame stored whole, 953. Colour at 60, as in the wave, 955. Colour at 40, 838, but the numbers on the dials and the lines between his fingers smear at twice a phone's size. The see-through channel fine on every frame, 991. Seven tenths of a frame's bytes are its colour and three tenths its see-through channel. Fewer pixels would save more than any of it (the wave's pictures lost 18 % at 2 pixels to the dp, 2026-10-09), and he would be softer than the waving mascot he takes turns with; that was not tried.
- **What was free:** two of the 80 frames are the frame before, to within what two runs of the renderer differ by (he stands still for a twelfth of a second, twice). They are not stored; the frame before is shown for 84 ms. In five more only the needles move; those are stored as the box that changed, 1 to 5 KB each, and laid over the frame before.
- **`pack_moonwalk.py` plays its own picture back** and holds each frame against the drawn one over the dark page: 1.2 of 255 a pixel on average at worst. A patch in the wrong place would stop it.
- **Two traps in the tools.** A drawn frame holds a colour under its wholly see-through pixels, up to full white: two frames that look the same differ there, and are compared only after that colour is cleared. And Pillow knows how long a frame of an animated picture is shown only once the frame has been read.
- **Cutting the frames down saved no bytes,** only memory: an empty margin costs next to nothing in the file. The sheet is 3,360 by 960 pixels, 12.9 MB on the phone, where uncut it would have been 3,920 by 1,184 and 18.6 MB.

**`[FINDING]` The moonwalk on the emulator, measured**
A small script, kept out of the repository, found in every recorded frame which of the 94 packed pictures the app was showing and where, by laying each over the frame's green. Six recordings of the moonwalk: three at 411 dp wide, one each at 274, 393 and 610.
- **Every frame in its place.** In both glides no frame showed the step of another place, and every frame stood within 0.2 dp of where its step belongs. The foot on its toe moved at most 0.8 dp across and 1.1 dp up and down while it stood; the clip as Blender draws it has 0.6 and 1.2 of that.
- **The seams.** The glide's last frame, one step short of the middle, was followed by the middle part's first 31 to 50 ms later. The middle part's last frame was followed by the glide's first, in the same place, 51 to 84 ms later: its 42 ms and up to one drawing of the screen. No other picture came between, in any recording.
- **The middle part.** All 80 frames, in their order, in 3.32 to 3.35 seconds at 274, 393 and 411 dp, which is its length, and in 3.42 at 610 dp.
- **The patches are laid right** by this Android (16). After the turn only the needles move for four frames, and those four are patches. In three recordings (274, 393 and 411 dp) the dials' part of each recorded frame there was held against the packed frames one by one: each was closest to its own frame, in order, and the rest of him was whole. The phone's Android is 14; MW-3.
- **The lengths.** By the rules 152 frames at 274 dp (6.4 s), 173 at 393 (7.3 s), 176 at 411 (7.4 s) and 211 at 610 (8.9 s). His green was in sight for 6.8 seconds at 411 dp, where it is 6.75 in the preview.
- **Against the preview, at 411 dp, where the emulator's screen is the film's size:** he stands on the same rows and in the same places, to within 3 pixels, and in the same poses at every quarter second up to the kick. From there the app is one to two frames behind: a frame is 42 ms where the film's is 41.7, and the middle part's last frame is held up to one drawing of the screen longer. At the end that is two frames, 164 in sight against 162. He is a little softer than in the film, which was drawn at the screen's own 2.625 pixels to the dp; the app's pictures have 2.5 and are packed harder. That shows on the dials' numbers when the two are laid side by side and enlarged.
- **How it ran there.** Smoothly: 23.0 to 23.5 recorded frames a second where 23.8 is every frame. That is this emulator on this Mac that evening. What a phone's screen does with a picture stepped 24 times a second is MW-2.
- **A press before he is drawn.** Once the bar's Trips button was pressed a second after MilO was drawn, before the mascot's pictures had been read. No greeting, the count stayed as it was, and the next start played the moonwalk that had been due.

**`[FINDING]` An install that fails says nothing if its last line is all that is read**
The emulator's storage was 96 % full, and the first `adb install` over `main`'s build failed with "Requested internal only, but not enough space". The command's output was cut to its last line, which belonged to something else. The next start was recorded as "the first start after the update", and showed the wave: it was `main`'s build still. Found because the settings file held no count afterwards. **Check that an install happened** (`dumpsys package`, `lastUpdateTime`), and read all of what `adb install` says. `pm trim-caches` made room; the emulator ran read-only, so nothing of it is kept.

**`[FINDING]` The release build packs a resource under another name**
In the release APK the four pictures are `res/Rn.webp`, `res/Il.webp`, `res/aQ.webp` and `res/6_.webp`. The line the Log gets when a picture is not an animated one names the picture. The first build of this change asked Android for that name (`getResourceEntryName`), which was not needed and would have been one more thing that only the release build does differently, in a path that is never taken. The names are written out in `Mascot.kt` instead.

**`[DEBT]` The moonwalk's halo is right on a dark ground only**
A see-through picture cannot store "add this light to what lies behind". The halo round the lit brake lights is stored as red that covers a part (the entry "A halo in a see-through picture" below). Over Home's dark page and dark tiles that is the glow. **Over the lime tile it is an orange mist that darkens the lime.** Seen on the emulator twice: on its side, where the Start trip tile is behind him, and at 274 dp wide, where everything is larger and the tile reaches down to him. Held upright at 393 and 411 dp wide he stands in front of dark tiles and the dark page, and that is what the preview showed. It lasts for the 20 frames with the lights in view, 0.8 seconds in all. Tagged `TODO(debt)` in `MascotMoonwalk.kt`; device check MW-10. **Cost to fix:** draw the halo in the app instead of into the pictures, as light added to the screen (a second small picture of the glow alone, laid on with the "plus" blend), which is right on any ground: the frames drawn again without the halo, the glow drawn and packed alone for 20 frames, one more picture read and laid in step with the animated one, which Android does not let the app follow frame by frame; a day, and the hard part is the last. Or leave the halo out where the ground is not dark. Or lock the greeting to the phone held upright. Shawn's to choose once he has seen MW-10.

**`[FIX]` A reading typed before driving off no longer loses the drive from the odometer**
Shawn: "i have my phone connected i made a trip reset the mileage and it was off by 9 km when complete. this doesnt seem to be a tracking the trips but adding to vehicle mileage problem". Asked when he typed the reading: "yes i typed the km to the accurate reading before driving today and the kms are now 102729". MilO showed 102,720. The day had three trips, 22.8 km together. The first, about 9 km, was the one missing; the two later ones were added. He was right about where the fault was: the trip was recorded in full, and the adding was wrong.
- **The cause.** The odometer's rule of 2026-10-07: "a trip counts by its start, so a reading typed just after parking holds the trip still open". It was written for a reading typed after parking. But MilO opens a trip the moment the phone connects to the truck. A reading typed in the truck before driving off is therefore typed with a trip already open. That trip had started before the reading, so all of it was taken to be in the reading, and the drive that followed was never added. The entry of 2026-10-09 even says so: "a reading typed during the trip already holds it, and the odometer then stands at that reading until the next trip".
- **The rule now.** A reading says what the dashboard showed at the moment it was typed. What the open trip drives after that moment is added. What it had driven before is not. Every other trip counts by its start, as before.
- **How.** "Save reading" keeps, with the reading, which trip was open and how far it had gone at the press: the trip's number and the trip controller's running distance in metres, the figure Home shows (`TripAtReading`, `OdometerViewModel.onSaveReading`). `odometerAt` cuts that one trip in two at the reading (`cutAtReadings`, new file `core/odometer/ReadingDuringTrip.kt`). The part before keeps the trip's start; the part after starts at the reading. All the old rules then hold for the parts: which reading is used, the correction of a slip, "est." on the report. `DrivenTrip` carries the trip's number for it, and `drivenTrips` hands it over.
- **The rule is in the one function everything asks,** so Home, Settings and the report follow it with no change of their own. Nothing under `feature/home/` changed. Nothing on Android Auto shows an odometer.
- **The report was wrong in two places, not one.** For a period that ended on such a day, the end was short by the trip. For a period that began on such a day, the start was too low by the trip as well, because the trip was taken away from the reading. Both are right now: the end is the reading plus the drive ("est."), and the start is the reading as typed, with no "est.", when it was typed before driving off.
- **No word on any screen changed,** and no string was added.
- **Tests.** 2,630 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,585 before; 45 new). The first was written before the fix and failed with his figures: "expected 102729 but was 102720". New: `OdometerDuringTripTest` (20: his day; before driving off, on the way, after parking; two readings in one trip; a slip corrected in the same trip; a moment before the reading; a trip that is gone; a trip that only shares the number; a distance lowered by hand; miles; an old reading; the report's start and end), `OdometerViewModelTest` (6: what "Save reading" keeps), `ReportOdometerDuringTripTest` (4), eight more in `OdometerStorageTest` and seven more in `VehicleOdometersTest`. **Existing tests changed:** four assertions in three tests of `TruckTripsTest` now expect the trip's number beside its start and distance, and one of the three is renamed for it. One test of `VehicleOdometersTest`, "a reading typed during the trip already holds it", stated the old rule as the rule; its figures stand, for a reading from before today, and its name now says so. Every other odometer test passes untouched.
- **On an emulator** (Android 16, the debug builds of `main` and of this branch, started for this and shut down; the phone not touched). A trip was started, the reading 102,720 typed in Settings with the trip open, and 2.1 km driven with one GPS fix a second. `main`: Home and Settings stayed at 102,720, "Your reading of 102,720 km, typed today". This build, the same again with the reading 102,722: 102,724, "Your reading of 102,722 km on Oct 10, 2026, plus 2.1 km of truck trips since". In a third trip Home counted up from the reading while the fixes came in: 102,731, then 102,732. One step was done by hand each time, outside the app (next finding).
- **Not run:** in the truck. Device checks OD-7 to OD-10. The release build was not made here (no key on this run); the change reaches no code by its name, so R8 has nothing new to break.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided for Shawn in the odometer fix**
He reported the fault and confirmed when he typed the reading. These points he was not asked:
1. **A stretch that is printed as 0.0 km is no driving** (less than 50 m). The GPS can count a few metres while the truck stands. Without this, a reading typed before driving off would have "a truck trip" before it, the report would mark it "est.", and a slip typed again a minute later in the same open trip would no longer be a correction. No sum loses anything: such a stretch adds 0.0 in either unit.
2. **The trip is kept with the reading for any open trip:** also one that no paired vehicle has been seen in yet, and whichever vehicle's tile the reading is typed on. Whose odometer a trip moves is decided when the odometer is worked out, as for every trip. So a trip started with the button counts from the reading on once the truck joins it.
3. **The trip is known by its number, and is cut only by a reading typed after it started.** A trip that only shares the number (after an import) and started later counts whole. So does a trip whose start he moves by hand to after the reading.
4. **A trip that ends shorter than it stood at the reading** adds nothing and takes nothing away. The closed trip is measured again without what came after the truck disconnected, and he can lower a distance by hand.
5. **Readings from before today are left as they are** (`[DEBT]` below). Nothing stored is rewritten.
6. **The trip goes into a second set in the settings file,** `odometer_reading_trips`, and the reading's own entry is unchanged. Why: a build from before today skips a reading whose entry it does not know. He has two channels, and the copy from Google Play can be a version behind the one from GitHub; after a restore an older build can meet a newer file. It then still shows every reading, by its old rule. Tried on the emulator: `main`'s build installed over this build's data read both readings, wrote one of its own, and left the second set alone; this build then read all of it.
7. **The line in the list of changes** says what he should do about the reading he typed today: type the dashboard's figure once more.

**`[FINDING]` An emulator cannot record a trip that moves an odometer; one step was done by hand**
A trip moves an odometer only once a paired vehicle has been seen connected in it (`movesOdometer`, `truckSeen`). MilO believes that only from a reading of the Bluetooth link or from Android's own Bluetooth broadcasts. An emulator has neither. `cmd companiondevice simulate-device-event` opens a trip "by TRUCK", but the trip is never confirmed and is discarded after 15 seconds as a false start (2026-10-10, the marketing kit's finding). A trip by button records, and counts for no odometer, on `main` and on this branch alike.
- **So one thing was done by hand, outside the app:** after the trip had been recorded, its row in the emulator's database was marked as a truck's connection would have marked it (`trips.truckSeen = 1`), and MilO was started again. No line of the app was changed for it. The emulator image has no `sqlite3` and no root, so the debug build's database was copied out with `run-as`, changed on the Mac and put back.
- **What that leaves unproven:** that a real connect marks the trip in time. That is the trip rules' part, proven on the phone on 2026-10-06, and this change does not touch it.
- **With the open trip marked and MilO restarted,** MilO read the truck, found it gone and began the two minutes of grace ("Waiting for the truck to reconnect"). The odometer counted up during them, then stepped back by one when the trip closed where the truck was "found gone". That is the step back of 2026-10-09, not something new.
- **Two debug builds of the same version install over each other in both directions,** with the throwaway debug key: that is how "an older build reads the newer file" was tried.

**`[FINDING]` Other ways a drive in the truck is missing from the odometer**
Looked for while fixing the one above. None of these is the fault of 2026-10-10, and none was changed.
- **"Ignore them".** With that chosen for trips outside the work hours, a Personal trip is recorded in the truck to its end and then stored as discarded. A discarded trip moves no odometer, and the app does not even hand it over: the odometer is given finished trips only. So with that switch on, the odometer falls behind the dashboard by every evening and weekend drive. That is what his choice of 2026-10-07, "Every truck trip", was made to prevent. Shown with a test that fails: a discarded trip of 12.3 km, truck seen, marked as left out by "Ignore them": the odometer shows the reading, where the dashboard shows 12 km more. The test is not in the repository, because it fails. The switch is off out of the box. `[DEBT]`: his decision. Cost to fix: count such a trip in `movesOdometer`, and read such trips for the odometer in its three places (Home, Settings, the report); a few hours, with tests.
- **A trip under the shortest distance that counts** (0.3 km out of the box) is discarded too, so a move across the yard is never added. By design; device check OD-6 measures what it adds up to.
- **A trip started with the button that the truck never joins** moves no odometer, also when it was in fact the truck with Bluetooth off. By design ("Every truck trip").
- **A trip whose start he moves by hand to the other side of a reading** changes sides with it, unless the reading was typed during that trip. Not guarded.
- **Not a loss:** a trip the parked truck's moving starts. It is opened when the movement is seen, after any reading typed while parked, and gets the watch's points, so all of it is added.

**`[DEBT]` A reading typed during a trip before 0.3.1 is not put right, and his of 2026-10-10 is one**
Such a reading does not say how far its trip had gone. MilO cannot tell from it whether it was typed before driving off or after parking, so it keeps the old rule for it: the whole trip is in the reading. **After the update his odometer is therefore still 9 km short.** The emulator showed the same: this build installed over `main`'s data left the old reading's figure as it was. **What he does: Settings, Adjust, type the number on the dashboard.** Once, at any moment, also during a trip. From then on it follows.
- **What stays off after that:** a figure for a moment of 2026-10-10 itself, if a report is ever made for a period that begins or ends that day.
- **Cost to fix:** the trip's GPS points are still on the phone, each with its time, so how far the trip had gone when the reading was typed could be worked out once and kept. Half a day with tests. Not done: one typed figure cures it, and the points are not in a cloud backup, so the repair would not work the same on a restored phone.

**`[CHANGE]` A moonwalk for the mascot, in a hat: a preview, and nothing in the app**
Shawn: "i want to have a animation with him moon walking on to the screen stops mid way. adoes some hip thrusts than spins and continues moon walking. i will supply a video for you to refference the movements. if we can add a hat for this animation it would be cool. i want it to be like Micheal Jackson video." Asked where it plays: "yes take turns with the wave, and show me a preview first". So this round is the preview: the model and its scripts in `design/mascot/` only. Nothing under `app/` or `website/` changed, no picture they carry was drawn again, and the list of changes has no line, because he will notice nothing in the app. New: a black fedora with a green band (the object `Hat`, a slider `hat` that puts it on); four clips, `Moonwalk`, `MoonwalkStop`, `KickSpin` and `MoonwalkOn`, the first to light the brake lights; `render_moonwalk.py`, which draws their frames, and `preview_moonwalk.py`, which lays them over `marketing/screenshots/home.png` as a film, a GIF, a sheet and stills. As a greeting it is 7.3 seconds on a phone 411 dp wide. The film and the frames are not committed. His video was looked at for the movements only; nothing of it is in the repository.
- **Nothing he had changes.** The twelve clips there were are the same in every key, bit for bit, and their 1,272 channels in the `.glb` byte for byte. The greeting's 72 frames, the website's 75 and its two pictures of the dials, drawn again from the new file and scripts, against the same from the committed state: within 3 of 255 everywhere but in four single pixels of 52 million, each alone among pixels that agree, which are off by 4. Two runs of the renderer on one and the same file differ by up to 3 (measured this time, on the website's 75 frames), so this is its noise and one step more in four places; nothing that can be seen. Two builds from the scripts give the same clips and the same `.glb`, byte for byte.
- **How it is built is in `design/mascot/README.md`,** "The hat and the moonwalk", with what the app would need if it is built in: two more pictures of about 1.2 MB together, where the greeting's two are 0.9 MB.

**`[FINDING]` The brake lights cannot be seen while he faces the viewer, so he stops with a skid**
The plan was "his brake lights come on as he stops". He glides with his front to the viewer, three quarters turned, and the lights are on his back: from there not a point of them is in view, and a glow that is hidden has no halo either. So the stop is a skid: his back end swings round to the viewer, the lights come on there and are held for a third of a second, and he comes back round into the pose. They are seen a second time as his back goes past in the turn. If Shawn would rather he did not show his back before the turn, the skid comes out and the lights are then seen in the turn only.

**`[FINDING]` Gliding straight back, a planted foot crept up and down the screen**
The greeting shows him half turned to the viewer and moves the picture level across the screen. A foot that goes straight back along the way he faces also goes away from the viewer, and the camera looks down a little on the floor: the foot that should stay put on its toe moved 7 dp up and down the screen in a third of a second. `Walk`'s feet go half as far and must creep about half as much; that was not measured. For the moonwalk, where the planted foot is the whole trick, the feet go 35 degrees to one side of straight back (`MOON_SLANT`), which is level across the picture at the 55 degrees he is drawn at, and the foot farther from the viewer goes 5.6 % further than the nearer one (`MOON_FAR`), because it is drawn that much smaller. Measured on the drawn clip: the foot on its toe stays within 0.6 dp of its place across and 1.2 dp up and down. **The cost:** the clip is right for that one angle. Drawn at another, both numbers have to be worked out again.

**`[FINDING]` A halo in a see-through picture: it can be stored, for a dark ground, and it has to be made to end**
The brake lights' halo was only ever drawn on a flat ground (`--ground`), because light is added to what lies behind and a see-through picture cannot store that. The preview lays him over Home, which is not one colour. `halo_see_through` in `render_greeting.py` stores the halo beside the mascot as pure red that covers a part, as much as the halo would be bright on black, in the curved numbers a phone lays pictures over one another with. Over the dark page that is the halo all but exactly; over a light ground it would darken what it should light, so it is for dark grounds only. **Blender's glow never quite ends:** half his height away from the lights it was still an eighth of full brightness, and the picture's edge showed over Home as the edge of a faint red square. That much is now taken off the halo everywhere beside the mascot (`HALO_ENDS`), and `render_moonwalk.py` stops if anything is drawn on a picture's border.

**`[DECISION]` The hat is put on by a slider, and every clip that does not ask for it is without it**
A slider `hat` on the skeleton is the size of the bone that carries the hat: at 0 the hat is one point inside the dials and nothing of it is drawn. The file is saved with it at 0, and `reset_pose` sets it to 0, and puts the brake lights out, before any clip is written or drawn. The other way, an object that only one drawing step shows, would have left the hat out of every picture made by the scripts there already are for any clip. The hat, the brake lights and the turn of the whole mascot (the bone `root`) are keyed by the moonwalk's clips only (`key_clip`): in `key_pose` they would have added curves to all twelve clips there were.

**`[FINDING]` The export step left the hat on in the saved Blender file**
Blender's exporter plays every clip to write the `.glb`, and what only the moonwalk's clips key stayed where the last of them left it: the first builds of this change saved the file with the slider `hat` at 1 and the brake lights lit, though the README said 0. No picture showed it, because every script that draws one sets both to 0 before every clip (`reset_pose`), which is also why it was found late, by reading the saved file and not by looking at a picture. `export_milo.py` now puts the sliders and the bones back before the file is saved. Checked in the saved file: `hat` 0 and `brake` 0; and with each clip shown as the scripts show one, the hat has no size, the lights no glow and he is not turned, in every frame of the twelve clips there were. **What stays true of Blender itself:** a clip chosen by hand after one of the moonwalk's is shown with the hat still on, because it says nothing about it (`show("Idle")` puts that right). The old clips could only be made to say so by changing them.

**`[DEBT]` The `.glb` plays the moonwalk without the hat**
A `.glb` has no slider. A clip that says nothing about the hat shows it as it was built, on his head, and Blender's exporter writes nothing for a part that keeps still through a clip, so the old clips cannot be told to hide it: he would have worn it in all twelve. So `export_milo.py` leaves the hat out (`LEFT_OUT`, tagged `TODO(debt)`). The bone `hat` is in the file and the moonwalk's clips move it. Nothing uses the `.glb` today. **To fix when something does:** a second file with the hat in it, or a viewer that is told to show the hat by the moonwalk's clips' names.

**`[CHANGE]` The mascot has brake lights on his back, and they light up**
The model only: nothing in the app or on the website changes, and no picture they carry was drawn again. Shawn: "I want to create three more animation but first i would like you to modify my mascot MilO I would like to add brake light to his back side like a butt. I would like these to be able to light up. Can you first add the lights and give me a visual before we work on the other three animations for the app". New in `design/mascot/`: the object `Brake_Lights` (two round red domes low on the back) with a material of its own, a slider `brake` on the skeleton that lights them, a bone `brake` that carries the slider's value into the `.glb`, `render_lights.py`, a way to draw any clip on a flat ground with a halo round the lit lights (`--ground`), and seven pictures in `renders/` (2.7 MB): his back with the lights off and lit and a three-quarter view lit, each see-through and on the app's dark page, and a turn-around as an animated picture. The mascot's README, "The brake lights".
- **Nothing he had changes.** The twelve clips are the same in every key, bit for bit, and in the `.glb` byte for byte. The greeting's 72 frames and the website's 77, drawn again from the new file and scripts, against the same frames drawn from the committed state: 3 of 255 at most, and not one pixel off by more than 3, which is what two runs of the renderer differ by. Rays cast from the camera to every point of the lenses, in every frame of every clip as it is drawn, the walk at its 55 degrees among them, reach none.
- **No clip lights them yet.** A clip that does keys the slider itself (`set_brake`, `key_brake` in `animate_milo.py`). It is not part of `key_pose`, which would have added a curve to every clip there is.
- **The lit red is kept very pure** (1.0, 0.004, 0.002): the screen cuts each colour off at full, and a bright red with a little green and blue in it is drawn pink.

**`[DECISION]` Of three designs he chose the round ones**
Three were drawn for him side by side, from behind, lit and unlit: A, two round domes close together and proud of the body, the most like a behind; B, two upright rounded rectangles in chrome at the back's outer edges, like a pickup's; C, two round lamps in chrome rims like his dials, a little apart, each lens a ring round a raised middle. Shawn: "use A for the brake lights". B and C are out of `build_milo.py` again, so the script says one thing; they are described here and nowhere else.
- **B would have changed the app.** The walk is drawn turned 55 degrees, and B's lights, at the outer edges, showed round his back edge in it: a sliver 7 pixels wide in all 12 frames of the walk and in 4 of the turn. A and C show in no picture there is.
- **Tried for C and dropped: a third light, high in the middle.** Two round lights and a bar above them read as a face on his back.

**`[FINDING]` Blender's renderer draws no glow round a light**
EEVEE, the renderer the pictures are drawn with, has no bloom of its own in this Blender (5.2). The halo is added after the frame is drawn, in Blender's compositor (`on_ground` in `render_greeting.py`): from the frame's emission pass and from nothing else, so that no highlight on the chrome or the gloss gets one, and added to the frame and to a flat ground under it. It has to be asked for (`--ground` and a colour); the greeting's and the website's pictures are drawn without it and are what they were.
- **A see-through picture cannot hold it.** Light is added to whatever lies behind, and a picture with a see-through background stores colour and cover, not "add this". On the dark page a red with some see-through would come close; on a light ground it is a red mist. The app's pictures are see-through, so this has to be decided before a lit clip goes into the app. `[DEBT]`: not built.
- **The halo shows on the dark page.** On the lime and on white it all but disappears; there the lights read as lit by their own brightness and the warm light they throw on his body.
- **Blender only offers the emission pass to nodes made after it was asked for.** Asked for afterwards, the script stopped with "Emission not found".

**`[FINDING]` The lights' shadow fell on his shoe, in pictures taken from the front**
With the lights on his back, the frames drawn from the front were no longer the committed ones: 366 pixels of the standing frame off by up to 15 of 255, all on the top of one shoe. The studio's rim light stands behind him, and the domes threw a shadow down his back onto the shoe. The lights now cast no shadow, as the glass over the dials does not, and the frames are the old ones again. From behind they keep their shape by their own shading and rims.

**`[FINDING]` What a `.glb` carries of the lights**
A `.glb` holds meshes, materials, a skeleton and its movement. It holds no slider and no rule that ties a material to one; Blender's exporter does not write a glow that follows a slider, with its "pointer animation" on or off. So the bone `brake`, which carries no mesh, grows with the slider from size 1 to 2: a clip that keys only the slider came out as one channel, that bone's size. The lit colour is not in the file, since the lens is exported as it stands, unlit. Whoever plays the file lights the material `MilO_Brake_Lens` from the bone's size: emissive (1.0, 0.004, 0.002) times 4 times (size less 1). The `.glb` is 2.40 MB.

**`[CHANGE]` Home while a trip is recorded: a low tile, a card with two sides, and an odometer whose last wheel turns**
Three requests of Shawn's for the phone's Home, each chosen by him from drawings. Phone only: the Android Auto screen, the widget and the notification are untouched. Built, not yet proven on the phone (device checks HS-1 to HS-14).
- **The lime tile is about half as high.** "also on hte home screen when a trip is recording can we reduce the size of the start trip green button at the top. can you give me different concepts." Of three he chose "Slim": "please use a for the start trip. but with the sqaure stop no text on that button. but also the text for minute for trip under the since time". Two rows: "Recording" with "since 2:14 PM" over the minutes at the end of the line; then the kilometres and a dark square with the square of "stop" and no words. "From (address)" is no longer on it. Measured on an emulator at the usual font size: 132 dp at 411 and at 360 dp wide, where it was 218 dp without the address, 250 with it, and 267 on the narrow phone with an address of two lines. While it waits for the truck, 160 dp (179 narrow), where it was 250 (267).
- **Today's card has the month on its other side.** "also the one card that shows months mileage changes to today business mileage. can we have it when you click it it toggles betwwen days and month". A press turns it; two dots at its foot say which side is up; each new trip starts with Today.
- **The odometer is a row of wheels.** "i also want to make the odometer more prominent display the way it is activey live. maybe by color and or showing the 1/10 of km or by slowly turning the last km number"; of three drawings, "for the live odometer please use build 3" ("Dashboard wheels"). Each digit of the whole kilometres in a dark cell, one more cell for the tenth, lime while a trip moves the odometer, with a pulsing lime dot before "Odometer" and "+4.6 km this trip" under the wheels. The last wheel turns with the trip's distance, and a carry rolls the wheels before it.
- **Where it is.** New: `core/odometer/OdometerWheels.kt` (the figure to the tenth, and how far the last wheel has turned); in the design system `DashboardWheels.kt`, `WheelPositions.kt`, `LiveDot.kt`, `FlipTile.kt`, and `HeroIconButton` in `HeroTile.kt`; in `feature/home/` `HomeCardSide.kt`, `HomeOdometer.kt` and `HomeOdometerPreview.kt`. Changed: `HomeRecordingTiles.kt`, `HomeOdometerTile.kt`, `HomeTexts.kt`, `HomeIdleTiles.kt`, `HomeUi.kt`, `HomeViewModel.kt`, `HomeFigures.kt`, `TruckState.kt`, and one line of `app/MiloNavigation.kt`. **`core/odometer/Odometer.kt`, the odometer's storage, `data/trip/VehicleOdometers.kt` and Settings' odometer tile were not touched:** another session was changing them the same day.
- **Gone with the old tile:** the address of where the trip in progress started as something Home reads (`HomeUi.startAddress`, `startAddressOf`, `HomeSources.openTripStart`; the Trips screen still shows it), the design system's worded dark button `HeroButton`, and `FigureSize.HERO`.
- **Checks:** 2,621 unit tests (2,585 before; 38 new, 2 about the start address removed), `spotlessCheck`, `lintDebug`, `assembleDebug`, `tools/changes_page.py --check`.
- **Seen on an emulator** (Android 16; the phone was not touched): the tile in each state at 411 and 360 dp wide and at the usual and the largest font size, before and after side by side; the card turned to the month and back, kept through a visit to Trips, and Today again on the next trip; End on the new button; the wheels at rest, in miles, with two vehicles, with eight digits, and with animations off. The turning wheels are the next entry but one.

**`[DECISION]` What the drawings did not show, decided for Shawn**
Each is small and each can be changed in one place. He has seen none of them yet.
- **The tile says the wait for the truck under its two rows,** in the sentence Home always used. It comes in under them so that the button does not move under a thumb. Recorded with the truck or without it stays the small tile's word; a start that was refused has its own tile above, as before (`recordingNoteRes` in `TruckState.kt`, with a test over every state).
- **The button is a dark square of 56 dp with a light "stop",** the old button's colours. Larger than the 48 dp a finger needs, because it has no word to find it by and is pressed in a truck. It still ends the trip at the first press.
- **The minutes are as small as the "since" line over them,** a little bolder. A larger figure there made the first row higher than the pill, and the tile was to be low.
- **The kilometres are the 44 of Trips' month tile** (`FigureSize.TOTAL`), the design's own figure for an accent tile, where the old tile had 56.
- **With a large font the times stand under the pill, from the start of the line.** Beside it they would be squeezed.
- **The card keeps one height,** that of its higher side, so a press moves nothing else. With no Personal trip today a line's room is empty under today's figure, and the pair of tiles is 120 dp where it was 85. The other way, a card that grows by a line when it shows the month, would give those 20 dp back and make the rest of the screen jump at each press. One line in `FlipTile` decides it.
- **The month's side has no thin bar.** His words name three lines, and the bar would have made the card higher again.
- **The dots are in the middle of the card's foot,** as on a pager; the lit one lime, the other the grey of an outline that has to be seen.
- **The month stays up for the trip it was asked for in,** through a turn of the phone and a visit to another screen, and the next trip starts with Today (he was told this and did not object).
- **At rest the odometer keeps its cells.** The tenth's cell is grey with a dark digit, which is the lime cell without its colour, so that it is never read as one more digit of the kilometres; a lighter grey with a white digit was tried first and was too easy to read as "1,234,602 km". No dot, and the line about the reading under it. Before the first reading the sentence stays, with no wheels.
- **Several vehicles each have their name and their own wheels;** only the one the trip is in is lime and has the line about this trip. One dot before "Odometer" for the tile.
- **In miles the last wheel is a tenth of a mile.**
- **With animations off the digits stand** on their cells and change in one step; the cell is still lime.
- **The wheels never roll backwards and never spin.** When a trip ends, or the truck joins a trip late and all its kilometres come at once, or the figure moves by more than half a kilometre in one step, the wheels are set to the figure.

**`[DECISION]` Home's wheels and Settings' whole number: one figure, written two ways**
`odometerAt` rounds the reading and the trips since to the nearest whole kilometre, and Settings and the report print that. The wheels need a tenth. Two ways were weighed.
- **Chosen: the wheels show the same sum before the rounding.** 123,456 and 4.6 km are 123460 and a 6; Settings says 123,461. Settings' number is always the wheels' rounded, and it is one more than their dark digits whenever the tenth reads 5 to 9. It agrees with Settings' own second line ("Your reading of 123,456 km on …, plus 4.6 km of truck trips since"), with the line under the wheels, and with what Shawn was told: the tenth counts from the reading. Right after a reading is typed the wheels end in 0.
- **Not chosen: start the tenth at 5 with each reading.** Then the dark digits would equal Settings' number at every moment (that is what rounding to the nearest means: the reading is taken for the middle of its kilometre). But a reading he has just typed would show a 5 after it, and 123,456 and 4.6 km would read 123461 and a 1, which no sum on any screen explains.
- **What holds it:** `inTenths` in `core/odometer/OdometerWheels.kt` works the tenths out from the figure Settings prints, checks that they round to it, and falls back to the whole number with a tenth of nought if they ever do not. `OdometerWheelsTest` makes every figure with `odometerAt` itself, so a change of that rule fails the test rather than the screen.
- **Nothing the report prints changed,** and no line of `Odometer.kt`.

**`[FINDING]` The trip's distance arrives in uneven steps, and the wheels follow it on one soft spring**
The last wheel is placed by the trip's metres (`lastWheelTurn`), which reach Home when the trip rules count a step. Rule 3 of the distance calculation counts a step only once the truck is far enough from the last counted position (20 m with 5 m fixes), and a position that is not yet far enough counts with the next.
- **Measured** in a throwaway copy on an emulator, fed a position every 1.2 seconds at an even 50 km/h, with MilO reading one every 2: the wheels were handed a new figure 2, 2 and 4 seconds apart, over and over, in steps of 0.28, 0.28 and 0.42 of a digit.
- **A spring tuned to the time since the last step made it worse.** After a wait of 4 seconds it was soft for the next 2, and after 2 it was stiff for the next 4: the wheel crawled and jumped, and stood still for up to 3.4 seconds in a drive at an even speed. Rolling each step in the time the last one took did the same.
- **One fixed spring is what is built** (`ROLL_STIFFNESS`, 0.64, no bounce): about two and a half seconds behind the figure. On the same feed the wheel's speed mostly went between about half and one and a half times its mean, and about once a minute it stood still for a second or two, after a wait of 4 seconds for the next step (2.5 seconds in one film of 50 seconds, 1.2 in another). On a figure that arrives evenly (the preview's drive, a step a second) it took about six seconds to come up to speed from rest and then stayed within a tenth of its mean.
- **What the truck will show is not known.** At speed, with good GPS, every position counts and the steps are 2 seconds apart, which this spring smooths well; in slow traffic they are further apart. Device check HS-9.
- **A wheel that stands between two digits** is the same honesty: the truck stopped between two tenths. Device check HS-11 asks whether it reads as a fault.

**`[FINDING]` What it took to look at this on an emulator**
- **A trip without a truck moves no odometer,** by the rule (`truckSeen`), and an emulator has no truck. The real app therefore shows the wheels at rest during any trip there. To see them turn through the real trip controller, GPS and view model, a copy was built in which `TripActivity.tripSoFar()` says the truck was seen: one word changed, built, and put back before anything else was done (the script checks that the file is as committed). **That copy is in no commit.** The preview `HomeOdometerDrivesPreview` turns the wheels without it, on a scripted drive.
- **A second preview started with `am start` shows the first one again** when the preview is the task's first screen: the intent goes to the instance that is running. `am start --activity-clear-task -n com.shawnkowalchuk.milo/androidx.compose.ui.tooling.PreviewActivity …` starts each afresh. The odometer's previews are in `HomeOdometerPreviewKt`: `HomeOdometerDrivesPreview`, and `HomeOdometerPreview` with `--es parameterProviderClassName com.shawnkowalchuk.milo.feature.home.OdometerSamples --ei parameterProviderIndex N`, N from 0 to 5.
- **At the largest font size Android does not double large text.** The old tile's 56 sp figure grew by a few dp only; the small lines doubled. Android scales small text more than large since Android 14.
- **`FigureSize.TOTAL` drew over its neighbours when it wrapped.** It holds a figure to one line's height, and a figure that a very large font sent onto a second line (seen at 270 dp wide) was drawn over the rows above and below. It is now left as high as it is in that case. Trips' month tile had the same fault.
- **Seen in passing, and not part of this change:** at the largest font size on a phone 360 dp wide, the amber tile "Setup needs attention" is higher than the screen, because its words are squeezed into a column a few letters wide beside its button; and the small truck tile breaks "Connected" in the middle of the word. Both were so before.

**`[DEBT]` The rough edges of Home's three changes**
- **Whether the last wheel turns evenly in the truck is not known** (the finding above). One value, `ROLL_STIFFNESS`; ten minutes once HS-9 says which way to move it.
- **The turning wheels cannot be seen in the real app on an emulator** without the hand-made copy. A debug-only stand-in for the vehicle's connection would serve this and the marketing kit's "Truck · Not connected" pictures alike; it is a change to the app and its own decision. Half a day.
- **Two copies of "moves only while it is looked at".** `rememberInFront` in `LiveDot.kt` is new; `TruckLink` keeps the same few lines of its own from before, and was left alone so that this change did not touch the truck's tile. Tagged `TODO(debt)` there. Fifteen minutes.
- **Text styles that wait again:** `sideFigure` and Material's `displayLarge` are the old tile's, and no screen of MilO's own sets anything in them now. They are the design's, so they were kept, and said so in `Type.kt`.

**`[CHANGE]` A marketing kit: pictures and a video of MilO, in `marketing/`**
Shawn: "please create a bunch of different images for marketing for me from all source and keep them in github to use latter. screenshots and everything", and "create a video too please please make sure it shows the cool opening animation of mascot". New: the folder `marketing/` at the top of the repository, 108 pictures and five videos, 29 MB in all. Nothing under `app/` or `website/` changed, and neither build reads the folder. `marketing/README.md` lists every file with its size and its use.
- **What is in it.** For Google Play: eight phone screenshots of 1080 by 1920, each a real screen in a drawn phone under a headline, the feature graphic, the icon. For Product Hunt: six gallery pictures and the thumbnail. For social posts: three link pictures, six squares, three stories, a banner. The mascot in seven poses (standing, waving, thumbs-up, celebrate, flex, peace, rock on), each see-through at two sizes, on lime and on dark, and all on one sticker sheet. The mark and the name "MilO Trip Log" together, on dark, on lime and see-through, as SVG and PNG. 27 plain screenshots. The website's front page, a drawing of the Android Auto screen, and the sample report as a PDF. A promo video of 29 seconds, upright and lying, its first scene alone in both shapes, and that scene as a GIF.
- **One script makes the pictures again:** `python3 marketing/build.py`, under a minute.
