Two expectations changed, each because this entry changes exactly that: the test that pinned the notification inside the jump is replaced by the first of the new ones, which expects none, and the held backward change in `ClockWatchTest` has the alarms asked for at once. With each of the three changes taken out on purpose, 5, 8 and 3 tests fail.
- **The gate** (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes: 2,546 unit tests, 13 more than before. Device checks CJ-1, CJ-11 and CJ-13 expect the new lines; CJ-14 and CJ-15 are new. The branch `fix/clock-jump` is three commits now: the clock, its verification, and this one.

**`[FIX]` What the verification of MilO's clock found, and what was done**
Two independent verifiers examined the clock on 2026-10-09: one the clock and its wiring, one the trip engine, with Shawn's real days of 2026-10-05 to 2026-10-07 replayed and about 1,760 jumps of the date put into them. **No blocker.** With nobody touching the clock, MilO's time was the phone's at every reading. No jump changed a trip, a wait, the hold-off, a sound or a stored point. They found two risks, decided the same day (the two entries below), and five smaller points.
- **The list of changes said more than is true.** "The Log gets one line each time" is now "The Log gets a line when MilO sees the date change." A jump that no MilO process sees leaves no line, and neither does one whose process Android ends before the date is back. `website/changes.html` was written again. **Not done:** storing "set aside since, and by how much" beside the anchor so that the next process could write the line. It would write the anchor's file at every change, for a line whose use is to see the fix working.
- **The test that reads the source tree missed some readers.** `WallClockReadersTest` blanked everything inside quotation marks, and a reading inside a string's `${…}` with it. It now reads that code. It also knows the readers that do not say so: Android's `DateUtils`, `measureTimeMillis`, a file's date (`lastModified`, `getLastModifiedTime`) and a notification's `setWhen`. Three files use one of them, each once, and each is allowed with its reason: `CrashFileStore.kt`, `ReportFileStore.kt` and `TripNotifications.kt`. They are the three of the debt "What Android stamps itself" (2026-10-07). A second test makes sure that only `PhoneClock.kt` asks the phone for the time itself.
- **A cut-off anchor line read as an anchor with wrong numbers.** The line "1 7 1791419562000 1000000000" with its last six characters missing read as an anchor taken 10 seconds after boot. `ClockAnchorStore.read` now takes a line only with its line end, and a test cuts a line at every length. The writer could not leave such a line (a second file, forced to disk, renamed in one step); a damaged file system could.
- **A process stopped between the two readings of one look saw a change nobody made.** A reading asks for the time since boot and then for the phone's clock. Stopped for 3 hours exactly between the two, it answered a time 3 hours old and wrote a line about a clock "set 3 h 0 min ahead". The time since boot is now read once more after the phone's clock, and the pair is read again if it moved by more than a second, three times at most (`TrustedClockReadingTest`; two of its tests fail without it).
- **Dates and wording.** The documents said the clock was "put onto main on 2026-10-09". It is on the branch `fix/clock-jump`, on top of main as it stood that day, and reaches `main` when its pull request is merged. ADR-006, the encyclopedia, ARCHITECTURE and the device checks now say so. Since the entry "MilO's clock put onto today's main" below, the branch was moved once more, onto main with pull request #34 (the odometer that counts up), which added no reader of the clock. The branch is two commits: the clock, and this one.
- **Left as it is:** a log line written inside a jump can be stamped up to the size of the last network correction early (558 ms in the replay). Inside a jump MilO's time is the anchor's, and the anchor was taken before that correction. Trips, waits and stored points were identical.
- **In the tests.** `JumpingPhone`, the phone whose date a test sets, now starts with a proven anchor from an earlier process, as a phone on which MilO has been running; `miloHasRun = false` gives the first MilO process of a boot. Three expectations of the clock's own tests changed, each because a decision below changes exactly that: the anchor a first start stores carries the probation mark, and the line the check and the reminder write inside a jump says "asked to count" where it said "not asked for now". No other test's expectation changed. 43 tests are new: probation (19), the paired reading (4), the watch during probation (4), the new line (1), the anchor's file (2), the source-tree test (2), a start inside a jump with the real trip controller (2), and the alarms inside a jump (6 for the daily check, 3 for the reminder; one of each is the scene the verifier used to show the missing alarm).
- **The gate** (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes: 2,533 unit tests, 43 of them new. **When this was written nothing of the clock had run on the phone or on an emulator;** it ran on an emulator later the same day (the entry above). Device checks CJ-1 to CJ-13; CJ-11, CJ-12 and CJ-13 are new, and CJ-2 and CJ-8 expect the new lines.

**`[DECISION]` While the clocks disagree, the daily alarms count from the phone's boot** (ADR-006, "Decided on 2026-10-09", A)
- **What was found.** After the date went ahead MilO asked Android for no alarm, and relied on `TIME_SET` to ask again when the date came back. The verifier ran it with the real `ClockWatch`: date ahead, back after 9 seconds, no broadcast for the return, MilO's process frozen. After 48 hours neither alarm had been asked for. Android freezes a process with nothing running after about 10 seconds, and the watch's 30-second look is a wait inside that process. So a phone that does not deliver `TIME_SET` to a frozen or ended MilO had no daily check until MilO next started. The build before the clock at least asked for the day after tomorrow.
- **Decided.** While MilO's clock and the phone's disagree, the check and the reminder ask for the same alarm on the clock that counts from boot, due after the time that is really left (the real due time minus MilO's now): `ELAPSED_REALTIME_WAKEUP` for the daily check, `ELAPSED_REALTIME` for the monthly reminder. Inexact as before, no permission. It is the same request to Android (`PendingIntent`), so the normal request that follows when the clocks agree takes its place, and there is never more than one alarm.
- **Why there is still no loop.** Setting the date does not move that clock, so the alarm cannot go off at the jump. A test asks for it six times inside one jump; it is never due there.
- **In code.** `ReminderAlarm` and `NothingRecordedAlarm` have a second request, `setAfter(delayMs)`, beside `setFor(atMs)`. `ReminderAlarm` is no longer a one-function interface. The line in the log: "…the next daily look is asked for at 2026-10-08 12:00:00, or soon after it (the daily alarm). The phone's clock is set differently from MilO's own, so Android was asked to count 17 h 27 min from now and not to go by the phone's clock. It is asked in the normal way as soon as the two agree."
- **Tests** (`NothingRecordedAlarmInAJumpTest`, `MonthlyReminderAlarmInAJumpTest`): inside a jump one request that counts, for exactly the time left, and none for a time of day; after the return one normal request, which is the one Android then holds; and with MilO gone between the two broadcasts the counted alarm is what remains: not due at 11:59:59 of the real next day, due at 12:00:00, also across a second jump and across the night the clocks go back an hour.
- **What it costs.** Nothing that was seen. It departs from the first wording of ADR-006 ("does not ask Android for an alarm") and keeps its purpose.

**`[DECISION]` An anchor taken with nothing to check it against is on probation** (ADR-006, "Decided on 2026-10-09", B)
- **What was found.** A MilO process that starts with no usable anchor takes the phone's clock as it is: the first start after an install or an update, the first MilO process after a restart of the phone, an anchor file that is missing or cannot be read, and every start on a phone that gives no boot number. If that start fell into the seconds the date is ahead, MilO believed tomorrow and stored it. When the phone came back, MilO did not, and later processes inherited tomorrow from the file, until one of them had watched for ten minutes in a row. Both verifiers showed it. In one test MilO was still a day ahead after 3 days of looks every 3 minutes. In the other, with the real trip controller, the next morning's 15-minute drive was stored as starting on Sunday 06:00 and ending on Saturday 06:15, "saved as Personal: it started on a Sun", and the clock stepped back a day ten minutes into it. The build's own `TIME_SET` receiver makes such a start likely where Android starts MilO for the change of the date.
- **Decided.** Such an anchor is on probation. It is proven when the phone's clock agrees with it, to within 2 minutes, at a reading at least 2 minutes (`PROBATION_MS`) after it was taken: longer than any of his jumps. The mark is stored with the anchor, so a later process of the same boot knows. While an anchor is on probation, a phone's clock found more than 2 minutes BEHIND MilO's time is taken at once, as a new anchor that is on probation itself. His trick goes ahead and then back, so a clock that goes back says the anchor was taken inside a jump. A phone's clock that goes AHEAD is set aside as always, and a proven anchor is never taken back at once.
- **When it happens:** one line, "MilO started while the phone's date was set 24 h 0 min ahead, and took the phone's time when it came back.", and the same settling as after any change that came back: both daily alarms asked for again, both checks look again. The look takes away a "No trip recorded today" that was shown by the date and forgets the day stored for it.
- **Decided while building, beyond what was handed over:**
  - **While its anchor is on probation, MilO looks at the clock every 30 seconds by itself** (`ClockWatch`), as it does while it does not believe the phone. So a MilO that stays awake sees the date come back without `TIME_SET`, and an anchor that was right is proven at the first look two minutes or more after it was taken, and the looking stops. **Corrected after the emulator run (the entry above):** the look is a wait inside MilO's process and does not run while Android has the process frozen, which it does within seconds to a MilO it started and left alone. Such a MilO is woken by its daily alarms, which are asked for two minutes ahead while the probation lasts.
  - **A change that is followed after its ten watched minutes leaves a proven anchor.** Ten minutes of watching is more than probation asks.
  - **A second change seen during probation, before the step back, is told of first** ("MilO saw it changed again … later"), then the step back.
  - **The line gives no length of time.** The process that took the date may be gone; the next one writes the line at its first reading and only knows that the phone's clock is back.
  - **The anchor's file has a fifth word,** `proven` or the time since boot at which the probation began. The layout number stays 1: no build with the four-word line was ever installed.
  - **`ClockAnchor` and `ClockNews` moved into files of their own,** to keep `TrustedClock.kt` under the 300 lines of STANDARDS §3.
- **Tests** (`TrustedClockProbationTest`, `ClockWatchProbationTest`, `ClockJumpFirstStartTest`): probation ends only by an agreeing reading two minutes on, not by time alone and not by a reading that disagrees; a step back during probation is taken at once and a step ahead is not; a proven anchor is never taken back at once; the mark survives a start of MilO and does not survive a restart of the phone; his trick one minute after a start with no anchor leaves every reading on the true time; a start inside the jump is on the true time at the first reading after the phone is back, also when the process that took the date is gone by then; with a proven anchor no reading goes backwards through 4,000 random steps of the phone's clock far ahead and far back. With the real trip controller, the verifier's scene now gives one trip, dated when it was driven, Business, and the clock does not step during it. With the rule taken out on purpose, 11 of these tests fail.

**`[DEBT]` A MilO that starts with no anchor inside a jump goes by the date until the date is back**
What probation does not cure. For those seconds, 5 to 16 with his trick, such a MilO is as every MilO was before the clock: log lines are dated tomorrow and stay so, a trip that starts in exactly those seconds is dated tomorrow, and a trip that is open can be closed by the parked rule. **Paid in part after the emulator run (the entry at the top):** it shows no "No trip recorded today" and no reminder in those seconds, stores no day for one, and asks for no alarm by that date. **Seen on the emulator:** the lines dated tomorrow, 14 of them, stand at the top of the Log, above today's newer lines and above the line that explains them, until that time tomorrow has passed. What was stamped stays. **How narrow it is:** it needs a start with no usable anchor to fall into those seconds. That is the first start after this update (install with the date right), an anchor file that cannot be read, or the first MilO process after a restart of the phone; MilO is normally started at the restart itself, long before the trick. On a phone that gives no boot number it would be every start inside a jump (CJ-1). And a date left ahead for 2 minutes or more after such a start is believed. Cost to fix: hold back everything that stamps a time until the first anchor is proven, or check the first anchor against Android's network time where it has one; neither was decided. Tagged in `TrustedClock.kt`. Device check CJ-11.

**`[DEBT]` The probation has one known gap: two jumps with no reading between them**
MilO starts with no anchor inside one jump, no MilO process reads the clock until a later jump, and a reading falls into that one. The two readings agree and lie more than 2 minutes apart, so the anchor counts as proven with tomorrow's date. MilO is then a day ahead, in later processes too, until one has watched for ten minutes: the fault the probation was decided against. It needs Android to start MilO for both jumps and to tell it of neither return, and no other reading of the time in between; the 30-second look closes it for a MilO that stays awake. **Narrower since the emulator run (the entry at the top):** two minutes after the first start an alarm now wakes MilO, which is a reading between the two jumps. The gap is left only where Android does not deliver that alarm. It was found while building and is the rule as decided, so it was reported and not redesigned. Pinned by a test ("an anchor taken inside one jump and next read inside a later one passes for proven"). Cost to fix: prove an anchor only by agreement that is seen without a gap, as a held change is; a MilO that sleeps would then stay on probation for longer. Tagged in `TrustedClock.kt`. Device check CJ-13.

**`[DEBT]` The clock's debts of 2026-10-07, as they stand today**
- **Paid:** "An alarm delivered at the jump can stay missing until MilO next starts". The alarm now counts from boot while the clocks disagree (the decision above). Whether HyperOS delivers `TIME_SET` to a MilO that is not running (CJ-8) no longer decides whether the daily check comes; CJ-12 is the check for it on the phone.
- **Open, unchanged:** "A change of the clock that is meant is followed late, and with a jump". One sentence of it is no longer true: until such a change is followed the daily alarms are asked for after all, on the clock that counts from boot, and come by MilO's time. **Seen on the emulator (the entry at the top):** a MilO that is idle in the background does not follow at all until it is opened or records, because Android freezes it; and when MilO follows with its screen open, the header and Home show the old day until MilO is brought to the front again.
- **Open, unchanged:** "What Android stamps itself still goes by the phone's clock". Its three places are now named in `WallClockReadersTest`, each with its reason.
- **Open, changed in one point:** "What the evenings before left on the phone is not all put right". Its last sentence, about the first start after the update and after a restart of the phone, is now the first debt above: MilO no longer stays a day ahead.
- **New, small:** a jump that no MilO process saw, or whose process was ended before the date came back, leaves no line in the Log. The one line is how the fix is seen working, so on a phone that does not start MilO for `TIME_SET` some jumps will show nothing. Cost to fix: store what was set aside beside the anchor.

**`[DECISION]` `main` requires the CI check before a merge, and auto-merge is allowed**
Shawn, of the odometer's pull request (#34): "merge it when ci passes". GitHub refused: "Auto merge is not allowed for this repository", and with no rule on `main` there was nothing for an auto-merge to wait for. Asked how to go on, he chose "Change the repo settings". Done the same hour through GitHub's API: (1) the repository's "Allow auto-merge" is on; (2) a branch protection rule on `main` requires the check "Check, test and build" from GitHub Actions (app 15368), not "up to date with `main`", no reviews, and it holds for administrators. With that GitHub also refuses a direct push, a force push and a deletion of `main`; since the very first commit nothing but pull requests has reached it. **This is STANDARDS §14's "Red build = no merge", now kept by GitHub and no longer by hand.** The rule names the CI job, so a renamed job must be renamed in the rule too, or nothing merges. Both are undone in the repository's settings (General, and Branches).

**`[CHANGE]` The odometer counts up as the trip is driven, on Home and in Settings**
Shawn: "can we have the mileage on the home screen change as we drive please". Asked which figure, he chose the odometer (the trip's own kilometres on the lime tile already followed the drive); asked which screens, phone only; asked whether Settings' odometer tile should count with it, "Yes, both together".
- **How.** `TripActivity.tripSoFar()` hands the open trip over as plain values (`TripSoFar`, in `data/trip/TruckTrips.kt`), `drivenTrips` adds it to the finished trips, and `vehicleOdometers` passes it down, so the one function both tiles ask still gives both the same figure. `HomeViewModel` and `OdometerViewModel` each follow `TripController.activity` for it. `CurrentTrip` has one more field, `truckSeen`, the trip rules' own `ActiveTrip.truckSeen`.
- **The rules are the finished trip's.** Nothing is added before a paired vehicle has been seen in the trip, so a trip by button counts from the moment the truck joins it, all of its kilometres at once. The trip goes to the vehicle it names, or to the first one. It counts by when it started, so a reading typed during the trip already holds it, and the odometer then stands at that reading until the next trip.
- **Never twice.** The trip worker writes a trip's end to storage before it publishes the new `TripActivity` (`TripWorker.handle`: `carryOut`, then `publish`), so for a moment the finished row and the trip "in progress" can both be there. `drivenTrips` leaves the trip in progress out once the list holds its id as ended.
- **It can step back at a trip's end.** The final distance is worked out again without what came after the truck disconnected, and a discarded or deleted trip takes its kilometres off again. Nothing smooths that over: the figure after the end is the true one.
- **Not changed:** the Android Auto screen and the home-screen widget (neither shows an odometer), the report (finished trips only), "Today · business" and the month (a trip is sorted when it closes), and every word on the screens. One line in version 0.2.0.
- **Checked on Shawn's Windows PC** (next entry), with the throwaway debug key: `spotlessCheck`, `lintDebug` and `assembleDebug` pass, and of `testDebugUnitTest`'s 2,362 tests the 35 that fail there are storage tests this change does not touch. The new ones pass: `VehicleOdometersTest`, and the added cases of `TruckTripsTest` and `TripActivityTest`. **CI passed on the pull request,** its unit tests included, so those 35 are the PC's and not the code's. **Not drawn anywhere, and not driven:** device checks HM-8 to HM-12.

**`[FINDING]` MilO builds on the Windows PC, and 35 storage tests fail there**
The first build of MilO on a Windows PC (the Mac has the key; a cloud session has neither). What it took:
- **A JDK.** `JAVA_HOME` pointed at `C:\Program Files\Android\Android Studio\jbr`, which does not exist. A JDK 17 that Gradle had fetched for another project is in `~/.gradle/jdks/`, and `JAVA_HOME` was set to it for each command. Gradle fetched its 9.8.0 itself, and the Android Gradle plugin fetched SDK platform 37 (only 36 was installed).
- **`-Pmilo.signing.debugKey=true` on every command:** the PC has no keystore. What it builds carries the throwaway debug key and must never go on the phone (STANDARDS §12).
- **35 of 2,362 unit tests fail on Windows, for two reasons that are Windows' own.** 34 are the settings file's tests (`SettingsStoreTest`, `ReportSettingsStoreTest`, `NothingRecordedStorageTest`, `ReminderSettingsStoreTest`, `OnboardingStorageTest`, `WidgetStorageTest`): DataStore throws "Unable to rename … settings.preferences_pb.tmp", because Windows will not rename over a file that a second store still holds open, as the tests that "survive the process" do. One is `CrashFileStoreTest`, "a written crash is read back exactly": a stack trace is written with Windows' line ends (CR LF) and does not compare equal after it is read back. **CI on Linux stays the authority for the test run;** a red `testDebugUnitTest` on the PC means one of these until they are made to pass there. Not fixed here: not this change's.
- **`python tools/changes_page.py` writes `website/changes.html` with CR LF on Windows** (`Path.write_text` translates line ends). `--check` passes all the same and git stores LF (`.gitattributes`); the working copy was put back to LF by hand. The script's "writes the same bytes every time" holds on one system only; `newline="\n"` in its `write_text` would make it true everywhere. Not changed here.

**`[CHANGE]` Home's truck tile is lower: the "Slim" form**
Shawn: "on the home screen can we please make the connect to vehicle more compact. i would like it not as tall". He was shown the tile as it was and three lower forms drawn at real size, and chose A, "Slim": the same drawing of the phone, the line and the truck, smaller and still as wide as the tile, with its movement unchanged. Phone only. The small "Truck · Connected" tile of a trip in progress is another tile and is as it was. No dependency, no setting, and nothing under `platform/` or `core/trip/` was touched. **Built, not yet proven on the phone:** device check TT-1.
- **Heights, before and after,** measured on an emulator set to a phone 360 dp wide, at the usual font size:

  | State | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Checking | 152 dp | 104 dp |
  | No truck paired | 168 | 104 |
  | Not connected, Setup in order | 168 | 104 |
  | Not connected, Setup needs attention | 168 | 104 |
  | Connected, after End | 183 | 120 |
  | Connected, no trip otherwise | 168 | 104 |
  | Connected and parked | 183 | 104 |
  | Connected, MilO no longer watching (its title takes two lines) | 187 | 139 |
  | "Connecting…" | 152 | 104 |
  | "Connected", "Trip recording started" | not measured (one line, as "Connecting…") | 104 |

  At 411 dp wide the new heights are the same; the old "Not connected" was 152 there. On Shawn's phone, 393 dp wide, the parked tile was about 183 dp high (his screenshot of 2026-10-08).
- **What the tile shows.** The title, with the vehicle's name small at the end of the same line (light while connected, grey otherwise). One short sentence. The drawing. The corner word "Truck" and the two labels under the drawing are gone, and the string "Your phone" is deleted. With no vehicle paired nothing stands where the name would; while MilO is still reading, only a name it has read already; a vehicle without a name is "The truck" (`vehicleShown` in `TruckState.kt`).
- **Sizes.** 14 dp above and below (16 before), 18 at the sides, 2 between title and sentence, 10 between the sentence and the drawing. All are spacing steps that existed, and **no token was added.** The drawing's two ends are 30 dp across (the design has 48), the mark 24 (36), the icons 13, 14 and 11 (20, 22, 16), the solid line 3 dp (4), a ring's line and the mark's outline 1.5 (2). The dashes stay 2 dp thick and 6 long, so they run at the design's speed.
- **The movement is not changed.** `TruckLinkMotion.kt` was not touched, and `TruckLinkMotionTest` passes as it was. A ring is sized by the patch it spreads from, so it is smaller with its patch. The arrival that Home plays was not touched. Filmed at five pictures a second, "Connecting…" was on 13 of them (2.6 seconds) and "Connected" on 8 (its 1.2 seconds and the fade to the trip).
- **`[FINDING]` Most of the old height was the sentence, and part of that was the layout.** The sentence stood beside the corner word, in a column 46 dp narrower than the tile's inside. So "A trip starts by itself when the truck connects" took two lines on a phone 360 dp wide. It now has the whole width and takes one, unchanged.
- **Six sentences were shortened,** so that each takes one line on a phone 360 dp wide, or two where one cannot say it:
  - no truck: "Tap here to pair the truck, so that a trip can start by itself when it connects." became "Tap to pair one, so a trip can start by itself."
  - Setup needs attention: "A trip may not start by itself when the truck connects. Setup says why." became "A trip may not start by itself. Setup says why."
  - after End: "… after the truck disconnects and connects again." became "You ended the trip. The next one starts by itself after the truck reconnects." (two lines)
  - connected, no trip: "No trip is being recorded. Press Start trip to record one." became "No trip is being recorded. Press Start trip."
  - parked: "The last trip ended where the truck stopped. A new trip starts by itself when the truck moves." became "A trip starts by itself when the truck moves". The first half is no longer said.
  - no longer watched: "The truck has not moved for a long time. …" became "It has not moved for a long time. Press Start trip before you drive off." (two lines)

  "Reading the truck's connection", "Found the truck over Bluetooth" and "Trip recording started" are unchanged. **No string that another place uses was changed:** the two parked titles, which the notification uses too, and "Trip in progress. Waiting for the truck to reconnect" are as they were.
- **`[DECISION]` The title comes before the name.** The title stays on one line whenever that leaves the name room for about five letters; a longer name is cut with "…". A title that cannot do that goes onto a second line, and the name may then take up to two fifths of the line, unless that costs the title one more line. On a phone 360 dp wide, "Truck connected and parked" leaves the name 57 dp: "F-150" (34 dp) fits whole, and a name as long as "Work truck" (about 69 dp) is cut. On Shawn's phone, 393 dp wide, it leaves 90 dp.
- **`[FINDING]` Two vehicles.** With two paired, the tile names the one that is connected or in the trip, and the first one paired while neither is, as before this change. A test now holds that (`HomeUiTest`). The sentences say "the truck" for whichever it is. Not changed here. Settings writes "Work truck + 1 more" for the same thing, and Home could; that is Shawn's to ask for.
- **A screen reader** is read the tile as one thing: the title, the name, the sentence. Until now each text was a stop of its own. Not tried with a screen reader.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), as previews of the debug build; never on the phone, never beside a truck.** Every state at 360 and at 411 dp wide. Font sizes 1.3 and 2.0 at 360 dp: every word there, nothing cut off or overlapping. The two moving looks filmed for three seconds each: the dashes, the mark and the rings moved, and no ring was cut by the tile's edge. 44 to 70 frames changed in a film while a look moved, for the old tile and the new alike (the Mac was busy with other builds), and 1 or 2 with the tile scrolled out of view or the phone's animations off.
- **Seen in passing, not changed.** At font size 2.0 on a phone 360 dp wide, the amber "Setup needs attention" tile breaks words in the middle ("attent" and "ion"). It did so before this change.
- **Tests and gate.** Four new unit tests: three in `TruckStateTest` for what stands where the vehicle is named, one in `HomeUiTest` for two vehicles. Every test of which state is shown when is as it was. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (Home's step 5 with the table, and `TruckTile` under "Design system"), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (TT-1 added; the sentences in NL-22 to NL-26 and NL-36 to NL-38 corrected), one line in version 0.2.0 of the list of changes.

**`[CHANGE]` MilO's clock put onto today's main**
The clock of 2026-10-07 (night), below, was built as one commit on main as it was that night. Main has had pull requests #25 to #32 since. On 2026-10-09 the commit was moved on top of that day's main, on its branch `fix/clock-jump`, and what main had added was brought under the clock. It is not on `main` until its pull request is merged.
- **Seven readers of the phone's clock that main had added are handed MilO's clock:** the Trips screen's three (`app/TripsEntry.kt`: its ViewModel, its corrections and the label's ViewModel), the first start's ViewModel (`app/OnboardingEntry.kt`), What's new's two (`app/WhatsNewObjects.kt`), and the catch-up that gives earlier trips the truck's address (`AppContainer`). An eighth was a default: `buildShownUnit` (`data/settings/UnitStorage.kt`) took the phone's clock when it was handed none; it has no default now and is handed the container's. They were found with the patterns of `WallClockReadersTest`, which passes again with its one allowed file. The test has one more pattern since today: Kotlin's own clock, `Clock.System`, which it did not know.
- **Main's wiring kept whole.** `MiloApplication` still makes the clock first. `MiloApp` provides `LocalMiloClock` around everything it draws, the first start's page and What's new included; what it drew itself is now `MiloScreens`. The anchor's file is in `no_backup/`, which main's backup rules leave out as before.
- **Main's new features were read for anything that measures a time, remembers a day or sets an alarm. None needs a rule changed.**
  - **The trip-start sound** (when the truck drives off) is decided by a fix's speed reading, or by distance over the time since boot. No time of day is compared.
  - **Several vehicles** have no hold-off, wait or limit of their own. The vehicle a trip records is the one last seen connected, held in memory. The time a vehicle was paired at, which orders the list, and the time of an odometer reading come from the clock that is handed in.
  - **The first start** remembers a stage and **What's new** a version. Neither remembers a day.
  - **Home's odometers and today's trips** get "now" from Home's ViewModel, which is handed the clock.
  - Main added no alarm, and changed nothing in the daily check, the monthly reminder, the driving alert or the start-up diagnostics.
- **The ADR is ADR-006.** It was written as ADR-003; main took 003 for the website and 004 for Google Play. The file is `docs/adr/ADR-006-trusted-clock.md`, and every mention in the documents and in code comments was changed. "ADR-003" now means the website everywhere.
- **Tests.** The clock-jump fixtures were brought to main's signatures: the stand-in service's `record(checkAtMs, sounds)`, and the unit a form and the widget are given. No assertion was taken out or loosened. Four scenes now also assert the two sounds: the control drive, the drive through a jump and the trip that starts inside one each have one connect sound and one trip-start sound; a jump beside the parked truck plays nothing, and the drive-off after it has its trip-start sound and no connect sound.
- **The list of changes** has one line for it under 0.2.0 ("Fixed"), and `website/changes.html` was written again.
- **The gate** (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes on today's main with the clock: 2,482 unit tests.
- **Still so:** nothing of the clock has run on the phone or on an emulator. Device checks CJ-1 to CJ-10; CJ-4, CJ-6 and CJ-7 now name the two sounds.

**`[FINDING]` Google Play made its own signing key for MilO when the app was created**
Shawn downloaded three certificates from the Play Console: `deployment_cert.der`, `hybrid_classical_cert.der` and `hybrid_pqc_cert.der`. Read here with `openssl`: all three are Google's ("CN=Android, OU=Android, O=Google Inc., Mountain View"), made at 04:22:11 UTC on 2026-10-09, about when the app was created, and valid for 30 years. The deployment and the hybrid classical ones are RSA 4096 (SHA-256 `0d856597…7bb1d4fc` and `72118254…a822534c`); the third is post-quantum, ML-DSA-65 (OID 2.16.840.1.101.3.4.3.18). **None of them is MilO's key** (`95a95107…6e9414b4`, ADR-004). A copy from Google Play signed with them could never update the phone's MilO. They are public certificates and hold no secret. **Changed the same morning, before any upload:** Protected with Play, App signing, Change key ("Change app signing key": its warnings about testers and uploaded versions did not apply), "Export and upload a key from Java keystore", `pepk.jar` run on the Mac against `~/keys/milo.jks` (alias `milo`, the password pasted from the Keychain), the encrypted zip uploaded and then deleted, no separate upload key. **The Console now shows the app signing key "In use" with SHA-256 `95:A9:51:07:…:94:14:B4`,** and the upload key certificate with the same SHA-256, SHA-1 `E3:58:80:FA:…:7F:0A` and MD5 `4D:E7:68:85:…:B3:46`, all three checked against the 0.1.0 APK's signing block. Google's quantum-ready (beta) key is no longer used.

**`[CHANGE]` Home: each vehicle's odometer, every trip of today, and no "Home" in the top line**
Shawn: "i want the milage on the home screen. and i also want to remove the home top right corner. also can we make the last trip on the home screen to show all trips that day one after each other ... just have todays trips sorted latest at the top". Asked which mileage, he chose "Odometer reading"; for the corner, "Just the word Home"; phone only.
- **The odometer tile** stands under the truck's tile with no trip open, and under the truck and today pair while one is recorded. **The figure is computed once for both screens:** the per-vehicle work moved from Settings' `OdometerViewModel` to `data/trip/VehicleOdometers.kt` (`odometerNow`, `vehicleOdometers`, `VehicleOdometer.shownName`), because Home may not import Settings (STANDARDS §3). Settings' tiles call it unchanged in behaviour; `OdometerCardStateTest` still checks them. Home reads every finished trip for it.
- **Today's trips** replace "Last trip": the recording layout's list (`TodayListTile`, now in `HomeTodayTile.kt`) stands in both layouts, without its note about the trip in progress when none is. `HomeTrips.lastTrip` and the words "Last trip" and "All 4 today" are gone; the tests that read the newest trip read the first row.
- **`AppHeader` takes `titleShown`;** Home passes false. The bottom bar's lit Home button says which screen it is.
- Two lines in version 0.2.0. Checked by the existing unit tests, run in CI. **Not drawn anywhere:** device checks HM-1 to HM-7.

**`[DECISION]` Google Play as a second channel, with the phone's own key (ADR-004)**
Shawn: "i may want to add this app to the play store eventually please walk me through the steps". He created the app in his organisation's Play Console (2795748 Alberta Ltd.) as "MilO Trip Log", `com.shawnkowalchuk.milo`, English (Canada), free, and was told to turn automatic protection off: it sends anyone with a copy from elsewhere to Google Play, which the GitHub APK would trip over. Then: "yes start working on the changes and i want the android auto screen". **Play App Signing is to use `milo.jks` itself,** uploaded at the first upload, so that Google Play's copy, the GitHub APK and the phone's MilO update each other; a key made by Google would cut the phone off. One build for both channels, no flavours. The Android Auto screen stays, declared as IoT. Asked whether to answer "Yes" to ads now and add AdMob later: "No" now, changed before an update with ads; ads would bring the internet permission and end the "nothing leaves the phone" promise, so they get their own ADR.

**`[FINDING]` What Google Play would refuse in MilO as it was**
Read on 2026-10-09 from Google's pages where they could be fetched, otherwise from developers' accounts of reviews: (1) background location needs a disclosure in the app before the request, with the words "even when the app is closed or not in use", and a declaration with a video; (2) `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` is for a short list of kinds of app, and a mileage logger is not clearly one; (3) a donation link to anyone but a tax-exempt organisation breaks the payments policy, and apps have been removed for one (WireGuard 2019, AnkiDroid 2026); (4) the Car App Library is open to navigation, point-of-interest, IoT, VoIP and weather apps, and MilO's trip screen fits none well. The manifest itself said "Google Play restricts it" beside the battery permission. **A copy from Google Play is the trusted-store install the Android Auto screen needs in a car** (ARCHITECTURE, "Distribution risk"), so Play is also the way the screen may finally show in the truck.

**`[CHANGE]` Ready for Google Play: the location question, the battery list, the coffee tile, and the first start's buttons in view**
- **"MilO uses your location"** (`feature/setup/LocationDisclosure.kt`) is shown at every press of Setup's "Allow all the time" button, before Android is asked: what is collected and why, even when the app is closed or not in use, that it stays on the phone, and that the two ends of a trip go to the phone's address lookup (the privacy page's own words). Android is asked only after Continue. It is the design system's `ConfirmDialog`. `SetupFix.AskPermission.locationDisclosure` marks the one row that needs it, and a test checks that no other row has it.
- **`REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` is gone** (ADR-002, amendment 36). The battery row opens Android's list of every app's battery optimisation, and its sentence says how to find MilO there. MilO's App info stays the fallback. The exemption the phone has stays.
- **The Buy me a coffee tile is hidden in a copy installed from Google Play:** `platform/system/InstallSource.kt` asks Android for the installer of record, once, in `MiloNavigation`, and Settings is handed `showCoffee`. The website's button and the privacy page stay, the latter with a line that Google Play's copy has no tile.
- **The first start's main buttons stay in view** (Shawn, while this was being built: "you might as well make it when the onboarding to pin the bottom where it says click next or continue so it shows all the time"). A new design-system layout, `PinnedButtonColumn`: the tiles scroll above a button that stays at the bottom of the screen. The welcome page's OK and Setup's "Done, go to Settings" (only during the first start) use it. Setup opened at any other time scrolls as before.
- **Version 0.2.0** is opened in the list of changes (three lines) with `versionName` "0.2.0" and `versionCode` 2. README has "Publishing on Google Play", with the signing step first; STANDARDS §1, §12 and §14, ARCHITECTURE and the encyclopedia say the same.
- **Checked:** the Setup rules, the battery screen list and the installer rule in unit tests, run in CI. **Nothing has been drawn on an emulator or run on the phone:** device checks GP-1 to GP-8, and OB-1, OB-3, OB-4 and OB-7 now expect the buttons in view.

**`[CHANGE]` The first release, 0.1.0, is on GitHub**
Shawn built it with `assembleRelease` on the Mac, installed it over the phone's MilO (the trips stayed) and published `v0.1.0` on `d7aa4fd`, with `MilO-0.1.0.apk` (38,883,509 bytes, SHA-256 `179425b0…c00c0b`) and the eleven lines of 0.1.0 as its notes. The APK was downloaded from the release afterwards and its signing block read: one v2 signer, certificate SHA-256 `95a951079ea89ce645392c404cccacc5e42aba781ba52efe7afb27dd6e9414b4`, the key of the phone's builds. README "Making a release" now gives that digest to check each release against, and installs on the phone with `adb -s`.

**`[FINDING]` `installRelease` installs on every device adb sees, emulators too**
The first try, `./gradlew installRelease`, failed with `INSTALL_FAILED_UPDATE_INCOMPATIBLE: Existing package com.shawnkowalchuk.milo signatures do not match`. It was not the phone: the only MilO attached was on `emulator-5680`, left running by earlier test work, signed with the throwaway debug key (certificate SHA-256 `9254dbbd…d1f9b4`); the phone was not connected. Comparing the certificates of the installed APK (pulled with `adb pull`) and the new one showed it before anything was installed. **Install a release on the phone by its serial, never with `installRelease`, and never uninstall to get past this error:** on the phone it would mean the key changed, and uninstalling deletes every trip.

**`[CHANGE]` Version 0.1.0 dated: the first release**
Shawn asked for the APK to be built and put on GitHub. Version 0.1.0 is dated 2026-10-09 in `changelog.json`, and the What's new page was written again, as README "Making a release" step 2 says. The APK itself is built with `assembleRelease` on the Mac and uploaded by hand (step 3 and 4): a cloud session has neither the key nor the Keychain, and an APK signed with the debug key could not update the phone's MilO. **The next change Shawn will notice opens version 0.2.0** (`versionName` "0.2.0", `versionCode` 2).

### 2026-10-08

**`[DECISION]` Several vehicles: the truck stays where it was, and the others are a list beside it**
Shawn: "can we add the option to link multiple vehicles to blutooth". His answers: several work vehicles; each trip records which one it was in; an odometer for each; the phone only. **The first vehicle is the truck, under the keys it always had** (`truck_address`, `truck_name`, `truck_association_id`), and the others are a new string set, `more_vehicles`. Moving every vehicle into one new list was the other way, and was not taken: every path that knew one truck (Setup, Home, the reconcile, the export, the pairing check, Android Auto's status) would have changed at once, for a phone that in most cases has one vehicle. Removing the first vehicle moves the next one into the old keys, so they always hold a vehicle while any is paired. **A trip or a reading with no vehicle counts for the first one,** so nothing recorded before needs a vehicle to be counted; the trips and the readings from before are still given the truck's address once (`TripVehicleCatchUp`), so that removing the truck later does not hand them to the next vehicle.

**`[CHANGE]` Several vehicles: pair each, any of them starts a trip, each trip records its vehicle, an odometer for each**
- **Pairing** ("Vehicles"): Add pairs one more; Remove, after a question, removes the vehicle and its association. After a pairing, associations that are no vehicle's newest are removed.
- **Detection:** the receiver and the companion service match every paired vehicle and name it; the reading asks about every address; the pairing check watches each; Setup names a vehicle Android does not watch. **A disconnect of another vehicle than the one last seen connected is read, not trusted**, so leaving the van's Bluetooth range while in the truck does not end the truck's trip.
- **Each trip records its vehicle** (database version 7, `trips.vehicleAddress`), once, while it is open. Export format 3 carries it.
- **Odometer:** a reading names its vehicle ("time:km|address"); Settings has a tile per vehicle; the report prints each vehicle's odometer, and the vehicle under a trip where the period had more than one; the CSV has a Vehicle column.
- **With one vehicle nothing looks different.**
- **Checked:** the trip rules, the signals, pairing, the odometer and the report in unit tests (the odometer and report tests, 527, also in a scratch Gradle project here), and the rest in CI. **Nothing has run on an emulator or on the phone:** device checks MV-1 to MV-12.

**`[DEBT]` A change of vehicle within the grace period keeps the trip in the first vehicle**
A trip records its vehicle once (`TripLedger.noteVehicle`). Leaving the truck and driving off in the van within the two minutes of grace continues the trip, as it always did, and the whole trip is the truck's: the van's odometer misses it. To fix: end the trip and start another when a connect names another vehicle during the grace period. Rare for one driver; left until it is seen.

**`[DEBT]` A trip added by hand names no vehicle**
The edit screen has no choice of vehicle, so a trip typed in has none and counts for the first vehicle's odometer (`TripRepository.addByHand`). With one vehicle that is right. To fix: a choice of the paired vehicles on the edit screen, shown only when there are several.

**`[DEBT]` An export carries the first vehicle only**
`TransferredSettings` has one truck, as before; the vehicles beside it stay as the importing phone has them. The trips in the file name their vehicles either way. To fix: a list of vehicles in the settings of the file, which is a new export format.

**`[DEBT]` Three files are further over the 300-line guideline**
`TripWorker.kt` (362 to 380 lines), `TripLedger.kt` (325 to 351) and `AppContainer.kt` (316 to 329) were over it before and grew with the vehicles. `MiloNavigation.kt` went down to 267: the Trips screen's entry moved to `app/TripsEntry.kt`. To fix: split each by its parts (the worker's vehicle and parked work, the ledger's vehicle and grace notes), in a change of its own.

**`[FINDING]` The schema of database version 7 was taken from CI, and matched**
This session has no Android SDK (Google's Maven is blocked), so Room's KSP could not export `app/schemas/.../7.json`. The file was written by hand from 6.json and the two new columns, with the identity hash Room printed in a CI run, and a temporary CI step compared Room's own export with it. The next run showed no difference, and the step came out again. The migration test now counts the two columns of the step to version 7, and checks its two statements.

**`[FINDING]` Kotlin 2.4 smart-casts after a `when` on `x?.property`, and a safe call after it is a warning**
`when (status?.state)` followed by `status?.missing` failed the build: with warnings as errors, the needless `?.` on a value Kotlin now knows is not null is fatal. Written `status.missing`.

**`[DECISION]` Trip labels: suggested by where the trip ended, only suggested, printed as the purpose**
Shawn: "add the ability to add a label for each trip pick from a list of previously used ones. have them from a gps quaridinate so that if im at work and labeled work before that is the one that is choosen from the list". His answers: the place is where the trip **ends**; within **200 m**; the label is **only suggested**, never set by itself; the report prints it **as a column**; the phone only. **The suggestion is the newest label used within 200 m,** not the most used, so a place that changed its name follows at once. It is chosen in the dialog already, which is how "that is the one that is chosen from the list" is met while Save is still his press. The report's "column" is a grey line under the trip's addresses on the PDF (the page has no room for another column beside the addresses), and a Purpose column in the CSV.

**`[CHANGE]` Trip labels**
A pressed trip has a Label button (Edit, Label, the other category, Delete: two lines of buttons). It opens a dialog: a sentence, the labels used before as choice buttons with the suggestion first and chosen, "No label" for a trip that has one, and a field for a new label; Save writes the typed label, or else the chosen one. Labels that differ only in capitals are one. One event-log line for each save. The trip's grey line shows the label after the times. `core/trip/TripLabels.kt` (pure, 7 tests, also run here), `TripLabelViewModel` (7 tests, CI) and `LabelDialog` in `feature/trips/`; the view model is its own, because `TripsViewModel` is at its size limit. **Nothing has run on an emulator or on the phone:** device checks LB-1 to LB-8.

The labels were to be chips (`ChipChoice`), but an unchosen chip is the colour of a tile, and a dialog is a tile: they would not be seen. The dialog uses the edit screen's choice buttons (`ChoiceButton`, made for a tile) in a column instead. No new component.

**`[DECISION]` Buy me a coffee: a tile that opens the browser, and a button the website draws itself**
Shawn: "can we add a buy me a coffee link in app and on the website. buymeacoffee.com/SeaWingman", with Buy Me a Coffee's button code, an image from img.buymeacoffee.com. **The website does not use that image:** its Content-Security-Policy allows images from the site only, and the privacy policy says the site loads nothing from other sites. The button is drawn in the site's CSS instead, in Buy Me a Coffee's yellow (`--coffee`, the one colour on the site outside its palette, for this button alone). **The app opens the page in the browser** (`ACTION_VIEW`), so it still has no internet permission and sends nothing with the press. Phone only.

**`[CHANGE]` Buy me a coffee in Settings and on the website**
Settings: a tile above Version, drawn as Version is, that opens the page; a phone with no browser says so on the tile (`feature/settings/CoffeeTile.kt`). Website: the button in the landing page's last tile, a link in each footer (the What's new page's through `tools/changes_page.py`), the "Is MilO free?" answer, and a line in the privacy policy for the tile and the links. Device checks BC-1 to BC-3.

**`[CHANGE]` The website describes several vehicles, labels and What's new**
The landing page's description, lead, lists, questions and structured data (a `featureList`; the FAQ's structured data matches the questions on the page word for word, as Google asks), the comparison's MilO column and "fits if" lists, and the privacy policy's list of what the app keeps. The CRA and IRS answer now says the report prints a purpose where a trip has a label; "Does it record drives in other vehicles?" became "Can MilO track more than one vehicle?"; "Can I note what each trip was for?" is new. **`[FIX]` Lint: "+ 1 more" is a plural**
`settings_vehicles_more` failed Android Lint's PluralsCandidate (a warning, so an error here). It is a `<plurals>` with the same words for one and several in English.

**`[DECISION]` What's new: the list of changes is a file in the repository, not a server**
Shawn: "make sure we have a version number in the app under settings. also please make it clickable and make it work same as gopherforms and seawingman. with including version and what has changed the only difference is im not sure how we are going to do the backend to edit the list and release of changes. also include this on the website". Both of his other apps were read (cloned read-only into the session): GopherForms' Settings has "Version 1.4.2 →", which opens "What's new", entries grouped by version with a tag each (New, Improved, Fixed, Security), and a "What's new" shown once after a new version; both keep the entries in a database behind their server, with an admin page that edits and releases them. MilO has no server and no internet permission, and its privacy promise rests on that. Asked, Shawn chose **"A file in the repo"** (over "A server like GopherForms", which would have needed both and an ADR), with the popup once after each update. So "the backend" is `app/src/main/assets/changelog.json`, edited in a pull request like the code; "release of changes" is dating a version in it. The app reads it as an asset, and the website's page is written from it by a script, so the two cannot disagree.

**`[CHANGE]` The version on Settings, What's new, and the list of changes**
- **The list** (`app/src/main/assets/changelog.json`): versions newest first, each with its date (null until released) and its changes (`new`, `improved`, `fixed`, `security`; a title and an optional body). The first version is 0.1.0, the first release, which is not published yet: eight lines on what MilO does, all "New".
- **The app** reads it strictly (`data/changelog/`, kotlinx.serialization, as the export file): an unknown key or kind, or a date that is not a day, makes it unreadable, and the screen says so. Settings' last tile is **Version**, "0.1.0 · build 1", read from Android (`platform/system/InstalledVersion.kt`, which the export file's `appVersion` now uses too), drawn as the Setup tile is; it opens **What's new** (`feature/whatsnew/`), a grey label for each version over a tile of its changes, each with a tag. The tag is the event log's `Tag` with four colour pairs of its own (`changeTags`), all colours the design already had.
- **Once after an update:** `whats_new_seen_version` in the settings file; when the first start is over and it is not the version installed, the app opens What's new and stores it. The first start's OK stores it too, so a fresh install never sees it, and a phone that had MilO before (past the first start) sees it once.
- **The rules and the website** (`tools/changes_page.py`, standard library only): versions are three numbers, each once, newest first; only the newest may be undated; dates go back in time; **the newest version must be the build's `versionName`**; and `website/changes.html` must be what the list makes. CI runs it with `--check` before Gradle, and the website's deploy before uploading. The page is committed, so the site stays plain files (ADR-003); it is in the menu, the landing page's footer, the sitemap and a rewrite (`/changes`).
- **How versions move now** (ENGINEERING_STANDARDS §14, README "Making a release", CLAUDE.md's build loop): a change Shawn will notice adds a line in its own pull request; while the newest version is released, that pull request opens the next (a new number, undated, `versionName` set to it, `versionCode` up by one); a release dates it. This replaces "raise the version as its own pull request at release time" and "there is no CHANGELOG.md": FINDINGS_LOG stays the engineering record, the list is the record for the person using the app.
- **Checked:** the parser's tests (9) ran here in a scratch Gradle project with the serialization plugin; the rest needs Android's libraries and runs in CI. The script's rules were tried on broken lists. The page was drawn in Chromium at 390 and 1280 pixels (no line wider than the screen). ktlint 1.8.0 is clean on every changed file. Nothing has run on an emulator or the phone: device checks WN-1 to WN-9.
- **Kept out:** the Android Auto screen; anything that needs a network.

**`[FINDING]` A coroutine can hand on a copy of an exception, whose cause is the exception thrown**
A test that checked `ChangelogUnreadableException.cause is IOException`, around a `withContext`, failed: with assertions on (as in Gradle's test runs) kotlinx.coroutines recovers stack traces by making a copy of the exception through its `(message, cause)` constructor, and the copy's cause is the original. The test checks the message instead. Anything that inspects the cause of an exception that crossed `withContext` has the same trap.

**`[DECISION]` The website calls the app "MilO Trip Log"**
Shawn: "theres another app on playstore same name and function as my app. what should we do?" (Milo Track – Mileage Tracking, `com.hsluiscampingcomfort.milotrack`, also on the App Store, at milotrack.com: trips started with a button, PDF and Excel reports, data on the phone). A search found at least two more mileage apps called Milo, one of them an established company's automatic tracker. **No technical clash:** Android tells apps apart by the package (`com.shawnkowalchuk.milo`), MilO is not on Google Play, and both install side by side; **the package is never renamed**, because that forces an uninstall, which deletes the trips. What is exposed is the public side: the site and the downloads advertise a "MilO" mileage tracker. Advice given: look up "MILO" in CIPO's and the USPTO's trademark registers for software (this session cannot reach them) and ask a lawyer if a live mark covers mileage software; meanwhile give the public side a distinct name. Shawn: "yes please switch to specify Milo TripLog. TripLog one word." Asked again, because "TripLog" as one word is the brand of TripLog, a competitor on `compare.html`, he chose **"MilO Trip Log"**, two words, which reads as a description and matches the domain. The site uses it in every top line, title, link preview and the structured data (`alternateName` "MilO"), keeps "MilO" in running text, and every footer says it is not affiliated with any other app or company named Milo. **The app's name on the phone stays "MilO"**, as Shawn's question was about the website.

**`[CHANGE]` Google Search Console's verification file on the website**
Shawn sent `google995357ceb40b715c.html` ("please add to the website for google search console"): Search Console's HTML-file check of ownership, which must be served at `https://milotriplog.top/google995357ceb40b715c.html` with its one line unchanged. It is in `website/` as it came. **`firebase.json` lost `cleanUrls`:** that setting sends every `….html` address to its short form with a 301, and Google's check of the file is not to depend on a redirect. `/privacy` and `/compare` are two rewrites instead, so every link on the site still works; the long forms (`/privacy.html`) now answer too, and each page's canonical address is the short one.

Which of W-1 to W-14 he went through was not said, so they stay open one by one (DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST). **The website** now gives it a line of its own in "With you" (the trip as it runs, Start and End, this month's and this year's Business kilometres in dollars at a rate you set) and a ninth question, "Is there a home-screen widget?", which says the dollars are for reference and not on the report, and adds it to the questions' structured data. **`[DECISION]` The website: milotriplog.top on Firebase Hosting, deployed on a merge (ADR-003)**
Shawn: "i created a web project in firebase for another project reactimate. i want to do the same for milo. i allready purchased a domain name milotriplog.top and created the project in firebase. what do we do now?" Reactimate (read from its public repository) is a web app on Firebase Hosting at reactimate.top, deployed by a GitHub Actions job with a service-account key in GitHub's secrets. MilO is an Android app, so its site is a page about it, not the app. Asked, Shawn chose **"Landing + privacy page"**, **"Auto on merge, like Reactimate"** (over deploying by hand from the Mac) and **"Plain HTML and CSS"** (over Vite and React). The project's display name is "MilOtriplog"; its ID, `milotriplog`, was confirmed from the console (Project settings) the same evening. It is on the free Spark plan. The deploy key is MilO's first secret outside the Mac, so ADR-003 was written before it, as CLAUDE.md asks. **The app is not changed and gets no Firebase.**

**`[CHANGE]` The website**
- **`website/`:** `index.html` (what MilO does, the screenshots, "Download the latest APK" to the newest GitHub Release, install steps, a privacy summary), `privacy.html` (the policy: what the app keeps, what leaves the phone and how, each permission and why, deleting, and what the site itself does), `404.html`, `styles.css` (the app's colours, corners and Sora, copied from the design system), `mark.svg` (the launcher icon's mark), `fonts/` (a copy of `sora.ttf` with its licence). No JavaScript, no cookies, no analytics, nothing from another site.
- **The screenshots moved** from `docs/screenshots/` to `website/screenshots/`, so the README and the site share one copy; the README's image paths changed with them.
- **`firebase.json`:** `website/` is the public folder; clean addresses; a Content-Security-Policy that allows only the site's own images, styles and font, with `nosniff`, a referrer policy and a permissions policy; cache times. **`.firebaserc`:** the project `milotriplog`.
- **`.github/workflows/website.yml`:** on push to `main` when the site's files change, and by hand. It fails with instructions when the secret `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_MILOTRIPLOG` is missing, then deploys with `FirebaseExtended/action-hosting-deploy` v0.11.0, pinned by commit SHA like the other actions, to the live channel.
- **`.gitignore`:** the Firebase command-line tool's local files, and the usual names of a downloaded service-account key, as a backstop.
- **Checked:** both pages and the not-found page drawn in Chromium at 390 and 1280 pixels wide: nothing wider than the screen, Sora loaded, no file missing. The first drawing showed the download button's words in the button's own colour, because the lime tile's link colour won: fixed with a selector of two classes.
- **Documents:** ADR-003; CLAUDE.md (the website's secret, and that it is not an app surface); ENGINEERING_STANDARDS §3, §12, §14; ARCHITECTURE (overview, folders, external services, environments); README (the link at the top, "The website" with the setup steps); APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (a "Website" entry and its index line); DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (WEB-1 to WEB-6).
- **Not done here, and Shawn's:** switching Hosting on, making the deploy key with `firebase init hosting:github` on the Mac, and connecting the domain at its registrar (README, "The website"). This session cannot log in to Firebase or to the registrar.

**`[CHANGE]` SEO for the website, and a comparison with TripLog, MileIQ, Driversnote and Everlance**
Shawn: "can you also make some SEO content for the site. I would like a comparison to other similiar products triplog, mileIQ, driversnote,everlance", and then "any other suggestions".
- **SEO:** `robots.txt`, `sitemap.xml`, a title and description with what people search for, schema.org `SoftwareApplication` and `FAQPage` data, eight questions on the landing page, and `og-image.png` for shared links. **A question that matters:** "Is the report enough for the CRA or the IRS?" says that the report has no column for each trip's purpose, which both agencies ask a logbook to show (the purpose field was dropped at kickoff, APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Monthly PDF and submission"). The site claims no compliance.
- **`compare.html`:** a table (price, free plan, phones, how a trip starts, starting on the car's own Bluetooth, account, where trips are kept, Business or Personal, reports, extras, support, where to get it), "MilO fits if" and "Another app fits better if", and a paragraph on each. **Facts:** a research agent read the four vendors' own pages through web-search extracts on 2026-10-08; the egress proxy refused every vendor domain, so no page was opened directly. Hence no paid prices (links to the pricing pages instead), tax claims given as the vendor's own words, "not found" where a feature was not found, and nothing from their privacy policies. **Found on the way:** TripLog, too, starts trips on the car's Bluetooth (above 8 km/h), so the page does not call that MilO's alone; MileIQ uses Bluetooth only to tell which vehicle, Driversnote its own iBeacon, Everlance phone motion.
- **Checked:** drawn in Chromium at 390 and 1280 pixels: the table first widened the whole page on a phone (a grid item's minimum width is its content's); `min-width: 0` on the page's tiles keeps it sliding inside its tile, with the row names pinned.
- **Suggested, not done (Shawn's steps):** Google Search Console for the domain, with the sitemap; publishing release 0.1.0 so the download button has something to open; the site's address in the repository's "About" box. Raised for later: Google Play, a short video of a trip starting, French.

**`[DEBT]` The Firebase command-line tool is pinned where Dependabot cannot see it**
The deploy action runs `firebase-tools` at the version it is given, "latest" by default. It is pinned to 15.33.0 in `website.yml` (`# TODO(debt):`), because every version in this repository is an exact pin, and a "latest" would run unknown code with the deploy key. Dependabot updates the action's SHA but not this input, so it is raised by hand: when Dependabot raises the action, or when a deploy warns that the tool is old. Cost: one line, now and then.

**`[DECISION]` Two sounds: the one MilO had for the connect, a second when the truck drives off**
Shawn sent five clips (giggity-allright, mario-1-up, mariostart, mk64_racestart, super_mario_bros_mushroom): "i want to add a second sound setting. i want to use the current one for connecting to a vehicle and another custom one when a trip starts. i want to include these sounds that can also be used for either sound". MilO starts a trip the moment the truck connects, so "connected" and "trip started" were one moment. Asked when the new sound should play, Shawn chose **"When the truck drives off"** (the first fix at 15 km/h or more, the parked watch's test; it also plays when a parked truck drives off) over "Right after the connect sound" and "Only when there's no connect". Asked how the clips get in, Shawn chose **"Add them on the phone"** over "Built in on your Mac only": they are Nintendo's and Fox's recordings, the repository and its APK are public, and the rule of the kickoff (no one else's recording committed or bundled) stands. **"Phone only"** for the settings; the sounds play whatever screen is up.

**`[CHANGE]` The connect sound and the trip-start sound**
- **Two sounds** (`TripSound` in `data/settings/SoundListStorage.kt`): `CONNECT`, the sound there was, renamed on the screen from "Trip-start sound" to "Connect sound" and kept under its keys (`sound_enabled`, `custom_sound_uri`, `custom_sound_name`); and `DRIVING_OFF`, "Trip-start sound" on the screen, under three new keys (`driving_off_sound_enabled`, on out of the box; `driving_off_sound_uri`, `driving_off_sound_name`). `MiloSettings.sound(which)` reads either. The one list of his own sounds (`own_sounds`) serves both, and one copy can be both; removing a sound takes it from both, and each that played it plays its built-in one.
- **The moment** (`TripProgress.drivenAtMs`, pure, in `core/trip/`): the first usable fix whose speed reading is 15 km/h or more (`DRIVING_OFF_KMH`), or without a reading, distance counted up to it at that speed. One fix is enough, unlike beside a parked truck: it decides a sound, nothing else. It is read from the stored points like the rest of the trip's picture, so a trip picked up after a restart that had driven off already plays nothing; `TripWorker.noticeDrivingOff` tells the service once per trip id, after every piece of work, and holds back while a companion trip waits to be confirmed. The speed between two fixes moved from `ParkedWatch` to `TrackPoint.metresPerSecondFrom` so both use the one function.
- **The service** gets both moments in one order (`TripRecorder.record(checkAtMs, sounds)`, was `tripJustStarted`), the connect sound first. **The player** (`TripStartSound` renamed `TripSoundPlayer`) plays either; in the trip service a sound asked for while another plays **waits for it to end**, so a 6-second clip for the connect sound is not cut off by the trip-start sound; the Settings Play buttons still cut off.
- **The built-in trip-start sound** is a new original chime (`res/raw/trip_go_chime.wav`, 0.73 s: C, E, G and the C above, the last held), from the same script (`tools/make_trip_start_chirp.py`), which now writes both files; the chirp's bytes are unchanged. The chirp keeps its resource name `trip_start_chirp`, because the R2-D2 clip on Shawn's Mac replaces it by that name.
- **Settings:** two tiles of the one design (`SoundTile(state, actions, which)`): each its switch, a grey line on when it plays, the shared list, Play, "Add a sound" and "Remove this sound". The file picker remembers which tile opened it (`rememberSaveable`), and copying or a refusal is said on that tile. The alarm sentence stands once, under the second tile.
- **Wording that changed meaning:** the event log says "Connect sound: …" and "Trip-start sound: …" where it said "Trip-start sound: …"; a parked truck's new trip is logged "with no connect sound"; after a restore, "the chosen sounds, whose files are in no backup"; the export file's `about` names "the sounds chosen from the phone".
- **Tests:** `TripProgressTest` (5), `TripControllerDrivingOffTest` (5, new), and the sound tests brought to two sounds with new cases: `OwnTripSoundTest` (4), `SoundListStorageTest` (4), `SettingsStoreTest` (1), `BackupAftermathTest` (1), `SettingsUiStateTest` (1), `TripControllerWaitingTest` (the parked drive-off now has its trip-start sound). The core tests (521) ran here; everything else needs the Android libraries and runs in CI only. ktlint 1.8.0 is clean on every changed file. Nothing has run on an emulator or the phone: device checks TS-1 to TS-10.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Trip-start sound" is now "Connect and trip-start sounds", with its index line, and the Settings, detection, event log and backup entries), ARCHITECTURE (the settings keys, `own_sounds` included, which was missing; the sounds folder, now one copy per sound on the list), README, DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (TS-1 to TS-10).

**`[DEBT]` An export file carries the connect sound's switch, not the trip-start sound's**
`TransferredSettings.soundEnabled` is the connect sound's switch, the one there always was. Adding the trip-start sound's changes the export file's form, which needs a new format version and a reader for both, as for the parked limit, the odometer readings, the widget's rate and the unit (`TransferStorage.kt`, its `TODO(debt)`). An import leaves this phone's trip-start switch as it is. Cost: one more field in the format that takes them all.

**`[DECISION]` A first start: a page, then Setup, then Settings**
Shawn: "when the app starts for the first time i want a onboarding. What the app does and how it works. than a ok. than go to the setup screen so the user can set up all settings for vehicle. once finished please send them to the settings screen." Asked four things, Shawn chose: **"Phone only"** (the Android Auto screen is not touched); **"One page, then OK"** (over a few pages with Next); **"A 'Done' button"** on Setup (over moving on by itself once every required row is ready, which would have kept anyone on Setup whose HyperOS rows MilO cannot read); and **"Yes, once"**: a phone that already has MilO sees it once, after the update that brings it, not only a fresh install.

**`[CHANGE]` The first start**
- **The stage** is `first_run_stage` in the settings file (`data/settings/OnboardingStorage.kt`): absent until OK, then `setup`, then `done`. A value a build does not know is read as `done`. Not in an export file: it is this phone's; Android's backup carries it.
- **The page** (`feature/onboarding/OnboardingScreen.kt`): drawn by `MiloApp` in place of every other screen, without the bottom bar, while the stage is absent. One page that scrolls: the top line with "Welcome", the accent tile, five plain tiles (how a trip starts and ends, Business or Personal, each month, privacy, what comes next) and OK. Built from the design system's parts; nothing added to it. Outside the bottom bar's frame the page gives itself the page colour and keeps clear of the system bars (`safeDrawingPadding`), as Home's preview does.
- **OK** stores `setup` and opens Setup on top of Home. **While the stage is `setup`,** `MiloNavigation` hands Setup a Done action, and Setup shows the main button "Done, go to Settings" at its end (`onDone`, null at any other time, which is every other caller). **Done** stores `done` and shows Settings as the bar's button does.
- **Nothing keeps anyone on the page** (`OnboardingViewModel`): a stage reached counts at once, stored or not, with an `ERROR` line if it could not be stored; a settings file that cannot be read counts as `done`, with an `ERROR` line.
- **Why `MiloApp`, and not a screen on the back stack:** the page is not a place to come back to, the bottom bar must not show over it, and the back stack the screens restore after Android puts MilO away would otherwise have to learn a ninth key that is only ever seen once.
- Tests: `OnboardingStorageTest` (4), `OnboardingViewModelTest` (5). They need the Android libraries (DataStore, the lifecycle ViewModel), so they run in CI only; ktlint 1.8.0 is clean on every changed file. Nothing of it has been drawn: device checks OB-1 to OB-7.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (a new entry, its index line, and Setup's Done button under the permission checklist), ARCHITECTURE (the first start before every screen, the package map, the settings key), README (first-time setup, and installing the APK), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (OB-1 to OB-7).

**`[DECISION]` Releases on GitHub: an APK built on the Mac and uploaded by hand**
Shawn: "Put a Big Download Button at the Top", and "Every time you update the app, bundle it into an `.apk` file and upload it to the Releases section". Asked how a release should be built and signed, Shawn chose "You build on the Mac, then upload" over a GitHub Actions build signed with a new public key, and over one signed with the phone's own key kept in GitHub's secrets. So no secret is added anywhere and no ADR is needed: the release is `assembleRelease`, signed with `~/keys/milo.jks` as the debug build already is, and its APK installs over the phone's MilO too. Each release raises `versionCode` by one and sets `versionName` (until now every build was 1 and "0.1.0"); a comment at the two numbers in `app/build.gradle.kts` says so.

**`[CHANGE]` A download button, an install section and a release guide in the README**
- **At the very top:** a large lime "Download latest APK" button and a text link under it, both to `releases/latest`, which always opens the newest release; with "Android 14 or newer" and a link to the install steps. **No release exists yet:** until the first is published, the link opens an empty Releases page.
- **"Installing the APK":** download it on the phone, allow the browser or Files to install unknown apps when Android asks (Developer options are not needed), Play Protect may warn about an unknown developer, then Settings, Setup. Updating is installing the newer APK over the old one; uninstalling deletes the trips. And what to expect elsewhere: MilO is tested on one Xiaomi phone, and the Android Auto screen is not expected on a car's display for an app installed this way (ARCHITECTURE, "Distribution risk").
- **"Making a release"** (For developers): the five steps, and never an APK from CI or from `-Pmilo.signing.debugKey=true`. ENGINEERING_STANDARDS §14 says the same in one rule. **`assembleRelease` has never been run:** the first release is to be installed on the phone and looked at before it is published.

**`[DECISION]` The repository is public, with no license**
Shawn made the GitHub repository public on 2026-10-08 ("i made the project public on github"). Asked about a license, Shawn chose "No license, all rights reserved" over MIT and Apache 2.0: the code is public to read, and may not be copied, changed or redistributed without permission. Asked about the street addresses that the debugging notes of 2026-10-07 had quoted (the shop's, and clients' sites, with times), Shawn chose "Replace with generic words" over leaving them and over rewriting the git history (which would have broken every clone and the open pull request). **The older versions of those lines stay in the git history,** where anyone can still read them.

**`[CHANGE]` The README for a public repository, with screenshots**
- **The top of the README is for a visitor:** what MilO is, a row of four screenshots, what it does by itself and with you, privacy (no account, no server, no internet permission), how it is built, an honest status (what the truck has proven, what has run nowhere), and the license. The owner's guide that the README used to be follows under its own heading, "The owner's guide", so that nothing a visitor reads first is written for one phone.
- **Screenshots** (`docs/screenshots/`): Home, Trips, Settings and the Log, taken on the phone by Shawn on 2026-10-08. On Home the last trip's two street addresses are blurred; nothing else was changed. And a sample report page (`report-sample.png`), drawn from the layout code of the same day with made-up trips, a made-up name and company, in static instances of Sora. Asked first, Shawn chose "Report now, phone shots from you".
- **The README was out of date in five places,** each corrected: Setup was the bottom bar's third button (since 2026-10-07 it is the tile at the top of Settings, and the third button is Settings); pairing the truck "has never been done" (it was done on 2026-10-06); the parked rule "has not been seen on the phone" (it ran in the truck on 2026-10-07); the wait beside a parked truck read GPS "every 30 seconds" (since 2026-10-07 for the first hour only, every 5 seconds after a report of getting into a vehicle, then the motion sensor); and the table of where things are had neither the widget, the odometer nor the sound list.
- **"Private" in the other documents:** ENGINEERING_STANDARDS (the project profile, and §14: on a public repository GitHub Free can make the CI check required before a merge; it is not switched on), ARCHITECTURE (the GitHub row), ADR-001 (a note under its consequence about blocking merges).
- **The addresses:** FINDINGS_LOG (four lines of 2026-10-07 and one of 2026-10-08), APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (one line) and ADR-002 (amendment 34) say "the shop" and "a client's site" where they gave street addresses.
- **Not done here, and why:** the repository's topics (GitHub's "About" box) cannot be set from this session; nor could the old branches be deleted, which this session's permissions refused. Both are a few clicks on GitHub.

**`[FINDING]` October 7 was on the report all along, on page 2: a day that fitted on a page was moved there whole**
Shawn sent October 2026 to the accountant as a test: "it only showed october 6 but i had entries for october 7 too", and sent the PDF (`Mileage-2026-10-Shawn-Kowalchuk.pdf`). Page 1 held the top tiles and October 6 (2 trips, 48.7 km) and then half a page of nothing; page 2 held October 7 (6 trips, 47.7 km) and the total, 96.4 km for 8 trips, which is right. The layout's rule "a day that fits on a page is not split; if it does not fit in what is left of the page, it starts the next one" (2026-10-06) had sent October 7's six trips to page 2 because they did not fit under October 6. Nothing was missing; the page made it look so.

**`[DECISION]` The report fills its pages and is more compact; the addresses stay whole**
Asked, Shawn chose "Fill the page, continue the day", adding: "It also should be more compressed. i can see this being pages and pages."
