Then: "Keep full addresses" (over Canada Post's short forms, "12846 146 St NW, Edmonton", and over dropping the city), "Day groups, slimmer" (over one flat table with a Date column and no daily subtotals), and "Same look, tighter" for the top of page 1 (over one compact band, and over leaving it). Phone only: the Android Auto screen makes no report.

**`[CHANGE]` A compact report that fills its pages**
- **Page breaks** (`layoutReport`): the rule that moved a whole day to the next page is gone. A day starts in the room left and goes on at the top of the next page under its heading with "(continued)", as a day longer than a page always did. A heading is still never left alone, a row is never cut, and a subtotal still stands with its day's last trip.
- **The column titles stand once on each page,** above its first day, not under every day. On a page that continues a day they come first, then "(the day) (continued)".
- **Smaller and tighter** (`ReportTextStyle`, `ReportBlocks`): a trip's line 8 points (9), the column titles 7 (7.5), the day's heading 8.5 (10) on a 16-point bar (22), 2 points above and below a row (3), 8 between two days (14), 6 between two columns (8). At the top: tiles 6 apart (10), 10 to 12 points of room inside them (16 to 18), the mark 28 points (36), the large figures 20 points (26), the total 18 (22); the notes 8 (8.5), the footer 7 (7.5). The look, the colours, the order and every word are unchanged.
- **What it comes to:** laid out and drawn on a computer from the layout itself, in static instances of Sora at the four weights. Shawn's own October of 8 trips is one page (two before), with both days and the total on it. A month of 8 trips on each of its 22 working days is 7 pages (12 before). At 8 points most of the addresses fit on one line whole; the longest, a long street name with its direction and town, still wrap.
- **Tests:** four were written for what changed and were rewritten: the day that "starts the next one, whole" now starts on page 1 and goes on; the day longer than a page has the column titles above its repeated heading; a short report has the column titles once; the footer's cut name, with a smaller footer, keeps one more letter at the old width. The shared check of every layout (`assertSound`) now also holds each page to one set of column titles above its days. `core/report`, `core/util` and `core/odometer`, 168 tests, ran here with the pinned Kotlin 2.4.20 and warnings as errors; ktlint 1.8.0 is clean. The drawing pass (`platform/report/ReportPdf.kt`) did not change: it draws whatever sizes the layout hands it. Not yet made on the phone: device checks NL-124 and NL-125.

### 2026-10-07 (night)

**`[FINDING]` The phone's date is set a day ahead for a few seconds, and MilO took it for real**
On the evening of 2026-10-07 the phone showed "No trip recorded today" after a full day of trips. Android's own time log, read from the phone that evening (`dumpsys time_detector`), shows why: "origin=manual … Settings: Set date", the clock 24 hours ahead, and the network setting it back 5 to 16 seconds later. Five times that evening (18:32, 18:39, 18:44, 18:51 and 19:02), once on 2026-10-06 (21:43) and once on 2026-10-05 (21:02). Shawn, asked: "i do that to get lives in candy crush". He will keep doing it.
- **What a jump is.** The clock goes ahead by 24 hours, some milliseconds short, and comes back when automatic time is switched on again. The time zone does not change. The clock that counts from the phone's boot is not affected. Android sends `TIME_SET` for each change.
- **Where MilO read the phone's clock:** in some forty places. Most were `clock = System::currentTimeMillis` in the wiring (`app/`); the rest read it directly (the trip service and its timer, the driving receiver, the widget, the backup agent, the crash handler, the header's date, the Log screen's "today"). Nothing listened for a change of the clock.
- **What one jump did,** proved the same day by unit tests on main and by a run on an emulator:
  - Android delivered both daily alarms at once. "No trip recorded today" was shown for tomorrow, tomorrow was stored as "already notified", and both alarms were asked for again for the day after tomorrow. This is what happened on the phone. On the last evening of a month the monthly reminder would have announced a month that had not ended.
  - Beside the parked truck, in the first hour of a wait: one GPS fix inside the jump made MilO think the hour was over and switch GPS off. On the emulator the next drive was recorded by nothing.
  - After more than 48 hours beside the truck: one reading inside the jump "reached" the 72-hour limit, and MilO stopped watching for good.
  - During a drive: the first GPS fix inside the jump made the parked rule think the truck had stood for 24 hours. The trip was closed at speed. With the truck connected the drive became two records; without it the rest was lost.
  - A trip that started inside the jump was dated tomorrow (wrong day, weekday, month, Business or Personal), ended before it started, and was cut one parked limit after its start.
  - The hold-off after End lapsed at once, a grace period ended at once, and a disconnect inside the jump stored its cut a day ahead.
  - Lines written to the event log in those seconds are dated tomorrow.
- **What a jump did not do.** A finished trip that was already stored was never changed. The distance calculation, the pacing of the minute check and the watch's "two fixes in a row" already ran on the clock that counts from boot.
- **Still on the phone from those evenings,** going by the copy of its database taken that evening after the first jump: five log lines dated 2026-10-08 18:32 (they head the list until their time comes), and the daily check's stored "shown on 2026-10-08". No trip in that copy is dated ahead, and none ends before it starts (15 trips, the newest started at 17:01). The four later jumps of the evening are not in the copy.

**`[DECISION]` MilO keeps its own clock** (ADR-006)
Written that night as ADR-003. It is ADR-006 since 2026-10-09: main took 003 for the website and 004 for Google Play before this was moved on top of it. One small mechanism behind the `clock` that every object was already handed. The trip rules, the parked rule, the grace period, the hold-off, the waiting limits, the two checks' rules and the database are not changed: they are handed a time that cannot jump.
- **The rule.** MilO's clock holds an anchor (a time of day, and what the clock that counts from boot read then). At each reading it compares its own time with the phone's. Within 2 minutes: the phone's clock is taken, so MilO's time is exactly the phone's while nobody touches it. Further apart: MilO keeps its own time, and follows the phone only when the difference has held for 10 minutes.
- **The anchor is kept in a file** with the number of the phone's boot, so a MilO that Android starts while the date is ahead has the right time at once. After a restart of the phone, or with no file, the phone's clock is taken as it is. (Since 2026-10-09 such an anchor is on probation: the entries at the top.)
- **Decided while building, beyond the design as it was handed over:**
  - **"Held for 10 minutes" means seen to hold.** If more than 2 minutes pass between two looks at a change, its 10 minutes start again, and while MilO does not believe the phone it looks every 30 seconds. Without it, two looks 10 minutes apart that both fell into a jump would pass for one change that held. On 2026-10-07 the first and third jump were 12 minutes apart, the fourth and fifth 10. A process that Android has put to sleep looks at nothing in between.
  - **What an earlier process saw is not handed on.** Only the anchor is stored. Each process waits its own 10 minutes, for the same reason.
  - **The anchor's file is read and written in `data/clock/`,** not in `platform/`, because `data/` is the layer that touches storage (STANDARDS §3). It is written on a thread of its own.
  - **A record of a process that died while the date was ahead** carries tomorrow's time, which Android gave it. It is written at the time MilO reads it, with a note in the line, and the "imported up to" mark is not moved past the present. Because the mark then cannot say that this record is done, each record is looked for in the newest 500 process lines before it is written. A mark that the build before left in the future is not trusted: the records are read from the start and the same look keeps them from being written twice.
  - **The clock's lines are `PROCESS` lines.** A new category would be a word no earlier build can read (ARCHITECTURE, section 6).
  - **The line says what MilO saw:** "MilO saw it back 9 s later", not "for 9 s". A process that was asleep sees the clock back only when it next looks.
  - **Android's word that the clock was set has both alarms asked for again whenever the clocks agree,** also when MilO followed the change at once. The request is the same one and harmless to repeat.
  - **The screens get the time through a composition local** (`LocalMiloClock`), which `MiloApp` provides once. Its default is a fixed moment, for previews.
  - **A phone that gives no boot number is said in the log.** Without that number the anchor cannot be trusted across two starts of MilO, and a MilO started while the date is ahead would take it for real again, silently. Each start then writes one `ERROR` line. Android has kept the number since Android 7; that the POCO X5 gives it has not been seen (CJ-1).
  - **Two small functions were taken out of `TripService`** (`ParkedGps.rateAt`, `nextChangeAfter`) so that the switching of GPS beside the parked truck can be tested. What the service does is unchanged.

**`[CHANGE]` MilO's clock, and everything that asks the time handed it**
- **Built on 2026-10-07, on main as it was that night, and moved on top of main as it stood on 2026-10-09,** after pull requests #25 to #32 (2026-10-09, "MilO's clock put onto today's main"). The figures in this entry are those of 2026-10-07.
- **The clock:** `core/clock/TrustedClock.kt` (the rule, pure, with `FOLLOW_AT_ONCE_MS`, `HOLD_MS`, `SEEN_AGAIN_WITHIN_MS`, `ANCHOR_SAVE_EVERY_MS`), `data/clock/ClockAnchorStore.kt` (one line in `no_backup/clock/anchor`, replaced in one step), `platform/clock/PhoneClock.kt` (the one place that reads the phone's clock; one clock per process), `ClockWatch.kt` (the line, the looking again, the alarms), `ClockChangeReceiver.kt` (`TIME_SET`, in the manifest, not exported, no permission), `ClockLogText.kt`.
- **Wiring:** `MiloApplication` makes the clock first and hands it to the `AppContainer`; `container.clock` goes to everything that had `System::currentTimeMillis`. New part of the container: `ClockObjects`.
- **Readers that asked the phone directly, now handed the clock:** `TripService` (its two timers and the GPS window of a wait), `TripCheckTimer`, `TripServiceIntent`, `DrivingReceiver`, `HomeWidgetViews`, `MiloBackupAgent`, `CrashHandler`, `AndroidAutoLog`, `MiloCarAppService`, the header's date (`AppHeader`) and the Log screen's "today".
- **The daily alarms:** `MonthlyReminder` and `NothingRecordedCheck` do not ask Android for an alarm while the clocks disagree, and write a line that says so. `ClockWatch` has both asked for again when the clocks agree. (Changed on 2026-10-09: while the clocks disagree they ask for it on the clock that counts from boot.)
- **Stored state put right:** a "shown" day after today counts as not shown and is taken out of the settings, for the daily check and for the reminder (`reminderMoment`, so the Home tile agrees).
- **Tests.** `WallClockReadersTest` reads the source tree and fails if anything but `PhoneClock.kt` reads the phone's wall clock. 49 tests of the clock itself, its file, its lines and its watch. The investigation's proof tests were turned round and are now the regression suite: each drives its scene through the real `TrustedClock` on a phone whose date a test sets (`JumpingPhone`), and asserts the right outcome. Run on the phone's own clock instead, 18 of the 22 trip scenes fail. Existing tests are unchanged but for one new constructor argument in two fixtures and two tests.
- **The gate** (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes: 2,304 unit tests, 128 of them new.
- **Nothing of this has run on the phone, or on an emulator.** Device checks CJ-1 to CJ-10.

**`[DEBT]` A change of the clock that is meant is followed late, and with a jump**
A date really set by hand, or a phone whose clock was wrong and is put right by more than 2 minutes, is followed only by a process that has watched it for 10 minutes without a gap: during a trip, a wait beside the truck, or with MilO open. Until then MilO's times differ from the phone's and the daily alarms are not asked for (since 2026-10-09 they are, on the clock that counts from boot). The reading that follows the change moves MilO's time by the whole change at once, and a trip that is open then is hit as trips were before (the parked rule, the grace period). A date held ahead for longer than 10 minutes is believed, and so is its way back, 10 minutes late. Cost to fix: a rule for when a change may be followed (not while a trip is open), and a way to keep looking while MilO's process sleeps. Tagged in `TrustedClock.kt`. The older debt in `TripState.kt` ("a wall clock corrected by more than the time left…") now concerns only corrections of up to 2 minutes and a change that is followed.

**`[DEBT]` An alarm delivered at the jump can stay missing until MilO next starts** (paid on 2026-10-09: the decision at the top)
Android delivers both daily alarms when the date goes ahead, and MilO asks for them again only when it sees the clocks agree. If MilO's process is gone by then, the `TIME_SET` broadcast has to start it. Whether HyperOS lets it has not been seen (CJ-8). If not, the alarms are asked for at MilO's next start, which the truck's connect gives every working morning; a day on which nothing starts MilO would get no daily check. Cost to fix: an alarm on the clock that counts from boot does not go off at a jump at all (`ELAPSED_REALTIME`), and would make the asking again unnecessary; it was not part of ADR-006. Tagged in `ClockWatch.kt`.

**`[DEBT]` What Android stamps itself still goes by the phone's clock**
- The trip notification's running time is worked out by Android from the phone's clock. Posted inside a jump it reads a day long until it is next posted (within 15 seconds while the distance changes). Tagged in `TripNotifications.kt`.
- A date picker opened inside a jump marks tomorrow as "today". The picker is the Material library's and draws that mark itself. What can be picked is still limited by MilO's clock.
- A file's date comes from the file system. MilO uses it in two places: a crash file that cannot be read in full (its line is now dated no later than the moment it is imported), and the clearing of old report files from the cache (a file written inside a jump is kept a day longer).

**`[DEBT]` What the evenings before left on the phone is not all put right**
The log lines dated a day ahead stay. A trip stored with tomorrow's date stays too, and the edit screen takes it once its day is set back by hand; the copy of the phone's database of 2026-10-07 holds none, but it was taken before the evening's last four jumps. A wait beside the truck that was stored as begun tomorrow keeps its hour of GPS and its three days a day too long, until the truck next moves. A driving alert stamped tomorrow would keep one half hour of tomorrow quiet. None of these was seen on the phone; they are what the proof tests showed could be stored. And the first start after this update, like the first start after any restart of the phone, takes the phone's clock as it is: if the date is ahead in that moment, MilO starts a day ahead. (Since 2026-10-09 it then takes the phone's time as soon as the date is back: the entries at the top.)

### 2026-10-07 (evening)

**`[FIX]` Kilometres or miles: what a review found, and what is left for Shawn**
A second pair of eyes went through the change below. It found no wrong figure: kilometres are what they were, the report in miles adds up, the dollars do not move, and a change of unit alone never says a month has changed. Four small things were put right.
- **The odometer's tile now says "miles" where a reading is typed.** With Miles chosen the field said "Reading on the dashboard now" and the first sentence "Type the number on the truck's dashboard once", with no unit. The number typed is taken in the unit chosen, whatever the dashboard shows, so 123456 read off a dashboard in kilometres would have been stored as miles and printed on the report. Now the field is "Reading on the dashboard now, in miles" and the sentence "Type the miles on the truck's dashboard once. …". In kilometres both are worded as before. The unit a reading is stored in is still the one the tile shows at the press of "Save reading". (`odometerFieldRes`, `odometerFirstReadingRes`; two new cases in `OdometerCardStateTest`.)
- **The edit form reads its unit from the settings file,** in the read that gives it the work schedule (`TripEditing.formSettings`, which replaces `schedule()`). It took the unit held in memory, which is kilometres for the first moment of a process. A form opened in that moment, as when Android puts MilO back on the edit screen, would have stayed "Distance, km" with Miles chosen, because a form keeps its unit. No stored trip was at risk: the label named the unit the form really read. Only if the file cannot be read is the form in the unit the other screens show, and the log line for that now ends "the work schedule and the unit". `TripEditViewModelTest` is new (3 tests); its first fails with the old line.
- **Four unit fields no longer default to kilometres:** `TripForm.unit`, `EditSession.unit`, `TripsUiState.unit` and `HomeWidgetCardState.unit`. Why: the rounding functions have no default so that the compiler finds every caller, and a form built without its unit would read miles he typed as kilometres. Every caller in the app already named it; two previews and the tests now do.
- **`ARCHITECTURE.md`:** the row for the report that waits for "Did you send it?" said both "tenths of the unit the report was printed in" and "tenths of a kilometre", and an import removes seven keys, not six. The paragraph on the unit in memory now says the edit form does not go by it.
- **Left as built. Each is Shawn's to change; none is a wrong figure.**
  1. A kilometre report and a mile report of the same month have the same file name and the same email subject. The PDF says its unit after the figures at its top, in "Distances are in miles.", in the column's title and in the total line; the CSV only in the column's title. A revision in the other unit does not say that the unit changed.
  2. The distance column is titled "mi", as it is titled "km", not "Distance (mi)".
  3. After a switch, three places can show both units, each labelled: "Changed since the report was sent" (in the report's unit), the odometer's grey line (the reading as typed, the trips in the unit shown), and on the report a reading typed in the other unit, which is converted and not marked "est.".
  4. In kilometres a screen reader now hears "kilometres" for a figure that stands alone, where it heard "km". Nothing drawn changed.
  5. Database version 6 and export format 2 cannot be taken back once this build has opened the database, and a file it exports is refused by an earlier build. Do device check UN-1 first, and merge this before or after any other change to either number, not beside it.
- **Tests:** 2,264 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,259 before; 5 new). Existing tests changed only by a named unit, the fixture's settings file, and the one test of the settings read. **On an emulator** (Android 16, a fresh install): the odometer's tile in kilometres read as on `main`, before a reading and with the field open; in miles it read as above, a reading of 76,543 was stored and shown as "76,543 mi", and after MilO was ended and started again the form for a missed trip said "Distance, mi". Nothing has been on the phone. Device check UN-4 now asks for the field's label.

**`[DECISION]` Kilometres or miles, chosen in Settings**
Shawn, 2026-10-07: "also add the ability in settings to change the unitys from km to mile". Asked where, he answered **"Everywhere"**: the phone's screens, the Android Auto screen, the home-screen widget and the notifications. Asked about the accountant's report (PDF and CSV), he answered **"Follow the setting"**: with miles chosen, its distances and totals are in miles, with the unit in every heading. Nothing in MilO had a unit setting before (the encyclopedia and the code were searched), so this is a new capability. The decisions below were made to build it. Each can be changed.
1. **Storage does not change.** Trips, GPS points, the export file and the backup stay in metres. The unit is one stored setting, kilometres until he picks miles. Why: switching back and forth must lose nothing, and a stored trip must never be rewritten for a display choice. A mile is 1,609.344 m exactly.
2. **One rounding rule, in either unit.** A trip is rounded once to a tenth of the unit shown, and every total is the sum of those printed tenths. Why: a column added up by hand must give the total under it, and the phone and the report must not disagree. So a month in miles is the sum of the miles printed, not its kilometre total converted. The rule was given a unit (`tenthsOf`, `sumOfTenths`); no second set of functions was written.
3. **Kilometres do not change by a digit.** Why: he drives and reports in kilometres today, and a setting he has not touched must not move a figure. On the emulator this build's kilometre PDF and CSV were byte for byte `main`'s.
4. **Money is not converted.** The widget's dollars stay Business kilometres at the rate per kilometre, in both units. Why: his rate is a rate per kilometre, as the CRA sets its own, and switching units must not move the dollars. With miles chosen one grey line says what the rate comes to per mile, for reading only.
5. **The odometer is typed and shown in the chosen unit,** and a reading is kept with the unit it was typed in. Why: a reading must come back exactly as typed (no 123,456 that returns as 123,455).
6. **The trip rules are not touched.** "Shortest trip that counts" keeps its stored metres and its steps; in miles it is only written in miles. The parked rule's speeds and distances stay metric inside. Why: trip detection is the number one requirement, and a display setting must not reach it.
7. **The edit screen takes and shows the distance in the chosen unit.** A trip opened and saved without its distance being changed keeps its stored metres, in both units, as it did in kilometres.
8. **The event log stays as it is.** Why: it is a technical record, and old lines cannot be rewritten.
9. **The report follows the setting at the moment it is made,** and what remembers a report remembers its unit. Why: a change of unit alone must never raise "changed since the report was sent", change what a sent report is listed as, or mark a month differently.
10. **One tile on Settings, "Units", under "Trips":** the two-part control and one grey sentence. It takes effect at once everywhere.
11. **"km" and "mi" are the short forms; a screen reader hears "kilometres" and "miles".** No string names a unit beside a figure of the other unit: a sentence is given the distance as one argument, already written with its unit.
- **Decided here, not asked:**
  - **A sent report's unit is a new column** (`sent_reports.distanceUnit`), so the main database is version 6, and the export file is format 2 to carry it. Why not something smaller: the row is the record of what the report printed, and a figure without its unit is not that record. `ExportRoundTripTest` already refuses a table column the export does not carry.
  - **"Changed since the report was sent" is judged in the unit of the newest report,** not in the unit on screen: the month's trips are added up in the report's unit and held against the report's own figure. Both figures of the warning are written in that unit, so they can be compared. With the other unit on screen the warning names a unit the screen does not; that is true, and the alternative was a converted figure the report never printed.
  - **The list of sent reports shows each report in the unit it was printed in, for good.**
  - **In miles the division is done in decimal arithmetic,** half a tenth upwards (see the finding below). Kilometres keep the arithmetic they had.
  - **The odometer's figure is the reading plus the truck trips since as they are printed in the unit shown.** A reading typed in the other unit is turned into the unit shown once, from its metres, and is still "his reading", not "est.". Which reading is used is always decided in kilometres, so it cannot depend on the unit.
  - **"Shortest trip that counts" is written with two decimals in miles, always:** its steps are 100 m apart, and with one decimal five pairs of steps would read alike.
  - **The edit form's limits are named in miles rounded down** ("more than 1242 mi", "more than 111 mph"), so the sentence is true of everything it refuses. The limits themselves are 2,000 km and 180 km/h, unchanged.
  - **The PDF's and the CSV's distance column is titled "mi",** as it is titled "km" today. "Distance (mi)" was the example in the plan; "mi" keeps the two units alike and leaves kilometres as they are. The PDF also says "Distances are in miles." and "Total business miles for …".
  - **A form keeps the unit it was opened in.** The report that waits for "Did you send it?" carries its unit, so the answer records what was printed even if the setting changed in between.
  - **New lines of the event log about a report name the unit of their own figure** ("3 trips, 70.1 mi"). That is not converting log text: a line that said "70.1 km" would be wrong.
  - **The unit is held in memory** (`ShownUnit`), because a notification and the car's screen cannot wait for the settings file. For the first moment of a process it is kilometres; what was drawn in that moment is drawn again.
  - **A spoken form for a figure that stands alone** (`FigureText.spoken`, a deliberate addition to the design system). In kilometres a screen reader is now read "kilometres" where it was read "km"; nothing drawn changed.
  - **With miles chosen, "About the widget" says "the report for the accountant shows no dollars"** where it says "stays in kilometres" in kilometres, because that would no longer be true.

**`[CHANGE]` Kilometres or miles: one setting, one rule, every surface**
- **The rule** (`core/util/`): `DistanceUnit`; `tenthsOf`, `sumOfTenths`, `formatDistance`, `metresOfTenths` take the unit. `formatShortDistance` for the one setting that is a distance. `tenthsOfAKilometre` and `formatKilometres` are gone: every caller says which unit.
- **The setting** (`data/settings/UnitStorage.kt`): `distance_unit`, "km" or "mi", absent until chosen. `ShownUnit` keeps it in memory, started by `MiloApplication`.
- **The tile:** "Units" under "Trips", made of `Tile`, `TileHeading`, `SegmentedChoice` and `Note`, like "Trips outside the work hours".
- **Surfaces:** Home, Trips, the edit screen, the Report screen, Settings (shortest trip, odometer, the widget's per-mile line), the Android Auto screen, the widget (the trip's figure and its unit), the trip's notification (written again at once when the unit changes), the PDF, the CSV and the email's first line. The encyclopedia entry "Units: kilometres or miles" has the table.
- **Strings:** sentences that named "km" now take the distance as an argument (`distance_km`, `distance_mi`), so they read the same in kilometres. New: `distance_mi`, `unit_mi`, the two spoken forms, the unit's whole words, two speeds, the tile's four, the per-mile line, two for miles only. `report_pdf_column_km` and `report_pdf_odometer_km` are gone (the shared ones are used).
- **What remembers a report:** `SentReport.distanceUnit` with `MIGRATION_5_6` (one `ADD COLUMN`, default `'KILOMETRES'`) and `app/schemas/.../6.json`; `ReportHandOver.unit` (`report_handed_over_unit`, written for miles only); `changedSinceSent` asks for the month's total in the report's unit. Export format 2: a sent report's `distanceUnit`; format 1 is still read.
- **The odometer:** `OdometerReading(atMs, value, unit)`; an entry is "time:km" as before or "time:miles:mi".
- **Not touched:** `core/trip`, `platform/bluetooth`, the trip controller and its rules. In `platform/trip` only the notification's text, and one collector in `TripService` that writes it again.
- **Tests:** 2,259 pass in the gate on this Mac (2,176 before; 83 new). Every test from before passes with its expected figures unchanged; they gained a unit parameter. New: `DistanceUnitTest` (kilometres held to the old arithmetic over 320,000 distances; miles at the half tenth; columns that add up; exact conversions), `UnitStorageTest`, `ReportInMilesTest`, and new cases for the dollars, "changed since sent", the unchanged save, the odometer's round trip, the migration and the export.
- **On an emulator** (Android 16, 2026-10-07): `main`'s build was installed first, four trips of 0.3, 12.35, 100.05 and 5.5 km added, an odometer reading typed and October marked as sent; this build was then installed over it. The database went to version 6 and the sent report was listed as before. In kilometres every screen said `main`'s words, and the PDF and CSV were byte for byte `main`'s (`cmp`). In miles: 0.2 + 7.7 + 62.2 = 70.1 on Trips, on the PDF (rows, Subtotal, Total) and in the CSV. A trip in progress changed its notification from "1.5 km" to "0.9 mi" at the press, and the widget with it; the widget's dollars stayed "$79". A trip opened and saved in miles kept 12,350.0 m. With the unit switched both ways, no "changed since the report was sent" appeared until a trip was really added. **Nothing has been on the phone, and the Android Auto screen has run nowhere:** device checks UN-1 to UN-12.

**`[FINDING]` A tenth of a mile cannot be divided in binary arithmetic without rounding some halves down**
A tenth of a mile is 160.9344 m. Dividing metres by it as binary fractions, 0.35 mi typed on the edit screen (563.2704 m) comes out as 3.4999999999999996 tenths and would be shown as 0.3. Of 200,000 typed distances that end on half a tenth, 80,436 rounded down that way. So `tenthsOf` divides in decimal arithmetic for miles (`BigDecimal`, half up), and the typed figure is the figure shown. Kilometres divide by 100 and were left exactly as they were: the old arithmetic is in `DistanceUnitTest` and is compared with the new function over every half metre to 30 km, the fractions next to every half tenth, and 200,000 random distances.

**`[DEBT]` The choice of unit is not in "Export all data"**
Left out on the same terms as the odometer readings, the widget's rate and the parked limit: it is a setting, and adding settings changes the export file's form. An import leaves this phone's choice as it is; Android's backup carries it with the settings file. **Format 2 was made this evening for one thing only,** the unit a sent report was printed in, because that is a record and a wrong unit there would be a wrong record. The four settings still wait for a format that takes them together. Cost to fix: one more format version and a reader for it. Tagged in `TransferStorage.kt`.

**`[DEBT]` Names in the code that still say kilometres**
Fields that now hold a figure in the chosen unit kept their names: `TripForm.kilometres`, `TripEditUiState.Ready.kilometres`, `PrintedRow.km`, `PrintedTally.km`, `PrintedDay.subtotalKm`, `PrintedReport.totalKm`, `ReportWords.columnKm` and `odometerKm`, `CsvWords.km`, `TripFigures.kilometres`, `TodayFigures.kilometres`, `formatCentsPerKm` (which also writes the per-mile figure). Each comment says what it holds. Why: renaming them is a refactor, which the standards keep out of a feature's diff, and leaving them kept every earlier test's lines as they were. Cost to fix: a rename in one pass, no behaviour.

**`[DEBT]` A screen reader hears the short word inside a sentence**
A figure that stands alone is read with "kilometres" or "miles". A distance inside a sentence ("4 trips · 48.2 mi business", "Personal: 3.4 mi · 1 trip", the questions of the Report screen) is read as it is written, and how "mi" is spoken is the phone's speech engine's. Cost to fix: a spoken twin for each such sentence.

**`[DEBT]` Four files that were already over 300 lines grew a little**
`AppContainer.kt` (304 before, 313 now), `SettingsViewModel.kt` (303, now 308), `SettingsStore.kt` (306, now 307) and `TripService.kt` (347, now 367, the collector that writes the notification again). Every other file this change touched is at 300 lines or under. Cost to fix: the split each was already due.

**`[DECISION]` The widget's rate: one rate for every kilometre, set in Settings, $0.70 to start**
Shawn, once the widget was merged: "lets just use .70 for a blended rate", then, before that was built, "or let the person set it under the settings screen". Asked which, Shawn chose "Settings, starts at 0.70" over "Fixed at 0.70 in the app". It replaces the afternoon's decision on the CRA's rates (below): no tiers and no table of years, so nothing is added to MilO each January, and the look at canada.ca that entry asked for no longer matters. Phone only: the widget and its tile are the phone's, and the Android Auto screen shows no dollars.
- **Decided here, not asked:** the rate is in whole cents, from $0.01 to $5.00 (more is taken for a typo); it is typed in dollars, as Shawn wrote it (".70", "0.70", "$0.70"; a comma counts as the decimal mark); one rate for the whole year, so a change reprices the months already shown; and the widget names the rate it used, "at $0.70/km", where it said "at the CRA rate", because the figure is no longer the CRA's.

**`[CHANGE]` The widget's dollars at the rate set in Settings**
- **Rules:** `core/allowance/CraAllowance.kt` is now `BusinessAllowance.kt`. The two tiers, the rates by year and the "(2026 rate)" fallback are gone. `allowanceCents` prices tenths of a kilometre at one rate, rounded once; `parseCentsPerKm` reads what was typed and `formatCentsPerKm` writes a rate ("$0.70").
- **Stored** in the settings file in cents (`home_widget_cents_per_km`, `WidgetStorage.kt`), beside the widget's switch. Nothing is stored until a rate is set, and 70 is read until then. A value outside the range, which MilO cannot write, is read as 70, never used.
- **The widget** follows the stored rate as it follows the trips (`HomeWidget`), so a new rate redraws every widget at once. A settings file that cannot be read gives "Business dollars: not available" and an `ERROR` line, as trips that cannot be read do.
- **The tile:** "Rate" over "$0.70/km" on a `ValueButton`, the quiet button the edit screen draws a date with. A press opens a `TextEntry` with "Cancel" and "Save rate" in its place, as the odometer's tile does. Nothing was added to the design system; `ValueButton`'s description now says a press may open a field as well as a picker.
- **Words:** the widget's line ends "at $0.70/km"; "Business dollars: not available"; the tile's line is "The trip, Start and End, and business kilometres in dollars"; "About the widget" no longer names the 2026 rates. `widget_dollars_older_rate` is gone.
- **Tests:** `BusinessAllowanceTest` (7; it was `CraAllowanceTest`); `HomeWidgetContentTest` (two rewritten, and the test of a year without its rate replaced by two: another rate, and no rate); `HomeWidgetViewModelTest` (three new: a rate stored, refused, not stored); `WidgetStorageTest` (four new). The seven of `BusinessAllowanceTest` ran here, compiled with the pinned Kotlin 2.4.20 and warnings as errors, and ktlint 1.8.0 is clean on every changed file. The rest needs Android's libraries, which come from `dl.google.com`, blocked in this environment, so CI is the first to compile it. Nothing has been drawn: device checks W-1 and W-4 changed, W-13 and W-14 are new.

**`[DEBT]` The widget's rate is not in "Export all data"**
Left out on the same terms as the odometer readings and the parked limit (2026-10-07; 2026-10-06 (evening)): adding it changes the export file's form. Android's backup carries it with the settings file; an import from a file leaves this phone's rate as it is. Cost to fix: nothing beyond the one new format version those two need. Tagged in `TransferStorage.kt`.

### 2026-10-07

**`[CHANGE]` A home-screen widget: the trip, Start and End, and business dollars at the CRA rate** (ADR-002, amendment 35)
Shawn: "add the ability to add a widget to the home screen with the dollar amount for business activity. this is just for reference. please use the cra value for this number. add the ability to turn it on and off in app", then "add the ability to view the current trip in this widget and start stop a trip". His answers: the dollars for "This month and this year"; the rate "Built in, updated each year"; the switch for "The whole widget"; End "No, end at once". Phone only by nature: Android Auto has no widgets.
- **Reused, not rebuilt.** The widget's status line, trip figures and button are the Android Auto screen's (`carScreenContent`, `differsOnlyInTripFigures` and its 15-second gap), so the two small screens of the trip cannot say it differently. The kilometres are `categoryTotals`, as on Trips and the report. Start is the notification tap's way into the trip service; End is `MANUAL_END` through a receiver that holds the broadcast, as the manifest receivers do. The tile is made of the Settings screen's existing parts (`TitledSwitchRow`, `TileButton`, `QuietExpander`, `Note`, `StatusRow`), drawn like the trip-start sound's.
- **No new library.** Plain `RemoteViews` and `AppWidgetProvider`, not Jetpack Glance: a widget this small does not need Glance's Compose runtime, and it would have been a dependency to keep current for one tile. The widget's six colours are written in `colors.xml` because the home screen draws it outside Compose; `WidgetColorsTest` keeps each equal to its theme role, as `NotificationAccentTest` does for the notifications.
- **"The whole widget" is a component state.** Off: every widget is redrawn as switched off, without a button, and then `HomeWidgetProvider` is disabled, which takes it out of the phone's list. The redraw comes first because a home screen that keeps a removed widget keeps its last drawing, and that drawing's Start would still reach the trip service. Android deletes a disabled provider's widgets, so "one already on the home screen says it is switched off" holds only for as long as the home screen keeps it; the tile says so ("says so, or is removed by the home screen").
- **The tile asks the home screen, not the widget, whether the button can be offered.** It is stored before it is applied, so asking the widget whether it is on would read the switch before it had been applied.
- **Not in the export file,** on purpose: the widget is this phone's home screen's. Android's backup carries the switch; it is applied again at every process start.
- Tests: `CraAllowanceTest`, `HomeWidgetContentTest` (13 tests between them, run on this machine with the Kotlin compiler against stand-ins for the Android-only types; all pass), `HomeWidgetViewModelTest`, `WidgetStorageTest`, `WidgetColorsTest` (in CI only: they need the Android libraries). **Nothing of the widget has been drawn anywhere:** there is no Android SDK on the machine it was built on. Device checks W-1 to W-12.

**`[DECISION]` The CRA's per-kilometre rates, built in by year**
The figure is the "reasonable per-kilometre allowance" the Department of Finance sets each year for the provinces: **2026: 73¢ for the first 5,000 km, 67¢ after** (announced 2026-01-14; one cent up on 2025's 72¢ and 66¢; 2024 was 70¢ and 64¢). The territories add 4¢; Alberta is a province, so that rate is not used. canada.ca could not be read from the build machine, so the 2026 figures were taken from the announcement's title and two reports of it: https://www.canada.ca/en/department-finance/news/2026/01/government-announces-the-2026-automobile-deduction-limits-and-expense-benefit-rates-for-businesses.html, https://news.bloombergtax.com/daily-tax-report-international/canada-mof-announces-2026-automobile-deduction-limits-expense-benefit-rates-for-businesses, https://www.ifebp.org/resources---news/news-and-regulatory-updates/regulatory-updates/2026/01/15/department-of-finance-announces-2026-automobile-deduction-limits. Worth a look on canada.ca from the phone or the Mac.
- **Why built in:** Shawn chose it over a rate typed in Settings. The cost is one line in `CRA_RATES` each December or January; until it is added, a new year is priced at the newest rate and the widget says which year's.
- **What the figure is not:** the allowance is what an employer can pay tax-free per kilometre. What a self-employed person deducts rests on actual costs and the business share of the driving. Hence "for reference", and the kickoff's "No money on the report" stands for the report.
- **How it is worked out:** the tiers count from the 1st of January, so the month is priced where it falls in the year (the kilometres of the months before it decide its tier); each figure is rounded once, to whole dollars.

**Superseded the same evening:** one rate for every kilometre, set in Settings, $0.70 to start (2026-10-07 (evening)). The tiers were never on the phone.

**`[FINDING]` The event log explains trip 12: a hop of under a minute fell between two fixes of the wait**
Shawn sent the event log (`MilO-log-2026-10-07.txt`, 674 lines). Trip 11 ended at a client's site and the wait began at 12:07:55, one fix every 30 seconds. At 12:16:08 the phone reported getting into a vehicle (the engine running, the truck still standing); at 12:18:34 the first fix that showed the truck away found it already at the shop, 513 m on; "GPS: the truck moved" at 12:19:03; "left a vehicle" at 12:19:16. No "Location has not been available" line: nothing failed, the hop was shorter than the gap between two fixes. The log also confirms the morning's diagnosis from the export: at 11:13:19 the phone reported getting into a vehicle, at 11:13:22 the minute check found the parked limit passed and closed trip 9, at 11:13:50 MilO was opened and at 11:13:53 Start was pressed. And trip 11's walking start: "GPS: the truck moved" at 11:45:27, the report of getting into a vehicle only at 11:53:43.
- **Under the speed rule such a hop starts no trip of its own:** no fix sees it at 15 km/h. Shawn was asked whether a fix 250 m from the parked place while still connected (further than Bluetooth reaches) should start a trip as well, and chose "No, speed only". The hop is then counted at the start of the next drive.

**`[CHANGE]` After a report of getting into a vehicle the wait reads GPS every 5 seconds** (ADR-002, amendment 34)
His choice the same day: "Yes, every 5 s", for the ten minutes after each report, also within the first hour. `ParkedGps` (core) gives the trip service two times, until when every 5 seconds and until when at all; the service picks the rate at each order, at each fix and when its timer runs out at the next change. The log line now says "read every 5 s for the next 10 min". A report from just before the wait counts (the 11:13 case). Tests: `ParkedGpsTest` (two new), `TripControllerParkedGpsTest` (two new, two extended); the core ones ran here, the rest in CI. Device check NL-123.

**`[CHANGE]` Trip-start sounds: a list of his own to choose from**
Shawn sent mario-1-up.mp3: "I'm wanting to add more sound files to choose from, please add this to the app." The file is Nintendo's recording, so it is not committed or bundled, by the rule set at the kickoff for the R2-D2 film clip; he chose "A list of my own sounds" over keeping one. Phone only. Each file he adds is copied as before and stays on a list in the settings file (`own_sounds`, `SoundListStorage.kt`); the one in use is still the two settings a trip start reads, so `TripStartSound` and the trip service did not change. A sound chosen before the list existed is read as its first entry, so nothing is converted. The tile shows the list as `ChoiceRow`s, the chirp first; "Use my own sound" became "Add a sound", and "Use the built-in sound" became "Remove this sound" (the chirp is a row now). A restore drops the list with the copies, which are kept out of every backup. No limit on how many: each copy is 10 MB at most. Tests: `OwnTripSoundTest` (four new, two changed: a new sound no longer replaces the one before), `SoundListStorageTest`; they run in CI. Device checks SL-1 to SL-4.

**`[CHANGE]` Start pressed beside the parked truck begins the trip at the parked spot** (ADR-002, amendment 33)
From the missed trip's diagnosis (below): asked where a Start press during the wait should begin the trip, Shawn chose "At the parked spot". `ButtonRules.startByHand` passes `atParkedPlace` when MilO is watching the parked truck, and the ledger hands such a trip the watch's points, as for a drive-off start, and keeps the watch's fixes through the end of the wait (`WAIT_ENDS_IN_A_TRIP`). `fromParked` keeps its meaning (the truck's movement started the trip: no sound, and amendment 31's removal), and a press is not that. Phone and Android Auto alike: the Start of both goes through the same trigger. The rule's 308 tests ran here; the controller's two tests in CI. Device check NL-122.

**`[DECISION]` The truck's odometer, reversing the kickoff's "no odometer readings"**
Shawn: "we should also track vehicle mileage, have a way to custom adjust it too. This should also show monthly start and finish mileage on the vehicle's report to the accountant." Asked four things, he chose: "I enter it, MilO adds trips" (over typing it at the start and end of each month); "Every truck trip" moves it (Business, Personal and unsorted, recorded with the truck connected or added by hand; over Business only, which would fall behind by his personal driving); "Start and end, marked if estimated" on the report (over printing only readings typed that day); and "Settings, truck tile" for entering it (over Home and the Report screen). Phone only.

**`[CHANGE]` The odometer: a Settings tile, and its start and end on the report**
- **Rules** (`core/odometer/Odometer.kt`, pure): the figure at a moment is a reading plus or minus the truck trips that started between the two. A trip counts by its start, as everywhere, so a reading typed just after parking holds the trip still open. A reading followed by another with no truck trip between is taken for a slip the later one corrects; of the rest, the one with the fewest truck kilometres between it and the moment is used. A figure is "as typed" only when it is a reading typed that day with nothing driven between; otherwise it is an estimate.
- **Why the settings file and not a table:** a handful of readings a year, and a table would have meant database version 6 with its migration and a schema file that cannot be generated in this environment (no Android SDK). The settings file is backed up by Android with the rest.
- **The tile stands under the truck's, full width,** not inside it: the truck's tile is a half tile beside the driving alert's switch, and a figure, a line and a second button would have stretched both. The tile reuses the screen's parts (tile, label, `FigureText`, note, `TextEntry`, `TileButton`); nothing was added to the design system.
- **The report:** two figures on a tile like the sender's, under it; "est." on a worked-out figure and one note that says what it means. With no reading there is no tile, so a report looks as before until the first reading.
- Not run anywhere: device checks OD-1 to OD-6. The core rules and the report's tests ran here (476 core tests with the PDF's); the storage, the tile's state and the trip rule run in CI.

**`[DEBT]` Odometer readings are not in "Export all data"**
An export file is versioned (`data/transfer/ExportFormat.kt`) and adding the readings changes its form, which needs a new format version and a reader for both, as for the parked limit (2026-10-06 (evening)). Android's backup carries them with the settings file; an import from a file leaves this phone's readings as they are. Cost to fix: one format version for both gaps together. Tagged in `TransferStorage.kt`.

**`[CHANGE]` Beside the parked truck GPS runs for the first hour, then the phone's motion sensor turns it on** (ADR-002, amendment 32)
Shawn: "is there a way to disconnect the app from Bluetooth if inactive for like 1 hour … my car keeps connected for ever when off and in range of phone." Letting go of the Bluetooth link is not MilO's to do (Android owns it) and would cost the next automatic start; the battery's cost in a wait is GPS. He chose "GPS 1 hour, then sensor". `core/trip/ParkedGps.kt` gives the time GPS goes off: an hour into the wait, or 10 minutes after the phone's last report of getting into a vehicle, whichever is later; null (GPS throughout) when the phone does not report driving to MilO. The driving receiver hands a fresh "entered a vehicle" (at most 5 minutes old, `enteredVehicleAtMs`) to the trip controller before the alert judges it; the worker remembers it, also before a stored wait has been picked up, and passes the new time to the service with its order to watch (`TripRecorder.watchParked(checkAtMs, gpsUntilMs)`). The service turns location off when the time passes, by a timer and, if that is late, by the next fix, and writes one line; it turns it on again with a later time. Why the motion sensor rides on the driving alert's request rather than one of its own: the alert already asks the phone for exactly these reports, and a second request would have to be kept in step with the first on every arm, every switch and every permission change. The cost is that switching the alert off keeps GPS on beside the parked truck; the encyclopedia says so. What the report cannot do: start a trip. The fixes decide, by the speed rule, and the other-vehicle rule removes a drive in another vehicle. Not run on the phone; device checks NL-116 to NL-121. Unit tests: `ParkedGpsTest`, `TripControllerParkedGpsTest`, and new cases in `DrivingAlertRulesTest` and `DrivingAlertWatchTest`. The core tests ran here (459); the controller and alert tests only in CI, because the Android libraries cannot be fetched in this environment.

**`[FINDING]` After the first hour of a wait, a drive the phone does not report is missed**
With GPS off, the phone's "entered a vehicle" report is the only thing that turns it back on, and the truck reads as connected for the whole drive, so neither the minute reading nor the driving alert notices. How reliably HyperOS delivers those reports, above all to a MilO it has stopped, has never been seen (DA-8, DA-9, NL-119). If the phone shows that reports go missing, the two ways out are GPS for the whole wait (as before today) or a sparse fix every few minutes after the hour, which costs a little battery and catches a drive late rather than not at all. Shawn's to decide once NL-117 to NL-119 have run.

**`[FINDING]` The missed trip of 2026-10-07, read from the data export: the parked rule closed the trip in the moment the truck drove off, and Start was pressed before the watch could see it**
Shawn sent "Export all data" from the phone (format 1, database version 5, 12 trips, 4 518 points); the event log is not in that file. Times are Edmonton's. Trip 9 ended at 11:03:16 at a client's site, engine off and Bluetooth still connected; its points go on until 11:13:19, standing within 7 m of its end. The parked limit (10 minutes) ran out at 11:13:16. The last two fixes, at 11:13:14 and 11:13:19, had an accuracy of 64 m and so counted for nothing (rule 3: over 25 m), the second already 34 m from the end: the truck was pulling out. The parked rule closed the trip where it last moved, and the notification turned to "Truck connected and parked". At 11:13:53, 34 seconds later, Shawn pressed Start (trip 10, `MANUAL`, truck seen); its first fix, at 11:13:54, is 244 m from the parked place at 66 km/h. Those 244 m are lost: a Start press starts where it is pressed. None of the hypotheses below was it: the wait began, and the service was up.
- **What the builds since change.** With the speed rule (amendment 31) the first fix of the wait, 30 seconds after the close, shows 66 km/h away from the place, and the trip starts at the parked place; no press is needed. Shawn chose the same day that a press of Start during the wait also starts at the parked place ("At the parked spot"), so the stretch before a press is no longer lost either.
- **Trip 11 was the walking start the speed rule is for.** The watch began at 11:30 at the shop; at 11:44:59 a fix 25 m from the truck at 0.0 m/s, then 47 m at 0.4 m/s, two in a row: a trip, though the first fast fix of it is at 11:54:00.
- **Trip 12 is a gap this export cannot explain.** The truck stood at a client's site from 11:57:48; the wait began at 12:07:54. The next stored fix, at 12:18:34, is 513 m away at the shop with the truck standing, so the drive back is one straight line dated 12:18:34, with no fix from it. Either no usable fix came during the drive (HyperOS holding back location for a service with the screen off, or a slow first fix), or the drive itself took place at 12:18. The event log has the "Location" lines that tell which; it was asked for again.

**`[FINDING]` A trip was missed after a stop: the truck stayed connected, and nothing started the next trip**
Shawn, in the truck: "Today I started a trip, went to a location and the trip ended. Then about 15 minutes later I got in the truck, the phone was connected to the car still, but the new trip didn't auto start." Asked how the first trip ended, he said "Engine off"; and later: "it seems my car keeps connected for ever when off and in range of the phone". So the parked rule most likely closed the trip after 10 minutes of standing, MilO began to wait beside the truck (ADR-002, amendment 28), and the watch did not start the next trip when the truck drove off. That is the first time the wait met a real truck: its device checks 265 to 290 had not run. **Why it did not start is not known yet.** The event log of that day says what MilO decided at each step and was asked for; it had not arrived when the changes below were made, and none of them claims to be the fix. Candidates to read the log for: the wait never began (the reading of the truck said "not connected" at the close); the service watching was stopped by HyperOS; the watch's fixes did not come, or came far apart; the truck's radio dropped as the engine was cranked, which ends a wait at once, and the new link that followed did not reach MilO.

**`[CHANGE]` A parked truck drives off by speed, and a drive in another vehicle is deleted** (ADR-002, amendment 31)
Two of Shawn's decisions the same day, asked after the missed trip. Phone and Android Auto alike: the trip rules are shared, and neither screen changed.
- **Driving off is a usable fix away from the parked place at 15 km/h or more** ("Speed over 15 km/h", over "speed or distance" and over the distance rule as it was). The phone's own speed reading had been stored with every fix "because it is the best evidence for tuning the parked-truck rule later"; this is the first thing that reads it (`TrackPoint.speedMetresPerSecond`). One fix with a speed reading is enough; one without is judged by the old two-fix rule plus a speed worked out from the step. The trip starts at the parked place, as it did, and is now dated at the first moving fix of the last two minutes, so a truck that rolled on a few metres and stood an hour is not given that hour (until now it was dated at the first fix "away", which a rolled-on truck could be for an hour).
- **A trip the watch started that loses the truck for good under 1 km is deleted, row and points** ("1 km", "Delete for good"). The first deletion of a trip row in MilO: until now a row was never removed, only marked (`Trip.kt` says so, and now says what the exception is). Two narrow queries do it, each matching only what it is for: the open trip by id, and that trip's points. Which open trip the watch started is kept in the settings file (`driven_off_trip_id`), not in the trips table, so no database migration was needed; it is left out of exports, as the wait is, and forgotten after a restore.
- **Tests.** Six of `ParkedWatchTest`'s tests described the distance rule's behaviour that the speed rule changes on purpose: the crawl out of a yard (now not a start until it is fast), the poor fixes, and four about fixes that come late or far apart, which now carry a speed reading where they meant "the truck is driving", and keep none where they test the fallback. Seven new tests there, five in `AnotherVehicleTest`, five in `TripControllerAnotherVehicleTest`. The trip rules' 304 tests ran here, compiled with the pinned Kotlin from Maven Central; the controller's tests need Room, DataStore and Play services, which Google's Maven serves from `dl.google.com`, and that host is blocked in this environment, so they ran in CI only.
- **Asked and decided the same day, built next:** "GPS 1 hour, then sensor" for the wait (his question: "is there a way to disconnect the app from Bluetooth if inactive for like 1 hour, I don't know if I want to kill the phone battery"). MilO cannot drop the phone's Bluetooth link, and must not: the next trip's start depends on it. What the wait costs is its GPS fix every 30 seconds for up to three days. And an odometer for the report, which reverses the kickoff's "no odometer readings".
- **Documents:** ADR-002 (amendment 31), APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (rule 15, its gotchas, the code and the tests), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-113 to NL-115).

**`[CHANGE]` The report PDF in the app's look, with the Business and the Personal kilometres at its top**
Shawn's request, in his words: "when submitting the mileage to accounting can we make the layout nicer. Please put some thought into this. I want the logo and app name at the top. Also at the top it should show business and personal mileage separate. Please make it similar to the app design and beautifully designed, not some generic PDF." Phone only: the Android Auto screen makes no report. Not asked, so not changed: which trips are listed (Business only), the figures and how they add up, where the pages break, the CSV, the file names, the email.
- **What the first page looks like now.** A dark tile with the app's mark (the lime square and "M"), "MilO" over "Mileage report", and the period in large type over the day it was made; two tiles side by side, "Business" on the lime and "Personal" on grey, each with its kilometres in large figures and its trips; and a grey tile with Name, Company and Vehicle side by side. The days stand on grey bars, the trips under small grey column titles with fine rules between them; the total is on a dark tile with its figure in lime; every page's last line starts with a small lime "M". All in Sora, the app's typeface, in the four weights the screens use. Before: black Android sans-serif, a title, five lines of details, rules.
- **Personal kilometres are a new figure on the report,** and the only new number. They are what the period's counted Personal trips add up to, each rounded to a tenth first, as every figure in MilO is: `ReportSelection.personalTenths`, from the same selection that counts the Personal trips the Report screen already said were left off. The report lists none of them. A trip not yet marked Business or Personal is in neither figure, as on Trips.
- **Paper, not a dark page.** The screens are dark; a PDF that is printed, and read by an accountant, is white with dark words, and the app's dark is kept for the two tiles that frame the report, its top and its total. Four colours are the theme's own (page, accent, and the two colours of words on a dark tile), repeated in `ReportInk` because the PDF is drawn outside Compose; `ReportInkTest` keeps them equal to the theme. Three are made for paper: a quiet grey for words (5.9 to 1 on white), a tile grey, a rule grey.
- **The total is now "Total business kilometres for…"**, not "Total kilometres for…": with Personal kilometres on the first page, "kilometres" alone could be read as both.
- **The details lost two labels.** "Period" and "Generated" are no longer lines among the details: the period is the large type at the top, and "Generated October 7, 2026" stands under it. Their two strings are gone; four are new (`report_pdf_generated_on`, `report_pdf_business`, `report_pdf_personal`, the plural `report_pdf_personal_trip_count`).
- **`[FINDING]` Sora has no narrow no-break space** (U+202F), which Android puts before "a.m." and "p.m." in English times. Android draws that one character from the phone's own font, on the screens as in the PDF, so nothing shows; a renderer without that fallback draws an empty box (the drawing of the pages on a computer, below, had to put a normal space in its place). Sora has the ordinary no-break space and the ellipsis the footer uses.
- **What ran.** The layout pass is plain Kotlin, and was compiled and tested here with the pinned Kotlin 2.4.20 from Maven Central: the report's 84 unit tests pass: six are new (the two tiles of figures, the top tile, a period on two lines, the footer's mark), five were rewritten for the new top (the details in columns, the first page), and two had their numbers moved (a taller first heading leaves less room on page 1; a smaller footer type fits a longer name). `ReportSelectionTest` has one more, for the Personal figure. The pages were then drawn on a computer from that very layout, in static instances of Sora at the four weights, and looked at. **What did not run:** Android's drawing pass (`ReportPdf.kt`), the selection's new figure (`ReportSelectionTest`, which needs the app's Room classes), and the rest of the build: CI is the first to compile them.
- **Not known until the phone:** whether Android's PDF writer keeps the variable font's text selectable or draws its weights as shapes (device check NL-112), and how the lime prints.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the PDF, its required behaviour, the design system's note on the PDF), ARCHITECTURE (the typeface), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-109 to NL-112).

**`[CHANGE]` The app's mark on every screen and on the notifications, and Settings in the bottom bar**
Three requests of Shawn's, in one session. Phone only: the Android Auto screen already showed the app's icon and "MilO" in its header, and none of this is there. **None of it has run anywhere, and it was not compiled:** this cloud session's network policy blocked `dl.google.com`, so there was no Android SDK, and Gradle could not start either (no JDK 17). What ran: ktlint 1.8.0 (the pinned version, run by hand on every changed Kotlin file: clean), a rendering of the new notification icon, and a careful reading of the diff. CI, or `./gradlew spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest` on the Mac, is the first proof. On the phone: device checks NL-100 to NL-108.
- **The notification icon** ("the phone's notification icon for the app is the same one, M with green square"). Asked which shape, since Android paints a small icon in one colour of its own and only uses its shape, he chose "Square, M cut out" over the letter alone. `res/drawable/ic_stat_trip.xml`, the icon of all five notifications, was Material's navigation arrow; it is a rounded square (20 of 24 units, the mark's corner) with the launcher icon's "M" cut out of it by an even-odd fill, the letter scaled to 12 units wide so its strokes are not a pixel thin in the status bar. Every notification now also has the accent as its colour (`setColor`), which stock Android paints around the icon in the shade. The colour is written in `res/values/colors.xml`, beside the window's; `NotificationAccentTest` keeps it equal to the theme's accent, as `WindowBackgroundTest` does for the window. **What HyperOS shows in its shade is not known:** it is reported to draw the app's launcher icon there, which since this morning is the mark in full colour.
- **The top line on every screen** ("the top MilO icon, name and date on the top left of the screen stays on all interfaces… the button text is now on the top right for all interfaces, with the back arrow chevron to the left of that text"). `AppHeader`, until now Home's alone, is the first thing on all eight screens: the mark, "MilO" and today's date at the start, the screen's name at the end. On a screen opened from another (Setup, the pairing screen, the edit screen, the Report screen, and Settings when the Report screen opens it) a lime arrowhead stands before the name, and the two are one button that goes back, the way the square back button did. For the bar's four screens he chose the name without an arrowhead. `ScreenTitle`, `ScreenTitleAction` and `ScreenTitleWithLine` are gone. Trips' month steps and the Log's share button, which stood at the end of the old title, each stand on a line of their own under the top line; on Trips that line names the month, large.
- **Settings in the bar** ("the bottom button Setup I want to now be Settings. I want to remove the top Settings button on the home page and integrate the two buttons"). Asked how Setup should fit in, he chose a Setup tile at the top of Settings over the checklist on the same page and over a two-part switch. The bar is Home, Trips, Settings, Log; its third icon is the three sliders, and Setup's box with a tick is gone from `MiloIcons`. Home's square button is gone. Setup is opened from the tile and from Home's warning. The tile is Setup's own count tile with "Setup" and an arrowhead over it (`TileLinkLabel`, new in the design system), handed to Settings through a slot by the app, with Setup's own view model, because a feature never imports another one; "2 to fix" is red on it.
- **Two things that are less obvious than they look.**
  - `topLevelOf` now reads the screen directly on top of Home, not the nearest bar screen under the top. Settings is a bar screen that is also opened on top of the Report screen; read the old way, the bar would have jumped from Trips to Settings there. `MiloNavigation` gives Settings its way back only when it is not directly on Home.
  - `UnsavedWorkSaver` restores the bar button a question was about by its name. `SETUP` became `SETTINGS`, and `valueOf` on a name saved by the older build would have thrown while the screens came back, which takes the trip service down with them. An unknown name is read as Back now.
- **The date on every screen** is read by `AppHeader` itself each time MilO is looked at, as the Log already read "today": a screen has no view model of its own for it. Home's figures still go by Home's view model's day; both are read at the same moments.
- **The screen's name at the end is 17 sp, as "MilO" is,** not the 22 or 26 sp the titles were. With the larger size, "‹ Add a missed trip" beside "MilO" and the date would not fit on one line of a 360 dp wide screen.
- **Not done:** no screenshot, emulator or phone. The top padding of every screen is now Home's (8 dp), so the top line stands at the same height on each; the others had 10.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (Home's top line, Settings, the permission checklist, Trips, the Log, the design system), ARCHITECTURE (how the screens are reached), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-100 to NL-108, and the note on where Setup is).

**`[CHANGE]` MilO runs on Android 14 and newer only**
Shawn decided it on 2026-10-06. Asked which Android versions MilO should run on, he chose "Android 14 and newer (Recommended)". The reason: MilO runs on one phone, the POCO X5 with Android 14 (API 34), and every branch for Android 12 and 13 was code nobody could test. `minSdk` is 34; it was 31. `compileSdk` and `targetSdk` stay 37. Nothing of it is on a screen, on either surface. No dependency, setting or table changed. **It has not been on the phone:** device checks NL-95 to NL-99.
- **The rule that was followed.** A branch for "Android 13 or newer" or "Android 14 or newer" lost its condition and kept its newer side, as it was written. A branch for "older than Android 14" is gone. A branch for Android 15, 16 or 16 QPR2 was not touched. So what runs on the phone is what ran before.
- **What went, in `platform/bluetooth/`.**
  - `CompanionLink.kt`: Android 12's ways to list MilO's associations and to remove one (by address), and Android 12's callback for the consent dialog. An association now always has the number Android gave it. Watching for the truck keeps its two forms: by address up to Android 15, by a request from Android 16.
  - `TruckCompanionService.kt`: Android 12's two callbacks, which named an address only. Two shapes are left: the association (Android 14 and 15) and the presence event (Android 16).
  - `TruckBluetoothReceiver.kt` and `BluetoothSwitch.kt`: a receiver registered in code is always registered with Android's "exported" flag, and the device a broadcast is about is always read with the typed call. One lint exception that only Android 12 needed is gone.
  - `TruckSignals.kt` and `TruckPairingCheck.kt`: a companion callback always names its association, so the words for "no number" are gone.
- **What went elsewhere.**
  - `GeocoderAddressLookup.kt`: Android 12's blocking address lookup, and the helper that put a time limit around it. The lookup that answers on a listener is the only one left, and it is the one the phone always used.
  - `SetupFacts.kt` and `SetupRules.kt`: the Notifications row always offers Android's permission dialog. "Only in the settings" was Android 12.
  - Two values that were written out by hand because Android 12 did not have them are Android's own names again: the notification permission, and the consent dialog's result for "Don't allow". Each has the value it had.
- **Looked at and found without a branch on the version,** so nothing there changed: the start of the trip service and its type "location"; the two daily alarms (inexact, no permission); the backup rules; Back. The boot receiver's branch is for Android 15 and stays. Reading the truck with one call is for Android 16 QPR2 and stays.
- **The manifest.** No permission was removed: each one is needed on Android 14. None carried a version limit. Nothing but comments changed there, five of them: they described what Android 12 and 13 did, or said "from Android 13" or "from Android 14" of something that is now always so.
- **One thing left as it was, on purpose.** The request to pair still goes through the pairing screen's own Activity. Only Android 12 needed that. It is the call that paired the truck on the phone on 2026-10-06, so it was not touched.
- **Tests.** Two removed: both of `GeocoderAddressLookupTest`, which tested the time limit around Android 12's blocking lookup. Three rewritten for what is left: pairing when only the dialog's result has come (`TruckPairingTest`; the association has its number now), a companion callback that names the address and a number MilO has not stored (`TruckSignalsTest`), and the Notifications row (`SetupRulesTest`; its Android 12 half is gone). Two only renamed, because their names said "Android 12" or "Android 13". `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 2,062 unit tests and no lint finding. No lint check was switched off.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** The build before this one was installed fresh and a trip of 1.3 km recorded. This build was then installed over it, with no uninstall: the trip, the Log and the permissions were still there, and the Log said "MilO was updated". A second trip was started and ended with Home's button: the trip service was in the foreground with the type "location", the notification "Trip in progress" showed the km, 22 GPS points were stored, and both addresses were found. The pairing check listed an association made with adb and did not adopt it, as it should for a device that is not paired with the phone.
- **Not seen, and it matters.** An emulator has no truck, and this one is Android 16. The companion service's callback for Android 14 and 15 is the code this change touched most, and it has run nowhere since. On 2026-10-06 the phone logged that callback beside the Bluetooth receiver's lines, for a connect and for a disconnect ("First day in the truck", under that date). NL-96 asks whether it still does.
- **Documents:** ENGINEERING_STANDARDS (the project profile and the SDK levels), ADR-001 and ADR-002 (the lowest version, with the date), README (one sentence), ARCHITECTURE (a row in the stack table, the truck's stored fields, the note on pairing), APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the pairing and trip detection entry, the address lookup, the Notifications row, three lines under "Design system", the Report screen's "Period"), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-95 to NL-99, and one older note).

**`[DEBT]` Two ways to choose one of two, built side by side**
Settings and the Report screen were laid out at the same time on 2026-10-07, each on a branch of its own.
- **What was cut:** Settings brought `SegmentedChoice` (the design's two-part control in a dark container, for "Save as Personal / Ignore them"). The Report screen, which could not see it yet, chose its period with two `ChoiceButton`s side by side. `ChoiceButton` is right for the edit screen, where the design stacks "Business" over "Personal" as two buttons; on the Report screen's "Period" the segmented control is the design's own answer.
- **Cost to fix later:** small: the "Period" tile takes `SegmentedChoice`, and one screenshot is looked at again.

**Closed 2026-10-07 (the same day):** the Report screen's "Period" is a `SegmentedChoice`, as in Settings. "A whole month" and "A date range" stand in one dark frame, and the one in force is lime. The frame keeps 4 dp around the two parts, so the tile is 8 dp higher than it was. `ChoiceButton` stays for the edit screen's "Business" over "Personal", its one user. Phone only. Seen on an emulator (Android 16), both ways pressed; on the phone it is device check NL-99.

**`[DECISION]` The trip-start sound is played as an alarm, so that it is heard when the phone is quiet**
Shawn decided it on 2026-10-06, in his words: "Sound: Always play". He had missed the sound once because the phone was in Bedtime mode (2026-10-05, "First results from the phone"). Built on 2026-10-07. **It has not been heard on the phone since:** device checks NL-70 to NL-74.
- **What changed.** One line in `platform/trip/TripStartSound.kt`: the sound is handed to Android as an alarm (`USAGE_ALARM`), no longer as a notification sound. When it plays did not change. A trip start and the Play button of Settings go through the same player, so both changed.
- **What that means.** It follows the phone's alarm volume, not the ring and notification volume. Silent and vibrate do not mute it. Do Not Disturb and Bedtime mode let alarms through, unless they were set not to.
- **What still silences it.** A Do Not Disturb set to "total silence", or one whose list of what may sound has alarms switched off. And an alarm volume of zero, on a phone that lets it go that low: the sound is then played and not heard, and the Log still says "playing". MilO reads no volume and turns none up.
- **Why an alarm and nothing stronger.** It needs no permission. Going around a Do Not Disturb that silences alarms too would need the phone's "Do Not Disturb access". Turning the alarm volume up for him would change a setting of the phone that is his. Neither was asked for.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), with Play.** Android reported the sound as an alarm, playing and not muted, with the ringer on silent, with it on vibrate, and in Do Not Disturb "priority only" and "alarms only". In "total silence" Android muted it. The alarm volume there has a lowest step of 1: asked for 0, it stayed at 1, so zero could not be tried. Whether HyperOS lets the alarm volume reach zero is not known (NL-74). Nothing was heard: an emulator on a Mac says what Android does with a sound, not what a phone's speaker does.
- **Where it is said.** One grey line on the sound's tile in Settings. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Trip-start sound". Checks 15, 16 and 118 were written for the notification sound and are corrected. README's habit "Bedtime mode and Do Not Disturb silence the trip-start sound" is replaced by "Keep the alarm volume up".

**`[CHANGE]` Settings is laid out as the owner drew it, with a slider for the work hours**
Shawn's drawing of Settings is built as drawn: the same tiles in the same order, with the drawing's sizes, spacing, type, colours and words. Everything the screen could do before is still there. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. No setting, table or dependency was added, and nothing under `core/trip/`, `data/` or `platform/` changed but the one line of the entry above. **Nothing of it has been on the phone:** device checks NL-60 to NL-69.
- **Top to bottom:** "Settings" with the square back button. "Truck" beside "Driving alert". "Report for the accountant", four fields with grey example words. "Monthly reminder". "Trips · applies from the next trip". "Work schedule · trips in these hours are Business". "Trips outside the work hours". "Daily check". "Trip-start sound". "Backup, export and import". APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Settings", has each tile.
- **What MilO has and the drawing does not show** is kept, in the same style: the third number under "Trips" (how long the truck may stand still), the reminder's day, the tile of the daily check, "Use the built-in sound", and on the last tile the switch for the GPS points, the date of the last export, the safety copies, and what an export or an import came to.
- **The sentences that explain a tile are put away** behind one small grey line with an arrowhead at the end of the tile ("About the reminder"). They are the sentences the cards had. What has happened is never put away: a refused time or file, a change that was not stored, the last export.
- **The work schedule is still stored day by day:** for each of the seven days whether it is a work day, and its start and its end to the minute. The drawing shows one pair of hours for the whole week. So: while every work day has the same hours, one slider stands for the week, and moving a handle writes the hours of all seven days. When two work days differ, or when "Set each day by itself" is pressed, every work day has a slider of its own.
- **The slider** moves in quarters of an hour between 4 AM and 10 PM, and its two handles stay half an hour apart, as in the design's own script. A time can still be set to the minute: a press on the start or the end opens the time picker, as before. Hours outside 4 AM to 10 PM, which only the picker can set, are shown on a line of the whole day, midnight to midnight; a handle at its very end is 23:59. A time between two quarters stays as it is until its own handle is moved. So the slider can show every time the app can store, and with the picker every one can be set.
- **New shared components** (`core/designsystem/component/`): `SpanSlider` with `SpanScale` (the slider with two handles, and its arithmetic), `SegmentedChoice`, `ToggleButtonRow`, `QuietExpander`, `SwitchTile` and `TitledSwitchRow`, `TileButton`. **Small additions to older ones:** `StepperRow` is laid out as drawn (only Settings uses it); `ActionButton` can carry an icon and can wait; `TextEntry` can show example words; the drawing of `SwitchRow`'s switch is shared.
