**`[FIX]` The review of the report: six findings, one of them major; five acted on, one written down as debt**
Reviewers read the package below before it was committed. Each finding is listed with what was done. **No table, no migration and no stored value changed:** the database is version 5 and `5.json` is byte for byte what it was, so the emulator proof of the migration below stands and was not run again. No library and no permission was added, and MilO still has no INTERNET permission. Under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` and `platform/bluetooth/`: nothing. Device check 207 is new; item 14 of the order of checks and checks 181, 188 and 190 to 200 are rewritten.
- **The sending checks would have marked a real month as sent, for good (major).** They said to set the accountant's address to Shawn's own, to send last month's report, to answer "I sent it", and to send it again as Revision 1. A row in the list of sent reports cannot be removed (the `[DEBT]` entry below). Run as written, September would have read "Submitted … (revision 1)" with nothing in the accountant's inbox, and the first report the accountant really got would have been asked about as "Send this report again?" and arrived as "Revision 2": in its subject, its file name and on its first page. **The checks are rewritten so that nothing is recorded for a month with trips.** A paragraph above check 190 says what an "I sent it" costs. A month with real trips is answered "Not sent" every time, also when the test email was sent, because it went to him and not to the accountant. "I sent it" is pressed only for a **spare month**: one from before MilO was on the phone, with no trips, that will never be claimed. The first send, the revision and the date range (checks 193, 195 and 198) are done there and leave three rows that touch no real month; check 199 expects exactly those three. **Check 207 is new: the first real report,** with the real address back, which also shows that the checks before it left the month clean (no "Send this report again?"). The reviewer's other way, building the "Remove" button first, is the `[DEBT]` entry's own cost and is still not built.
- **The legend said of every marked trip that it "was not recorded automatically".** That is untrue of a recorded trip whose distance or time was corrected, and the accountant reads it. The legend is one string for both marks, so it now says what is true of both: "* This trip was added by hand, or changed by hand after it was recorded." One line of `strings.xml`; the PDF was made and read again (below).
- **A CSV of a period that was sent before was named as a revision.** The CSV was made from the report as the PDF would be made next, so after September was sent, Export CSV wrote `Mileage-2026-09-…-rev1.csv` and offered it under a title ending " - Revision 1": one number ahead of anything that was sent. **A CSV now carries no revision at all** (`ReportSources.reportFor`): it is the period's trips as they are now, it replaces nothing, and exporting it sends nothing. The reviewer's other choice, the number of the newest report that was sent, would have been wrong as soon as a trip changed after sending.
- **"Did you send it?" promised a revision a new date.** The question for a month said "the month is marked as submitted on that day" whatever was sent before; after a revision the month keeps its first date and only gains "Sent again on …". The question now has three texts, chosen by one pure function from the period and the list of sent reports (`sentEffect` in `data/report/Submission.kt`): a month never sent, a month sent before ("…it is added to the list of sent reports as Revision 1. The month stays marked as submitted on (date), the day its first report was sent."), and a date range. It is worked out for the report that was handed over, not for the period on screen.
- **"Not saved" could stand around the stored address.** The Settings screen's ViewModel remembered that the address had been refused, and the field kept the text; when the field was built again (the phone turned, or back from the pairing screen) it started from the stored address and was still drawn in red. **The ViewModel now keeps the refused text itself,** and the field starts from it: the text and what is said about it come from one value and cannot part. Chosen over clearing the red when the field is rebuilt, because that would also throw away what he was typing each time the phone is turned.
- **A month can be sent before it has ended, and a sent month that changes says nothing: not built.** The reviewer offered a question before sending and a line on the month card, or else a `[DEBT]` entry. Both are new behaviour on a screen whose behaviour Shawn settled at kickoff, so it is written down as debt (the next entry), tagged in `feature/report/ReportScreen.kt`, and said plainly in the encyclopedia.
- **Tests:** 1,168 in all (1,162 before). New: what one more report for a period would do, for a month never sent, a month sent once and twice, and a date range (3); that the question is about the report that was handed over (1); a CSV of a sent period is no revision while the PDF of it is, with the same trips (1); the refused address stands in its field with its refusal, and the stored one is never shown as not saved (2, replacing 1). The test of the legend now also has a report whose only marked trip was edited.
- **Seen on an emulator afterwards** (Android 16, API 36, `emulator-5736`, read-only, without a window or sound, started for this and shut down afterwards; every adb call through one script that refuses any serial that is not an emulator's; the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read; a fresh install, so the database was made at version 5 and no migration ran). Five trips were put into a copy of its database with `sqlite3` and the copy put back: three in October, one of them edited (its distance) and one added by hand, and two in September.
  - **The PDF, read.** October: "PDF created: 1 page." The file was pulled to the Mac (its SHA-256 equal to the emulator's own), drawn to an image with PDFKit and looked at, and its text taken out: the edited trip (18.4 *) and the added one (23.4 *) carry the asterisk, the recorded one (12.3) does not, the subtotals are 30.7 and 23.4, the total 54.1, and under "3 business trips" stands "* This trip was added by hand, or changed by hand after it was recorded." on one line, above the signature line.
  - **A month without trips can be sent,** which the rewritten checks rely on. August: "No Business trips in this period. The report would say so, with a total of 0.0 km."; a PDF of one page that says "No business trips in this period." and 0.0; Send opened Gmail; "Not sent" left August "Not submitted". A range of 1 to 2 August, picked on the calendar, was sent and answered "I sent it": listed with 0 trips.
  - **The question.** September, first send: "…If you sent it, the month is marked as submitted on that day…". Sent again as a revision: "…If you sent it, it is added to the list of sent reports as Revision 1. The month stays marked as submitted on Oct 6, 2026, the day its first report was sent…", and afterwards the card read "Submitted on Oct 6, 2026. Sent again on Oct 6, 2026 (revision 1)." For the range: "…A report for a date range does not mark a month as submitted…".
  - **The CSV.** With September sent once, and again with it sent twice, Export CSV offered `Mileage-2026-09-Sam-Driver.csv` in the share sheet, and the cache held that file beside `…-rev1.pdf`.
  - **The address field.** With `accounts@example.ca` stored and ".ca" deleted: red, "Not saved: …". With the emulator turned on its side and back, and after the pairing screen was opened from Settings and closed, the field still held `accounts@example`, in red. After Settings was left with Back and opened again it held `accounts@example.ca`, without red.
  - The table then held `MONTH` 2026-09 revision 0 and 1 and `RANGE` 2026-08-01 to 2026-08-02; `integrity_check` ok; twelve `REPORT` lines and no `ERROR` or `CRASH` line.
  - **Which build saw what.** All of it was seen with the build made from the code as it is committed. Only documents were written after it, and the gate was then run with a fresh JVM.
  - **Not seen:** any of it on the phone; a draft in Gmail (no account, as before; Gmail's screens were closed from outside to come back to MilO); the title the share sheet hands on with the CSV (the sheet shows the file's name); a screen reader.

**`[DEBT]` A month can be sent before it has ended, and MilO does not say that a sent month has changed since**
The Report screen opens on the month the Trips card shows, which is the current one unless Shawn stepped back, and "Send to accountant" asks nothing about days still to come. Sent on 28 October, the month reads "Submitted on Oct 28", and the trips of the 29th to the 31st are on no report until he sends the month again as a revision; nothing reminds him. The same holds for a trip added, edited, deleted or marked Business after its month was sent: the list keeps the figures that were sent, "In this report" shows what a report would hold now, and nothing compares the two. Found by the review; not built, because a question before sending and a notice on the month card are new behaviour that Shawn has not decided on. Cost to fix: a question before a month that has not ended is sent (one pure check of the period against today, one `ConfirmDialog`), and a line on the Report screen's month and on the Trips card when the Business trips or the total of a sent month differ from its newest sent row. The second catches a changed count or total, not an address or a time that was changed. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `feature/report/ReportScreen.kt`.
**Closed 2026-10-06 (phase 4, part A):** "Send to accountant" asks "Send before the month has ended?" for a month whose last day is today or still to come, and the Trips screen's month card says in red when a submitted month's Business trips or their total differ from its newest sent row, as costed here; an address or a time that was changed is still not noticed. The tag is gone from the code. Neither has run on the phone (device checks 227 and 229).

**`[CHANGE]` The report for the accountant (phase 3)**
Phone only. Run on an emulator (the finding below), never on the phone, and never with an email account. Device checks 177 to 206.
- **A Report screen** (`feature/report/`), opened with "Report for the accountant" on the Trips screen's month card, for the month the card shows. The period is a whole month, any month up to the current one, or a date range of two days picked on the calendar. A card says what a report made now would hold: how many Business trips and km, how many carry an asterisk or lack an address, and what is left off (Personal trips, trips not marked yet, the trip in progress). Then Create PDF (and Open PDF, to look at it), Send to accountant, and Export CSV.
- **The PDF** is made with Android's own `PdfDocument` and no library: US Letter; a heading with name, company, vehicle, period and the day it was generated; one table for each day with Start, End, From, To and km and a subtotal; the total for the period; a blank signature line; "Page n of N" on every page. No rate, no amount, no purpose, no odometer. A trip added or edited by hand has an asterisk and the report a legend. A trip without an address says "No address recorded", never coordinates.
- **Two passes,** as the research set out. `core/report/` is pure Kotlin and decides every word and where it stands: columns as wide as their widest entry, addresses wrapped inside their column, a day kept whole on a page, a day longer than a page continued under its heading repeated with "(continued)", no heading alone at the foot of a page, a subtotal with its day's last trip, the end of the report in one piece. `platform/report/ReportPdf.kt` only draws that, with the paints that also measured it.
- **Sending.** "Send to accountant" opens the email app with the accountant's address, the subject "Mileage - (Month Year) - (name)" (for a date range, the range in words) and the PDF attached. MilO sends nothing itself and still has no INTERNET permission. The file is in the app's cache and is read by the email app through a `FileProvider` that exposes that one folder, with a read permission given explicitly.
- **"Did you send it?"** Android cannot tell an app whether an email was sent. What was handed over is kept in the settings file, and when Shawn is back MilO asks. Only "I sent it" records the report. A tap beside the question puts it off; it is asked again. A report for a whole month is what marks the month as Submitted, with the date; a date range is listed and marks no month. Sending a period again warns first, is allowed, and is a revision: on the PDF, in the subject, in the file's name and in the list.
- **Submitted is not stored: it is worked out** from the list of sent reports by one function (`monthSubmission`), for the Trips screen's month card and for the Report screen.
- **The CSV** has a row of titles and one row for each trip of the same report, with the date, the two times, the two addresses, the km and whether the trip was added or edited by hand. Fixed formats for a spreadsheet, quoting as the CSV standard asks. It goes to Android's share sheet through the same file provider.
- **Settings:** a card "Report for the accountant", second on the screen: name, company, vehicle, and the accountant's email address. Each is stored as it is typed. An address is stored only once it is one address; until then the field is red and says it is not saved. A report cannot be made without the name or sent without the address, and the Report screen says so, with the way to Settings, before a button is pressed.
- **Storage: the main database is version 5.** One new table, `sent_reports` (`id`, `kind`, `firstDay`, `lastDay`, `sentAtMs`, `tripCount`, `distanceMetres`, `revision`). The step from 4 to 5 is one `CREATE TABLE`, the very statement Room would make the table with; it names neither `trips` nor `event_log`. `app/schemas/…MiloDatabase/5.json` is exported; `1.json` to `4.json` are byte for byte what they were, and in `5.json` the two older tables are what they are in `4.json`. No destructive fallback, as before. The settings file has ten new keys: four for the report's settings and six for the report that waits for its answer.
- **The event log has a new category, `REPORT`:** a file made, a report handed to the email app, and each answer. The constant arrives with database version 5, which no earlier build can open, so the rule about new constants (ARCHITECTURE section 6) is covered by the rule about versions.
- **One dependency, declared:** `androidx.core:core` 1.19.1, for `FileProvider`. The library was in the build already, brought along by others at 1.18.0; MilO's own code and manifest now name a class of it, so it is pinned in the catalog. 1.19.1 is the newest stable release (checked on Google's Maven index today) and the version ADR-001 pinned for the family. ADR-001 lists `core-ktx`; only `core` was taken, where the class is.
- **Design system, small and deliberate:** `TextEntry` can be told it holds an email address (the keyboard for one); `ConfirmDialog` can be told that a press beside it is something else than its second button (`onPutOff`); `core/designsystem/text/SubmissionWords.kt` says whether a month is submitted, for both screens. `core/util/TimeSpan.kt` got `daysSpan`, a run of days as a span of time.
- **Under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` and `platform/bluetooth/`: nothing.** The report reads finished trips and writes none.
- **Tests:** 195 new, 1,162 in all. The report's rules (78): a period's span at the edges of a month and of a range and over both clock changes, and how it is written; the rounding, subtotals and totals as sums of printed figures; every word of the PDF; columns and wrapping; the page breaks, with every length of a first day from 1 to 60 trips; the CSV with commas, quotation marks and line breaks; the subject for a month, a range and a revision; the file's name. Storage and its rules (41): which trips a period includes, the time zone, both clock changes; submitted and revision; the list of sent reports against a stand-in table; the report files against a real folder. The settings (20): which addresses are taken and which slips are caught; the ten keys read back, by name, and with nonsense in the file. The screen's logic (42): the period chosen, what the screen shows, the report a press would make, and what is recorded for each answer, failures of storage included. And 14 in existing files (the days span, the back stack, the migration, the Settings state, the shared words). **Fourteen deliberate faults were then put into the code one at a time; the tests caught thirteen at once, and the fourteenth after the test of "a day is kept whole" was given a case in which keeping it whole matters.**

**`[FINDING]` The migration to version 5 on an emulator, the PDF read page by page, the hand-over to Gmail, and what has never run on the phone**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, 1080 by 2400 px, `emulator-5720`, read-only, without a window or sound, shut down afterwards; every adb call pinned to it through one script that refuses any serial that is not an emulator's; builds signed with the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read). Its time zone was set to America/Toronto so that its clock stood inside the work hours. Two builds: version 4 from the commit this work started on (`d369dc8`, the APK of the gate run before anything was changed), and this change.
1. **Real rows.** With the version 4 build, six trips recorded with the Start and End buttons and GPS fixes fed from the console (948 m, 781 m, 43 m discarded as too short, 1,097 m, 691 m, 1,078 m). Then on Trips: one marked Personal by hand, one deleted, one edited (its distance set to 1.4 km), and a missed trip added by hand (23.4 km, "Shop" to "Site 7"). The database was copied off: `user_version` 4, `integrity_check` ok, 28 columns, Room's identity hash that of `4.json`, seven trips (five finished, one discarded, one deleted), 92 log lines, 51 raw points.
2. **Version 4 to 5.** This change's build was installed over it with `adb install -r` and MilO opened. No crash; Home showed the totals it had shown (26.8 km in 4 Business trips). The copy afterwards: `user_version` 5, `integrity_check` ok, the identity hash of `5.json`, `trips` still 28 columns with its index, `sent_reports` there with 8 columns and no row. **All 28 columns of all seven trips, every earlier log row and all 51 raw points compared equal to the copy before,** each value written out in full (SQLite's `quote()`), as text and by SHA-256. No status changed. **Run four times:** with the first build of this change; with the build the gate first passed; and twice more after later changes, the last time with the last build whose code changed, on an emulator started afresh, from the same copy put back (each file checked byte for byte after the write).
3. **From version 3, 2 and 1.** No build of those versions was made for this. A file of each was made with `sqlite3`: the tables created by the very statements in `3.json`, `2.json` and `1.json`, with that version's identity hash and `user_version`, and filled with the seven trips and 92 log lines of the copy of step 1, less the columns that version did not have. Each was put on the emulator and MilO opened. One start took each through every step: `user_version` 5, `integrity_check` ok, 28 columns, the identity hash of `5.json`, an empty `sent_reports`; the 21, 17 and 13 old columns of all seven trips and all 92 log rows equal by SHA-256; the catch-up of version 3 then sorted the trips that had no category. **What this does not show:** a file written by a real version 1, 2 or 3 build going to version 5. The phone holds version 3 or 4.
4. **An older build over the new database fails, and loses nothing.** The version 4 build installed over a version 5 database (205 trips, 169 log lines, three sent reports) died at its start with "A migration from 5 to 4 was required but not found". Every row of all three tables was equal afterwards, by SHA-256. This change's build installed again read everything, and the month's card still said it was submitted. The rule is in ARCHITECTURE section 6 and in "Before the first check" of the device checklist.
5. **A month for several pages.** 198 trips were inserted into a copy of the version 5 database with `sqlite3` and the copy put back: a September 2026 with 22 working days and a Saturday; one day of 70 trips, three minutes apart, which ran past midnight and so made the next day a long one too; addresses of every length, one of them a single word of 94 letters; two trips added by hand and eight edited; one trip with no address at all and one that had given up on its end address; and five that must stay off: a Personal trip, a deleted one, a discarded one, one that started in the last second of August and one in the first second of October. Trips showed "Business 2220.1 km, 193 trips"; the Report screen "193 Business trips · 2219.1 km", ten with an asterisk, two without an address, one Personal trip left off.
6. **The PDF, read.** Create PDF said "PDF created: 13 pages." The file (118 KB) was pulled to the Mac, its SHA-256 equal to the emulator's own, and every page drawn to an image with macOS's own PDF engine (CoreGraphics, by a short Swift script, because `sips` and `qlmanage -t` draw the first page only; `sips` drew page 1 as a cross-check). **What was looked at:** pages 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 and 13 one by one at 979 by 1267 px, and then all 13 pages of the PDF of the build the gate passed, four to an image, with page 1 on its own. The PDF of the last build is byte for byte the first of these.
   - Page 1: "Mileage report", then Name, Company, Vehicle, Period and Generated with their values in bold, "Business trips only. Distances are in kilometres.", a rule, and the first two days whole. The third day did not fit under them and starts page 2.
   - Addresses wrap inside their columns, up to four lines, and the row under them starts below the longest. The word of 94 letters is cut into three lines inside the From column and does not touch the To column.
   - The long day starts on page 5 under the day before it, and goes on at the top of pages 6 and 7 under "Tuesday, September 15, 2026 (continued)" and the column titles. Its subtotal (405.1) stands under its last trip on page 7. The next day begins on that page with its heading and two trips, and continues on pages 8 and 9. Pages 6 and 8 are full to the bottom margin and nothing runs into the footer.
   - "No address recorded" stands where an address is missing, three times. The asterisk stands after the km of ten trips and the legend on the last page.
   - Page 13: the last day (with a trip from 11:50 PM to 12:15 AM, under the day it started), its subtotal, a rule, "Total kilometres for September 2026" with 2219.1, "193 business trips", the legend, and a line over "Signature" with a shorter one over "Date". Every page ends "Sam Driver · September 2026" and "Page n of 13".
   - **The figures were checked apart from MilO:** the text was taken out of the PDF with macOS's PDFKit (so it is text, not a picture) and the 23 subtotals add up to 2219.1; a Python script worked the same days out of the database by the rule (finished, Business, started in the month, each trip rounded to a tenth) and got the same 23 subtotals and 193 trips. The trips of August and October are not in it, and no coordinate is.
   - **Changed after looking:** the thin rule above a subtotal stood directly under the last row's letters; it has room now. And, found by thinking and not by looking: a very long name with a date range could have run into the page number in the footer; the footer is now cut short with an ellipsis, and that was then seen on a PDF made with a name of 80 letters.
7. **The same PDF in an Android viewer.** "Open PDF" opened it in Google Drive's viewer, through MilO's file provider, under the name `Mileage-2026-09-Sam-Driver.pdf`, and it looked as on the Mac.
8. **The cache was being emptied.** The emulator's storage was 93 % full (415 MB free of 5.8 GB), and Android was emptying every app's cache: the first PDF was gone seconds after it was made, and the viewer said the file did not exist. MilO now looks for the file before it opens it and makes it again if it is gone. The emulator's low-storage threshold was then lowered to carry on. On a phone that is nearly full the same will happen, also to a draft that waits in the email app.
9. **Gmail: two things learned, and one thing not seen.**
   - With Gmail's package named on the request, Android did not open Gmail's email screen: it asked "Gmail or Chat?", with Chat first, because the Gmail app holds both. The request now names no package; it carries a selector for an app that takes a `mailto:` address, limited to Gmail first and to any app second.
   - With a selector, a request that says what kind of file it carries found **no app at all**, on both tries, and the screen said "This phone has no email app to hand the report to." Android matches the selector with the request's type, and no email app takes a `mailto:` address "of type PDF". The request now carries no type, as Google's own example does.
   - After both, the log showed the request started as `com.google.android.gm/.ComposeActivityGmailExternal`, inside MilO's own task, and Android's list of permissions showed Gmail holding **one read permission, for the one file**, owned by that screen.
   - **Not seen: the draft.** Gmail on the emulator has no account and showed its welcome screen. Whether Gmail fills in the address, the subject and the text and attaches the file has not been seen on any device. Google documents none of it (device check 190).
10. **The question, as first built, could be lost.** Leaving Gmail's welcome screen with Back took MilO's screen away with it; MilO started again on Home, and the question, which was kept with the screen, was gone, with no way to mark the month but to send again. **What was handed over is now kept in the settings file.** Seen afterwards, with Gmail's screens closed from outside to stand in for a sent email: the question asked the moment MilO was in front again, naming the month and the day; a tap beside it put it off; it came again after Home and back, and again when "Send to accountant" was pressed, before anything was sent; "Not sent" recorded nothing and the month stayed "Not submitted"; "I sent it" made the Report screen and the Trips card say "Submitted on Oct 6, 2026" and listed the report with 193 trips and 2219.1 km.
11. **A revision, and a date range.** Sending September again asked "Send this report again?" with the day, the trips and the km; Cancel opened nothing; "Send revision" made `…-rev1.pdf`, whose first page says "Revision 1. It replaces the report sent on October 6, 2026." After "I sent it" the card said "Submitted on Oct 6, 2026. Sent again on Oct 6, 2026 (revision 1)." A range of 3 to 4 September (14 trips, 172.5 km, the two days' subtotals of the PDF) was sent and confirmed: it is in the list, and October's card still said "Not submitted". The table then held three rows: `MONTH` revision 0, `MONTH` revision 1, `RANGE` revision 0, each with the first and last day, the trips and the metres. The range's two calendars were used as well: a first day picked after the last moved the last to it.
12. **The CSV.** Export CSV opened Android's share sheet ("Sharing 1 file", with Quick Share, Gmail and Drive). The file was pulled: it starts with the UTF-8 mark, its lines end as the CSV standard says, and Python's CSV reader read 14 rows of 7 cells, the addresses with commas and with quotation marks each in one cell, "added" and "edited" in the last column, and km that add up to 172.5.
13. **The settings.** With nothing set, Create PDF and Send made nothing and the card said what was missing; "Open Settings" led to Settings and Back led back to the report. The four fields were typed into over adb. `accounts@example` was outlined in red and not stored; with `.ca` added it was. **With the keyboard open, the red line under the field was hidden by the keyboard;** the red outline was in view.
14. **Which build saw what.** Steps 5 to 13 were seen with builds made before the gate first passed, each change of step 8 to 10 being seen with the build that made it. The build the gate then passed ran steps 2, 3 and 4 and made the 13 pages that were read four to an image. Three changes followed (a question still owed is asked before another report is sent; a file that cannot be made is caught whatever was thrown; the storing of an answer moved into one class with tests of its own), and the build with them ran step 2, a hand-over that was put off, answered "Not sent" and then "I sent it", and the PDF. The last change to the code was the footer. The build with it passed the gate and, on an emulator started afresh, ran step 2 once more, made the September PDF (byte for byte the one read in step 6) and the PDF with the long name. After that only the documents and four comments were written (the comments that spoke of the report as something still to come), and the gate was run a last time: 1,162 tests, no warning but Gradle's own notice about AGP, as before this change. Apart from the older build of step 4, which is meant to die, nothing crashed, and the logs that were read back had no `ERROR` line.

**What the emulator could not show, and what has never run on the phone:** all of it on HyperOS and on the phone's own database, which holds Shawn's real trips at version 3 or 4; **what Gmail does with the request** (step 9); which app HyperOS shows a PDF with; HyperOS's keyboard on the four fields and its calendar on the range; what HyperOS does to MilO while the email app is open; a real email arriving with its attachment; the CSV opened in a spreadsheet; printing; anything with a screen reader; and what the accountant makes of the report. Device checks 177 to 206.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the report**
All are Shawn's to change.
- **The report adds up the figures it prints.** Each trip is rounded to a tenth of a kilometre, and subtotals and totals are sums of those. Whoever adds a column up gets the figure under it. The screens round a sum of metres once, so the Trips screen's month figure and the report's total can differ: 2220.1 against 2219.1 km over 193 trips in the test data, which was made to round badly; a few tenths in an ordinary month. The other way round, the report would agree with the Trips screen and its columns would not add up.
- **A day is kept whole on a page when it fits on one,** and only a day longer than a page is split. That is how "a long day continues on the next page" was read. It costs paper: 13 pages for the test month, where filling every page would have taken about nine.
- **The email is recorded as sent on the day the email app was opened,** not on the day of the answer. The question can wait; the email did not.
- **A tap beside "Did you send it?" is not an answer.** "Not sent" is. The first puts the question off, the second ends it.
- **Sending a date range again is a revision too,** and warns first, like a month: the rule goes by the kind and the two days. A range of a month's first to last day is not that month's report.
- **A revision is named in four places:** on the PDF ("Revision 1. It replaces the report sent on …"), at the end of the subject (" - Revision 1"), in the file's name (`-rev1`) and in the list. The brief asked for "labelled a revision" and gave the subject without one; the ending is a string of its own in `strings.xml`.
- **The subject has plain hyphens,** "Mileage - October 2026 - Name", as the work order wrote it. The encyclopedia had dashes until today.
- **Create PDF needs the name; Send needs the name and the address; the CSV needs neither.** The work order asked that a report with either missing explains what is missing; a PDF to look at is not held up for an address it does not print.
- **The email has a first few lines** ("Attached is my mileage report for September 2026: 2219.1 km in 193 business trips." and the name). Nobody asked for them; an email with a subject and nothing else reads like a mistake. One string, and he can change the text before he sends.
- **The file is named by period and by name:** `Mileage-2026-09-Shawn-Kowalchuk.pdf`. The accountant gets the same report from other people.
- **Times on the PDF follow the phone's 24-hour switch, and dates its language,** like the Trips screen. The CSV does neither: year first, 24 hours, a dot.
- **In the CSV a missing address is an empty cell,** where the PDF says "No address recorded"; and there are no subtotals. A cell that would be run as a formula by a spreadsheet gets an apostrophe in front.
- **The four settings are stored as they are typed,** like every setting; there is no Save. Only the address has a state in which what is typed is not what is stored, and its field says so.
- **The check of the address is a sensible one, not the whole standard:** one "@", something before it, a domain of two parts ending in letters, and nothing else in the field.
- **The card "Report for the accountant" is second on the Settings screen,** because the Report screen sends him there and the schedule card is long.
- **The CSV goes to the share sheet, not to the accountant.** The brief asked for an export, and an export has no one place to go.
- **A trip past midnight is under the day it started,** with its end time and no note. A report's period is measured in the phone's time zone on the day the report is made.
- **The list of sent reports is on the Report screen,** all of it, newest first. There is no screen of its own.
- **Report files are kept seven days** and removed when the Report screen is opened. The research's figure.
- **`REPORT` lines in the event log** name the period and the figures and no person and no address.
- **The gate was run with the throwaway debug key,** and the Keychain was not read.

**`[DEBT]` A sent report cannot be taken back, and a wrong "Not sent" can only be put right by sending again**
The list of sent reports can be added to and read, and nothing else. "I sent it" pressed for an email that was then discarded leaves the month Submitted for good, and "Not sent" pressed for an email that was sent leaves it "Not submitted" until the report is sent once more, which puts a second email in the accountant's inbox. Neither has a button. A stray tap is covered (it only puts the question off); a wrong answer is not. Cost to fix: a "Remove" on a line of the list, behind a question, and a "Mark as sent" beside a month that was handed over and answered "Not sent"; one delete in the DAO and the rule for a month whose only report was removed. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `data/report/SentReportDao.kt`. Also not built: MilO does not say that a month's trips were changed after its report was sent. That has a `[DEBT]` entry of its own, above. Because of this entry, the device checks never answer "I sent it" for a month with real trips (checks 190 to 199 and 207).
**Closed in part 2026-10-06 (phase 4, part A):** each line of "Sent reports" has a "Remove", behind a question that says what removing that report does; one delete in the DAO; a month whose only report is removed is not submitted again, and one whose original is removed is submitted by its revision, which keeps its number. The tag is gone from the code. **Not built: "Mark as sent" for a wrong "Not sent"**, which has an entry of its own under today's date. The device checks still press "I sent it" only for a spare month, until Remove has been seen to work on the phone (check 228).

**`[FINDING]` The phone's own database converted cleanly, and HyperOS woke MilO by itself**
At 06:40 the build with addresses, delete, settings, the schedule and the Android Auto screen was installed over the first build, after copying the phone's databases and settings to `~/MilO-backups/` on the Mac. Read back over adb:
- **The conversion from version 1 to version 3 ran on the phone without error.** Compared before and after: both trips' original columns, all 65 earlier log lines and all 60 GPS points are identical, and both databases pass their integrity check. The schedule catch-up then sorted the two test trips.
- **HyperOS started MilO's process on its own, straight after the install,** and MilO logged "MilO was updated" before anyone opened it. That is the first proof on this phone that a broadcast can wake a closed MilO (with Background autostart on). It was the update broadcast, not a Bluetooth one, so the truck is still unproven.
- **A parked phone added no distance.** The second test trip of 5 October ran six and a half minutes with 59 fixes and was closed at 0 m.
- Still to come from the truck: pairing, an automatic start, and a real drive's distance.

**`[DECISION]` Shawn's standing permission to merge**
On 2026-10-06 Shawn said the assistant may merge its own pull requests once their check passes. Until then he had approved each one. PRs 7 to 10 were merged that way the same morning. A pending or failing check still blocks a merge, and the repo does not allow GitHub's auto-merge, so a PR merges when the assistant is next active after its check goes green.

**`[FIX]` The review of the edit screen: seven findings, all acted on**
Reviewers read the package below before it was committed and made seven findings, one of them major. Each is listed with what was done. **No table, no migration and no stored value changed:** the database is version 4 with `4.json` as it was, so the emulator proof of the migration below stands. No library and no permission was added. Under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` and `platform/bluetooth/`: nothing. Device checks 172 to 176 are new; 158, 159 and 163 are rewritten.
- **Leaving the form with something typed never asked (major).** Back is how a keyboard is put away, so one press too many threw the whole form away; the arrow and the bottom bar did the same. It had been written down as a decision (below), and it broke the design system's own rule that what takes something away asks first. **Reversed.** Every way out now asks "Leave without saving?", with "Keep editing" and "Discard": the phone's Back, the arrow beside the title, and each button of the bottom bar. A form that is as it opened, also one that was changed and put back, is left at once. Whether the form holds something to lose is a pure function (`holdsUnsavedWork`): it compares what is typed by what it means, so the distance the form showed typed again is nothing to lose, and letters in the distance field are.
  - **The question is the app's, not the screen's** (`app/UnsavedWork.kt`, asked in `MiloApp`). The reviewer proposed to catch Back and the arrow inside the screen. That leaves the bottom bar, which the screen cannot see. All three ways out are carried out by the app, so the screen only reports whether it holds unsaved work and the app asks before it carries any of them out. The question is saved with the screens, so turning the phone keeps it.
  - What is typed is still lost, without a question, if Android ends MilO in the background. Saving the form with the screen is a change of its own.
- **The form could not say which day a trip ended.** A trip that ran past midnight could not be brought back onto one day (an end of 17:40 meant 17:40 on the next day: refused as "in the future" until then, saved as a trip of over 24 hours afterwards), and a missed trip from 23:30 to 00:15 could not be added at all. The card "When" now has a switch, **"Ended the next day"**, and under it the day the end is on. **MilO still never moves an end to the next day by itself:** an end before the start is far more often a slip of the dial, so it is refused as before, and the refusal now names the switch. A switch and not a second date: one more calendar, and two dates to keep in step, for something that happens a few times a year.
- **A time typed in the hour that happens twice** (the night the clocks go back) always meant the first of the two, so a trip that ended at the second 01:30 and was given the end 01:40 was saved 50 minutes long where it was 110, with no warning. A typed time is now read on the side of the change the stored time is on (`ZonedDateTime.ofLocal` with the stored time's offset). A trip that is being added has no stored time and still gets the first of the two; a trip across that hour cannot be typed in exactly. In the encyclopedia.
- **A saved trip could be stored out of sight.** Trips came back on the month it had been showing, so a trip added for 29 September while October was on screen was stored, counted and nowhere to be seen, and a second attempt stored it twice. **Back from a save, Trips now shows the month the trip is in,** from its top (`monthOfSavedTrip`; the app carries the trip's start from the one screen to the other, because the two features do not know each other). "Restore recorded values" does the same. The empty form still opens on today whichever month is on screen: no day of another month is a better guess, and a trip filed under today by mistake is now in front of Shawn when he comes back.
- **The keyboard slid the whole window up,** MilO's first text fields having arrived with no keyboard handling: the form's text was drawn through the clock and the icons, and the title left the screen. `MainActivity` is now `adjustResize`, and `MiloApp` ends every screen where the keyboard begins (`imePadding`, after the bars' room is marked as used). The field that is typed into stays whole above the keyboard, and the form scrolls to Save with it open.
- **What a press on Save found wrong was listed above the button,** a screen away from what it named, the fault the review of the work schedule had fixed on Settings the same day. Each refusal is now said in red where it is put right: the times in the card "When" (a red `StatusRow` for each), the distance under the Kilometres field (`TextEntry` got an error line: red text, a red outline, and "in error" for a screen reader). The screen moves to the first of them, once for each press that stored nothing. Only a failure of storage is still said at the button. The design system's pattern for a form is reworded to match.
- **The calendar could not be used with the phone on its side:** Material's calendar has one height, and in a lower dialog the weekday letters were drawn over the first weeks and the last week lay under the buttons. `DateDialog` now lets the calendar scroll inside the dialog. Scrolling and not text entry, because typing a date is one more way to get a day wrong. The clock dial needed nothing: Material lays it on its side by itself.
- **Three device checks did not behave as written.** 159 asked for "ten times the real figure", which is under 180 km/h for a slow trip and would have been saved: it now asks for `999`, on a trip of under five hours. 158 left a real trip with a moved start and the edited mark, and was refused on a trip of today before 16:30: it now asks for a Business trip of an earlier weekday that was never marked by hand, and ends with "Restore recorded values". 163 compared a row with its copy from before, which cannot be equal in two columns: the restore hands the typed address back to the lookup, which counts its attempts afresh and stores when it looked. Those two (`addressAttempts`, `addressLastAttemptAtMs`) are now left out of the comparison; the reviewer named the second, the first follows from the same code. Checks 155, 160, 164 to 166 and 171 are reworded for the question and for where a refusal is said.
- **One file split for the size limit, with no change to what is in it:** the card "What MilO recorded" moved from `TripEditCards.kt` to `RecordedCard.kt`.
- **Tests:** 967 in all (932 before). New: a trip past midnight typed in, brought back onto one day and put back to the millisecond, a trip of today that cannot end tomorrow, the hour that happens twice from both sides (11); when the form holds something to lose, and that a stored form holds nothing (8); which card each refusal is said in and where the screen moves (6); which ways out ask, what "Keep editing" and a saved form do to the question, where each way leads (7); the month shown after a save (3).
- **Seen on an emulator afterwards** (Android 16, API 36, 1080 by 2400 px, `emulator-5762`, read-only, without a window or sound, started for this and shut down afterwards, every adb call pinned to it through one script that refuses any serial that is not an emulator's; the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read; a fresh install, so the database was made at version 4 and no migration ran).
  - **Leaving.** With "Shop" typed into From: Back put the keyboard away, Back again asked. "Keep editing" kept the text; the arrow asked; Back on the question kept the form; Log in the bottom bar asked, and "Discard" led to the Log. An untouched form was left at once, also after a field had been tapped and the keyboard closed. The question survived the phone being turned. With the question open and MilO's process ended in the background, MilO came back on the edit screen with the trip as stored and no question, and Back left at once.
  - **Refusals.** "Add this trip" on an empty form moved the screen up to "When", with "Choose when the trip started." and "Choose when the trip ended." under the switch, and the Kilometres field outlined in red over "Type the distance in kilometres." `999` for a trip of 20 minutes gave the red line about 180 km/h under the field.
  - **Past midnight.** Yesterday 11:30 PM to 12:15 AM was refused with the sentence that names the switch; with the switch on, "The end time is on Tuesday, October 6, 2026." and the trip was stored as 23:30 to 00:15 on the next day. Opened again, its end set to 11:50 PM and the switch off, it was stored as 23:30 to 23:50 on the one day, and the log line shows the end moving back a day.
  - **The month.** With October on screen, a trip added for 29 September brought Trips back on "September 2026" with the trip listed; the next one, for 5 October, brought it back on October. A recorded trip moved to 30 September by an edit came back under September, and "Restore recorded values" under October again, its row equal to the copy from before the edit in every column but the three "recorded" ones, which keep what was recorded.
  - **The keyboard.** Upright and on its side: the field typed into whole above the keyboard, nothing drawn through the status bar, and the form scrolled to Save with the keyboard open.
  - **The calendar on its side** scrolled inside its dialog from the first week to the last, and a day in the first week could be tapped. Upright it looks as before.
  - **Which build saw what.** All of the above was seen with the build made before the gate was run. One line was added after it: the start of a saved trip is handed to Trips only while the edit screen is still the screen on top. The build the gate passed was then installed over it and showed, once more, a trip added for 28 September bringing Trips back on September (60.5 km in two trips, the second typed as "18,5"), the question after Back and its "Discard", and an untouched form left at once by the arrow. No crash and no `ERROR` line in the log; `integrity_check` ok.
  - **Not seen:** any of it on the phone, with HyperOS's keyboard and font; the red line for a failure of storage at the button (it needs storage to fail); the hour that happens twice and a trip over more than one midnight (unit tests only); anything with a screen reader.

**`[CHANGE]` Editing a trip and adding a missed trip (the second piece of phase 2)**
Phone only. Run on an emulator (the finding below), never on the phone. Device checks 153 to 171.
- **Edit a trip.** A tapped finished trip on Trips now offers "Edit trip" above "Mark as …" and "Delete trip". It opens a screen of its own (`feature/tripedit/`): the date, the start and the end, the from and the to address, the distance in km, and Business or Personal. Nothing is stored until Save is pressed. A trip that is being recorded, discarded or deleted cannot be edited: the screen does not offer it and the write refuses it.
- **What the form refuses:** an end that is not after the start, a time in the future, a trip that would run into the one being recorded, and a distance that is no number, negative, over 2,000 km or further than 180 km/h allows in the time. Each is one red line above the Save button, and nothing is stored. (**Changed by the review entry above:** each is said in the card it is about.)
- **What counts as an edit:** a start, an end, a distance or an address that is different after the save. The trip is then marked. Business or Personal alone is not an edit; it is stored as set by hand, as on the Trips screen. A save writes only what was changed: an untouched time stays exact to the millisecond, so opening a trip and saving it changes nothing.
- **Restore recorded values.** The first edit keeps what MilO recorded (start, end, distance) on the row. The edit screen of an edited trip shows it and offers to put it back, after a question: the recorded times and distance come back exactly, an address Shawn typed is removed and looked up again, and the mark goes.
- **Add a missed trip.** "Add a missed trip" on Trips opens the same form, empty, on today's date. The trip is stored as a finished trip marked as added by hand, with no GPS points and no positions, so nothing is looked up for it. Its Business or Personal is chosen from the work hours once there is a start time, and can be changed. It is counted, marked, edited and deleted like any other.
- **Shown:** such a trip has an asterisk after its times on Trips and a line "* Edited by hand" or "* Added by hand". The month's and the day's figures, Home's Today and the Android Auto screen's Today count it like any finished trip, with the figures it has now; `isCounted` and `todayTrips` were not changed, and tests now say so.
- **A typed address is never overwritten by the lookup,** and neither is one Shawn emptied. Each end of a trip has a mark for it; the rule that decides what is due, the query and the update that stores an answer all carry it.
- **Business or Personal after an edit** (the rule the work order asked for, `refileTrip` in `core/schedule/TripRefiling.kt`): a category picked in the form is Shawn's and marks the trip as set by hand; a trip already set by hand keeps its category whatever happens to its times; otherwise a changed **start** is sorted again by the schedule as it is at the save. "Ran past schedule" is worked out again whenever the start or the end changed, and kept as stored otherwise. The form shows the result before Save is pressed.
- **The event log:** one `TRIP` line for every save that reaches storage, naming each value that changed, old and new, addresses included; one for a restore, one for an added trip, one for a save that changed nothing and one for a write storage refused.
- **Storage: the main database is version 4.** Seven columns on `trips`: the marks `addedByHand`, `editedByHand`, `startAddressByHand`, `endAddressByHand` (integers, 0) and `recordedStartedAtMs`, `recordedEndedAtMs` (integers, empty) and `recordedDistanceMetres` (real, empty). The step from 3 to 4 only adds them: no row is read or rewritten. `app/schemas/…MiloDatabase/4.json` is exported; `1.json`, `2.json` and `3.json` are byte for byte what they were. No destructive fallback, as before. The settings file is unchanged.
- **Changed under `platform/address/`** (not the trip engine): `AddressRetry.kt` (an end whose address is Shawn's own is never "being looked up"; a fourth thing a row can say, "left empty by hand"), `TripAddresses.kt` (one line: such an end is not asked about) and `AddressLogText.kt` (the words for it).
- **Under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` and `platform/bluetooth/`: nothing.** No enum there got a value: a trip added by hand is stored with the existing start cause `MANUAL` and told apart by its own column.
- **Design system, two deliberate additions:** `DateDialog` (Android's calendar in a dialog; it wraps Material 3's date picker, which the pinned Material release marks as experimental like the time picker, and the opt-in is in that one file) and `TextEntry` (a labelled one-line field; MilO's first). And one new pattern, written down in the encyclopedia: a form that is stored as a whole, with a Save button and its refusals above it. (The review entry above moved the refusals beside what they name.)
- **Two files split for the size limit, with no change to what is in them:** the screen keys and the back-stack functions moved from `app/MiloNavigation.kt` to `app/MiloBackStack.kt`, and the Trips screen's preview to `TripsScreenPreview.kt`.
- **No dependency and no permission was added.**
- **Tests:** 160 new, 932 in all: the rule for Business or Personal after an edit (18); what counts as an edit and what is kept (15); what a restore puts back and for which trips (10); the log lines with old and new (11); the form as values, an untouched form asking for nothing, typed distances and addresses, both clock changes (21); every refusal at its edge (20); what the screen shows, and that the category it shows is the one a save stores (16); saving, adding and restoring against the stand-in trips table through the repository, refusals and failures of storage included (21); the lookup keeping away from an address of Shawn's own, and the query agreeing with the rule in Kotlin (8, and 2 in an existing file); the mark on a row and the totals (9, and 1 for the new words); the calendar's day arithmetic (3); the back stack with the edit screen (4); the migration, now with the step to version 4 (1).

**`[FINDING]` The migration to version 4 on an emulator, from versions 3, 2 and 1, the edit screen there, and what has never run on the phone**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, `emulator-5746`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it through one script that refuses any serial that is not an emulator's, shut down afterwards), with builds signed by the throwaway debug key. Two builds: version 3 from the commit this work started on (`c438ef1`, the APK of the gate run before anything was changed), and this change.
1. **Real rows.** With the version 3 build, six trips recorded with the Start and End buttons and GPS fixes fed from the console: 1,213 m in Edmonton time at 05:19 (Personal), then, with the emulator's time zone moved so that its clock stood inside the work hours, 1,663 m, 109 m (discarded as too short), 885 m (then marked Personal by hand), 774 m (then deleted on Trips) and 1,329 m. The finished ones got their addresses. The database was copied off: `user_version` 3, `integrity_check` ok, 21 columns, Room's identity hash that of `3.json`, six trips (four finished, one discarded, one deleted, one of them set by hand), 97 log lines, 64 raw points.
2. **Version 3 to 4.** This change's build was installed over it with `adb install -r` and MilO opened. No crash; Home showed the totals it had shown. The copy afterwards: `user_version` 4, `integrity_check` ok, 28 columns, the index, the identity hash of `4.json`. The 21 old columns of all six trips and all 97 old log rows compared equal to the copy before, as text and by SHA-256. On every row the four marks are 0 and the three "recorded" columns empty. No status changed, and the 64 raw points are as they were. **It was then run twice more, the last time with the build that passed the gate,** from the same copy put back on the emulator (each file checked byte for byte after the write): the same result each time, row for row and hash for hash.
3. **From version 2 and from version 1.** No build of those versions was made for this. A version 2 and a version 1 file were made with `sqlite3` instead: the tables created by the very statements in `2.json` and `1.json`, with that version's identity hash and `user_version`, and filled with the six trips and 97 log lines of the copy of step 1, less the columns that version did not have. Each was put on the emulator and MilO opened. One start took each through every step: `user_version` 4, `integrity_check` ok, 28 columns, the index, the identity hash of `4.json`; the 17 (and the 13) old columns of all six trips and all 97 log rows equal by SHA-256; the catch-up of version 3 then sorted all six. Both were run again with the build that passed the gate, with the same result. **What this does not show:** a file written by a real version 1 or 2 build going to version 4. Those were taken to version 3 by real builds earlier today (below); the step from 3 to 4 is what is new.
4. **An older build over the new database fails, and loses nothing.** The version 3 build installed over a version 4 database that held an edited and an added trip (eight trips, 130 log lines) died at its start with "A migration from 4 to 3 was required but not found". Every row of both tables was equal afterwards, by SHA-256. This change's build installed again read everything and wrote the crash to the log. The rule is in ARCHITECTURE section 6 and in "Before the first check" of the device checklist.
5. **An edit.** A recorded trip (12:24:36 to 12:25:35, 1,329 m, both addresses found) was opened from Trips. Its start was set to 12:10 on the clock dial, its distance to 2.5 and its From address to two typed words; the card for Business or Personal changed to "Chosen by MilO from your work hours and the start time." After Save, Trips showed "12:10 PM – 12:25 PM *", the typed address, "Business", "* Edited by hand" and 2.5 km, and the month's Business figure had grown by 1.2 km. In the copy: the start 12:10:00.000, the end untouched to the millisecond, 2,500 m, `editedByHand` 1, `startAddressByHand` 1, and the three "recorded" columns holding the start, the end and the 1,328.80344780629 m of before. One `TRIP` line, with each old and new value. Emptying the To field and saving gave "… → no address entered"; the Trips screen then asked for address passes twice and the trip was not touched and has no `ADDRESS` line.
6. **Restore recorded values.** The card "What MilO recorded" showed the day, "12:24 PM – 12:25 PM" and 1.3 km. Cancel in the question changed nothing. After Restore the row had no asterisk; in the copy its start, end, distance, positions, category and flags were equal to the copy of step 1, both marks for the addresses were 0, and both addresses had been looked up again and were the ones it had before. The log: the restore line, then "Trip 6: start address found; end address found."
7. **A missed trip.** The empty form opened on today; "Add this trip" at once gave three red lines. The calendar opened on today with every later day grey, and a tap on tomorrow chose nothing. With yesterday, 9:00 to 9:40, two typed addresses and "234" for the distance: refused, "more than 180 km/h on average". With "23.4" the line went away and the trip was added: "9:00 AM – 9:40 AM *", "Shop → Site 7", "Business", "* Added by hand", 23.4 km, under yesterday's date, and the month's Business figure up by it. The row: `FINISHED`, `MANUAL`, no positions, both address marks 1, `addedByHand` 1, and no raw point. Opened for an edit it said that MilO has no recording of it and offered no restore; with its distance changed and Personal chosen it still said "* Added by hand".
8. **The other refusals and the rest.** With a trip being recorded since 12:40, an earlier trip's end set to 12:45 was refused twice over while 12:45 had not come ("in the future" and "a trip is being recorded right now"), and once it had passed by the second alone; the recording went on. Business or Personal alone changed in the form left the trip without an asterisk and its log line says so. Typed text survived the phone being turned twice. With the edit screen open and MilO's process killed in the background, MilO opened on the edit screen again, for a stored trip and for the empty form, with no crash and with the trip as stored.
9. **Which build saw what.** This change was built three times while the above was done. Steps 5 to 8 were seen with the first or the second build. What the last one has more: the two repairs of step 10, a log line that is written after its write and outside the guard around it, and a guard for a stored distance that cannot be written as kilometres. The last build, the one the gate passed, ran step 2 and step 3 again, and one more edit and restore of a trip, after which every trip compared equal to the copy of step 1.
10. **Two defects found there and fixed.** Three new strings had lost their quotation marks and two their line breaks: an unescaped `"` and a real line break inside `strings.xml` are dropped by Android, and the screen showed it. And a refusal pushed the Save button under the edge of the screen, because only the red lines were brought into view; the lines and the button are now brought into view together, once for each press that stored nothing. (**Changed by the review entry above:** the lines stand in their cards, and the screen moves to the first of them.)

**What the emulator could not show, and what has never run on the phone:** all of it on HyperOS, and on the phone's own database, which holds Shawn's real trips at version 3 or lower; the phone's keyboard in the address and distance fields (the emulator was typed into over adb); the clock dial and the calendar under a thumb; a trip the truck starts while the edit screen is open; an address typed while a real, slow lookup is under way (the unit tests cover the order of the two writes, the emulator's lookups were too quick to race); anything with a screen reader. Device checks 153 to 171.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the edit screen**
All are Shawn's to change.
- **What MilO recorded is kept in three columns, and the distance is not worked out again from the GPS points.** The work order asked for a restore that "puts back the distance computed from the stored GPS points", and left it open whether a column is needed. It is, for the times: a trip's start is the moment it was opened, before its first fix, and its end depends on a cut-off (the moment the truck was found gone) that is stored nowhere, while the fixes after it are. The distance could be recalculated given the recorded end, and comes out the same today. It is kept in a column all the same, because the points file is deliberately left out of the backup (after a restore on another phone a recalculation would put 0 km on a real trip) and because the thresholds of the calculation are meant to be tuned. The three columns are written once, by the first edit, and never changed.
- **Two marks for the addresses, and an emptied address is Shawn's too.** Without them a restore could not tell a typed address from one the lookup found, and an address he removed because it was wrong would be looked up and put back. So an end he typed, changed or emptied is left alone for good; a row says "no address entered" for it.
- **An edit cannot stop a lookup that has not answered yet.** Leaving an empty address field empty is not a change, so a trip that is still being looked up is still looked up.
- **A trip added by hand is stored with the start cause `MANUAL`** and its own column, not with a new start cause: the enum is in `core/trip/`, and an older build would crash on a value it does not know. It is never marked as edited, whatever is changed in it afterwards: all of it is by hand, and it has nothing to restore.
- **The minimum trip distance and "Ignore them" are not applied to a trip added by hand,** nor to an edit. Both are for trips MilO records by itself. An edit never discards a trip.
- **Only a changed start sorts a trip again,** not a changed end, distance or address, and not the mere opening of the form: a schedule that was changed since must not quietly re-sort a trip whose start is the same. It is sorted by the schedule as it is at the save, and in the phone's time zone then.
- **A press on the Business or Personal that is already shown is not a choice.** It would mark the trip as set by hand with nothing to see for it. Any other press is Shawn's choice and final for MilO, also if the work hours would have said the same.
- **With settings that cannot be read, a trip whose start changed is left unsorted,** like a trip that closes then, and the form says that MilO cannot tell. The catch-up sorts it later.
- **The whole form is checked, changed or not.** A recorded trip whose stored times make no sense (the clock was corrected during it) therefore has to have its times put right before anything about it can be saved from the edit screen.
- **"Absurd" is more than 2,000 km, or more than 180 km/h on average over the trip.** The second catches a lost decimal point, which the first lets through; 180 km/h is the speed the distance calculation already treats as impossible. Zero km is taken.
- **Only the trip that is being recorded is protected from overlap.** That is what the work order named. Finished trips are not compared with each other, so two can be made to overlap and both are counted.
- **"In the future" is judged by the phone's clock at the moment Save is pressed,** and the overlap by the open trip as stored then, not as they were when the form was opened.
- **A typed time is on the whole minute; an untouched one keeps its seconds.** A time that is changed and put back to the minute it had counts as untouched. A distance retyped as the figure the form showed is not a change either.
- **One day per trip.** The form has one date, and the end is on that day. A stored trip that ran past midnight keeps ending on the next day and says so; the form cannot make such a trip. (**Reversed by the review entry above:** the form has a switch, "Ended the next day".)
- **The form opens for a missed trip with nothing filled in but today's date.** No time and no category are suggested before there is a start: a guess that is saved unnoticed is a wrong line on the report.
- **A Save button, on this one screen.** Every other change in MilO is stored at once. A trip's figures only make sense together, and the work order wants one log line per save.
- **Back and the bottom bar leave the form without a question,** and what was typed is gone. It also goes if Android ends MilO in the background, because the form is held by the screen's ViewModel and not in the saved state. Turning the phone keeps it. (**Reversed by the review entry above:** every way out asks first. The loss when Android ends MilO stands.)
- **A refused form writes nothing to the log.** What reaches storage does: a save, a save that changed nothing, a restore, an added trip, and a write that storage refused or failed.
- **These log lines spell addresses out.** The lookup's lines name none (2026-10-05). An edit's line must, or a typed address that is replaced would be gone without a trace: after the first edit the row keeps only what MilO recorded. The event log stays on the phone; whoever builds an export of it has to know that it now holds addresses.
- **The log line is written after the write, and outside the guard around it.** A change that was stored is never reported to the screen as failed because its log line could not be written; a second press on "Add this trip" would add the trip twice.
- **"Restore recorded values" is on the edit screen, under Save, with the recorded figures shown,** and it asks first. It drops what was typed, so it is not done at once like the other Restore.
- **The mark is an asterisk and words:** "* Edited by hand", "* Added by hand", with the asterisk repeated after the trip's times. The report is to use the same asterisk. Home's Today and the Android Auto screen do not show it; the work order named the Trips list.
- **"Add a missed trip" is a button in words under the month's card,** not a plus sign in the title: it is used rarely and must be found without knowing an icon.
- **A feature package of its own, `feature/tripedit/`,** beside `feature/trips/`, as the pairing screen is beside Setup. The two share nothing but what is in `data/` and `core/`.
- **The text fields keep their own text.** A value that travels to the ViewModel and back through a flow arrives a frame late in a text field. `TextEntry` therefore reports what is typed and is never told what to show.
- **The gate was run with the throwaway debug key,** and the Keychain was not read.

**`[FIX]` The review of the driving alert: ten findings, nine acted on, one left for Shawn**
Two reviewers read the package below before it was committed, one for the platform and for safety, one for the standards and the documents, and made ten findings between them; the missing conditions and the uncaught failure were found by both. Each is listed with what was done. No table, no column and no database version changed, and `app/schemas/` is untouched. No library was added. How a trip starts, is recorded and ends was not touched.
- **Three conditions of the alert were missing (major, both reviewers).** As first built the alert was shown with no truck paired, a second time 22 seconds after the first when the phone reported "left" and "entered" again, a third time after a restart of the process, and 6 seconds after End trip. A reviewer saw each on an emulator. `judgeDriving` now has the three conditions, each with a reason of its own in the log:
  - **A truck is paired** (a truck's address is stored): "No alert: no truck is paired". The phone has none today, so there the alert stays silent until pairing (device check DA-13). A settings file that cannot be read still does not silence the alert: whether a truck is paired is not known then, and it is taken to be.
  - **No trip ended in the last 5 minutes:** "No alert: a trip ended less than 5 min ago". The time is the latest `endedAtMs` of all closed trips (`TripRepository.findNewestTripEndMs`, one read-only query, no index: the table is small), so it is the same after a restart. It is the trip's own end, which for a trip the truck ended is the moment the truck was found gone and not the end of the grace period. A discarded or a deleted trip counts: it was recorded, and it ended.
  - **No alert in the last 30 minutes:** "No new alert: the last one was less than 30 min ago". The time is a new key in the settings file, `last_driving_alert_at_ms`, written right after the alert is posted. Posting comes first on purpose: stored first, a failure to post would leave MilO silent for half an hour about an alert nobody was shown. If the time cannot be stored, the alert has been posted all the same and the log line says the next report may bring it up again.
  - **This one condition leaves a showing alert where it is.** Every other failed condition takes the alert away, so that the notification matches the newest decision. The 30 minutes are asked last and change nothing either way: an alert that is still showing is still true.
  - **A stored time ahead of the phone's clock silences nothing.** A clock that was set back would otherwise keep the alert quiet until it had caught up.
  - **What the two spans cost,** Shawn's to change (two constants in `DrivingAlertRules.kt`): within 30 minutes of an alert, and within 5 minutes of a trip's end, a new drive on a day Bluetooth fails brings no alert, and since the phone reports entering a vehicle once for a drive, such a drive may get none at all. And after a "left" at a stop the alert stays away for the rest of the half hour. Device checks DA-10 and DA-14.
- **A failure inside the alert could have ended a recording (major for one reviewer, minor for the other).** Its work runs in the application scope, which has no handler for an exception, and only an unreadable settings file was caught. A report arrives about a minute into every truck drive: a database read or a log write that failed then would have ended the process, and the trip service with it. The three pieces of work (judging a report, asking the phone, taking the alert away for a trip that began) now run through `DrivingFailures.keptApart`: every exception but a cancellation is caught, written as one `ERROR` line "The driving alert failed while …" with its stack trace, and if the log is what failed, to a crash file. The receiver is let go in a `finally`, as before. It is the pattern of `TripAddresses.runPass` and `TripCategoryCatchUp`, written a third time and not shared: sharing it means changing those two classes, which is more than this fix should touch.
- **A tap on the alert skips the preflight (minor). Not changed.** The `[DEBT]` entry below says why, and what the ways out are. **Shawn's to decide.**
- **Four one-line faults passed every test (minor).** Each has a test now, and each was put back into the code afterwards to see the test fail: a report judged without waiting for the controller's first look at storage (the test holds the write that closes a stale trip back, and the report must not have been judged meanwhile); the second look after posting removed (a trip is started while the alert is reading storage); "entered" and "left" swapped, or a report's age taken the wrong way round (the reading of the phone's event moved out of the receiver into a pure function, `vehicleReport`, which also drops a kind of transition it does not know); and the Settings switch not stored, or the alert not told (`SettingsViewModelTest`, the first test of a ViewModel in the project: it supplies the main dispatcher with `Dispatchers.setMain`).
- **Checks that no longer matched the app (minor).** Check 54 said nine rows on Setup (ten), check 111 five cards in Settings (six), DA-3 expected a detail line that only a paired truck gives, and ARCHITECTURE spoke of two notifications (three). All four corrected. DA-10 is rewritten for the 30 minutes; DA-13 (no truck paired) and DA-14 (just after a trip) are new.
- **Files over the limit (minor).** `DrivingAlertTest.kt` had 397 lines and `SetupRulesTest.kt` 326. The first is now `DrivingAlertReportsTest`, `DrivingAlertQuietTest`, `DrivingAlertWatchTest` and `DrivingAlertFailureTest` on a shared `DrivingFixtures.kt`; the Physical activity row has its own `PhysicalActivityRowTest`. `DrivingAlert.kt` would have passed 300 with the failure handling, which is why that is a class of its own. A 119-column comment line in `MainActivity.kt`, which ktlint does not measure, is rewrapped.
- **The `[DEBT]` entry "The driving alert can sound more than once in one drive" is gone,** and with it the missing `// TODO(debt):` the review pointed at: the quiet period it asked for is built.
- **What the reviewers saw on an emulator, and what nobody has.** Their findings quote log lines of reports being judged, of the notification being posted and withdrawn, and of its tap starting a trip. The entry below says an emulator cannot be made to report driving; how the reports were produced in the review is not in the findings, so it is not known whether Play services delivered them. **The build as it is after these changes has run on no emulator and on no phone:** what is new rests on the unit tests and on Room checking the one new query when the app is built.
- **Tests:** 845 in all (819 before). New: the three conditions to the second (4 minutes 59 and 5 minutes, 29 minutes 59 and 30 minutes), their order, a time ahead of the clock; the reading of the phone's event; the three limits beside a real trip controller, across a restart of the process too; a failure while judging, while asking the phone and while withdrawing, and the event log failing as well; the wait for the controller; a trip that begins while the alert is being decided; the wording of the new lines; the Settings switch.

**`[DEBT]` A tap on the driving alert skips the preflight**
- **What:** the alert's tap starts the trip service directly, exactly as a tap on "could not start this trip" does (ADR-002, point 10). The preflight Home's Start button runs is not run, and unlike the other notification, none has run before this tap. A missing location permission is refused by Android itself and ends in the "could not start" warning. Location switched off for the whole phone is not: a reviewer saw, on an emulator, "Trip 4 started by MANUAL", the chirp and "Trip in progress", no fix in 33 seconds, and End trip discarding the trip at 0 m. Home's button in the same state refuses with "Location is switched off on this phone."
- **Why it was left:** the package was to start the trip through the existing tap-to-start path and to leave how a trip starts alone, and each way out changes a decision that is Shawn's. (1) Aim the tap at a small receiver that hands `MANUAL_START` to the trip controller, as Home's button does. The preflight then runs and a failure brings the "could not start" warning with its reason in the log. But the one tap then depends on Android, and HyperOS, letting a receiver that a notification started bring up the location service from the background, which nothing has shown on the phone; the direct start is the case Android documents. And the preflight is deliberately stricter than Android: it also refuses without the Bluetooth permission and without "Allow all the time", where a direct start may well record a usable trip (not tried). (2) Post no alert while the preflight finds a problem. That silences the alert on a phone without the Bluetooth permission, which is the case judgement 1 below wants it for.
- **Cost to fix later:** small either way, after the decision. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `TripNotifications.showDrivingAlert`. Listed under the gotchas of the encyclopedia's "Safety net".

**`[CHANGE]` The driving alert (the third piece of phase 2)**
Phone only: a notification, one Setup row, one Settings switch. Its rules run in unit tests; the registration, the row and the switch ran on an emulator (the finding below); **no report of driving had reached MilO anywhere when this was written, so the notification had never been posted or tapped** (what the reviewers saw afterwards is in the entry above). Device checks DA-1 to DA-12, and DA-13 and DA-14 since the review. **This entry describes the package as first built; the review above changed three things in it, each marked here.**
- **What it does.** During the work hours, when the phone's driving detection reports that it has entered a vehicle while no trip is open and the truck is not known to be connected, MilO posts "You seem to be driving" / "No trip is being recorded. Tap to start one." on a new high-importance channel, "Driving alert".
- **It never starts a trip.** Only the tap does, and through the path the "could not start this trip" notification already uses: a `PendingIntent` for the trip service carrying `MANUAL_START`. The two taps are told apart by the intent's request number, which is why the existing one now passes 0 by name; with one number for both, Android would have replaced the first notification's intent with the second's, and a tap on either would have been logged as the other. The class that judges a report (`DrivingAlert`) is given neither the trip controller nor the trip repository, and a unit test runs it beside a real controller and checks that no report asks for the trip service or opens a trip.
- **The rule** (`judgeDriving`, pure, `platform/driving/`): the newest report is "entered" and at most 5 minutes old; the switch is on; no trip is open in storage; a fresh reading of the truck is not "connected"; and `classifyTrip` would make a trip that starts now Business. (Since the review above also: a truck is paired, no trip ended in the last 5 minutes, and no alert was posted in the last 30.)
